## [Kimi K2.5: Open Multimodal Agentic Model with Agent Swarm](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.02276) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Moonshot AI released Kimi K2.5, an open-source multimodal agentic model that jointly optimizes text and vision through techniques like joint text-vision pretraining, zero-vision SFT, and joint reinforcement learning. It also introduces Agent Swarm, a parallel orchestration framework that reduces latency by up to 4.5x. Kimi K2.5 achieves state-of-the-art results across coding, vision, reasoning, and agentic tasks, positioning Moonshot AI as a major player in open-source multimodal AI. Its Agent Swarm framework could accelerate the adoption of multi-agent systems in real-world applications. The model checkpoint is released for research and applications. Agent Swarm dynamically decomposes complex tasks into heterogeneous sub-problems and executes them concurrently, achieving up to 4.5x latency reduction over single-agent baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Kimi is a series of large language models developed by Moonshot AI, known for long context support. Agent Swarm is a multi-agent orchestration framework similar to OpenAI's Swarm, which coordinates multiple AI agents to work on tasks in parallel. Zero-vision SFT is a technique where supervised fine-tuning is performed without visual data, often to improve reasoning before multimodal reinforcement learning.

## [Hugging Face Transformers v5.15.0 Adds Muse Glimmer and GraniteSWA Support](https://github.com/huggingface/transformers/releases/tag/v5.15.0) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Hugging Face Transformers v5.15.0 adds support for Meta's Muse Glimmer multimodal model, a 30B parameter dense model distilled from Muse Spark, along with GraniteSWA and GraniteMoeSWA models from IBM. The release also introduces A.X-K1/K2 and Cosmos3 Edge models, and includes several breaking changes to attention and kernel handling. This release is significant because it brings Meta's latest agentic multimodal model to the Transformers ecosystem, enabling developers to deploy privacy-aware AI applications locally. The addition of GraniteSWA models also strengthens enterprise-focused capabilities, and the breaking changes signal a shift toward more flexible attention and kernel configurations. Muse Glimmer consists of a 2B ViT-style vision encoder and a 28B text decoder, released under Apache 2.0. The breaking changes include making kernels opt-in for linear attention models, restricting cache cropping to negative values, and enabling SDPA for T5 family models, which may alter default attention behavior.

github · LysandreJik · Aug 10, 10:28

**Background**: Transformers is a popular open-source library for state-of-the-art machine learning models. Muse Glimmer is a distilled version of Meta's Muse Spark, designed for agentic tasks on consumer hardware. GraniteSWA models are part of IBM's Granite series, which are enterprise-focused LLMs. The release also includes breaking changes that affect how attention and kernels are handled, requiring users to update their code.

<a id="item-4"></a>
## [Docker Sandboxes: Disposable MicroVM Isolation for AI Agents](https://www.docker.com/products/docker-sandboxes/) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Docker has launched Docker Sandboxes, a new product that provides disposable, isolated microVM-based sandboxes for AI coding agents such as Claude Code, Gemini, Codex, and Kiro. Each sandbox runs its own kernel and Docker daemon, with features like outbound firewalls and secret injection. This addresses the critical need for secure execution of AI-generated code, which standard containers cannot fully provide due to shared host kernels. It offers a practical, managed solution for developers and enterprises integrating AI agents into workflows, potentially reducing security risks and improving adoption. Each session is a microVM with its own kernel, running on native hypervisors (Hypervisor.framework, WHP, KVM), and Docker wrote a new VMM rather than using Firecracker. The sandbox includes a private Docker daemon, isolated filesystem and network, and supports outbound firewall rules and secret injection with placeholders.

hackernews · etoxin · Aug 10, 06:02 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49239751)

**Background**: AI agents execute shell commands and manipulate files, posing security risks if they access sensitive data or the host system. Traditional container sandboxes share the host kernel, making them vulnerable to kernel-level exploits. MicroVMs provide stronger isolation by running a separate kernel per session, which is why Docker adopted this approach for AI agent sandboxing.

Tl;dv, a popular AI meeting transcription tool, exposed over 180,000 meeting transcripts due to misconfigured public sharing settings. The vulnerability was discovered and reported, and the company claims to have fixed it a few days later. This incident highlights the severe privacy and security risks inherent in AI meeting tools that store sensitive conversational data. It raises concerns about compliance certifications like SOC2, which may not adequately prevent such misconfigurations, and underscores the need for stronger data protection measures in the AI SaaS ecosystem. The exposed data included raw transcripts of meetings, which may contain personal and business-sensitive information. The misconfiguration involved public sharing links, a common issue in cloud storage and collaboration tools. Tl;dv responded by addressing the issue and publishing a blog post, but community members criticized their framing of the data as 'public'.

Misconfigured public sharing is a well-known cause of data exposure across SaaS platforms, as seen in incidents with Salesforce and other services.

<a id="item-8"></a>
## [CoCo: Faithful Response-Level Interpretation for MoE Reward Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06400) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Contribution-Contrast (CoCo), a novel response-level interpretation method for Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) reward models. CoCo characterizes experts using chosen-rejected response pairs with the largest contribution contrasts, capturing both routing and preference behavior more faithfully than routing-weight-based approaches. This is the first systematic study of interpretation methods for MoE reward models, addressing a critical gap in interpretability. CoCo provides more coherent, faithful, and specialized interpretations, which can enhance trust and safety in AI systems that rely on reward models for alignment. CoCo uses the product of routing weight and absolute expert score difference as the interpretation signal, selecting response pairs that maximize this product. Evaluations show CoCo outperforms router-based, score-based, and sparse autoencoder-based alternatives in coherence, faithfulness, and specialization while maintaining competitive reward modeling accuracy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models are a type of neural network architecture that uses multiple specialized sub-networks (experts) and a routing mechanism to activate only a subset of experts per input. Reward models are trained to predict human preferences and are used in reinforcement learning from human feedback (RLHF) to align language models. Traditional interpretation methods for MoE reward models rely on routing weights, which only show which prompts an expert receives, not how it judges responses, leading to incomplete understanding.

<a id="item-9"></a>
## [WebGrader: Self-Evolving Programmatic Grader for LLM Web Development](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06474) ⭐️ 8.0/10

WebGrader introduces a self-evolving programmatic grader that autonomously derives executable interaction flows, called Flow Contracts, from website requests and uses their execution outcomes as reinforcement learning rewards for training LLMs in web development. On WebGen-Bench, it trains an 8B policy to a 52.01% functional success rate, outperforming a matched appearance-plus-script reward by 7.88 points and surpassing o4-mini and DeepSeek-v4-flash. This approach addresses a key bottleneck in reinforcement learning for web development—reward design—by automating the creation of executable interaction flows, potentially reducing the need for hand-authored browser scripts and improving the functional correctness of LLM-generated websites. It could significantly impact LLM code generation and web development automation, making RL training more scalable and effective. WebGrader materializes the generated project in a live browser, grounds target actions against source code and live DOM, and collects visual, DOM, response, and persistent-state evidence along the same browser trajectory. A residual-driven offline loop discovers reusable verifier skills, screens them on disjoint validation pages, and freezes the promoted skill graph before policy training, ensuring a Pass verdict only after observing the requested transition.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models increasingly generate complete websites from natural-language descriptions, and reinforcement learning is used to close their functional gaps. However, RL training is bottlenecked by reward design: hand-authored browser scripts are costly to write for open-ended requirements, while VLM and GUI-agent graders may issue verdicts before observing the decisive state. WebGrader proposes a self-evolving programmatic grader that autonomously derives interaction flows as executable Flow Contracts, using their execution outcomes as rewards.

<a id="item-10"></a>
## [Frontier Models Show Distinct Response Modes Under Steering Pressure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06578) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study evaluated behavioral steerability across six frontier language models using 300 paired base and steered items, revealing distinct response modes, including GPT-5's unique deflection behavior (99% vs. 0% for others) and Claude Opus 4.7's resistance to suppression. The study also traced the largest behavioral split in Llama to its internals, with a linear probe decoding behavior at 0.87 held-out accuracy. This research is significant for AI safety and alignment, as it shows that frontier models respond differently to steering pressure, which could impact how safety evaluations are conducted and how models are developed. Understanding these response modes can help improve model interpretability and control. The study used 24,480 judgments from six models acting as blind peer judges, scored by leave-one-out consensus. It covered three categories: values-conflict, reasoning-elicitation, and reasoning-suppression, plus 40 validation items. All findings held under token-budget remediation and a hypothesis-blind judgment prompt control.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Frontier language models are trained with distinct data, objectives, and safety pipelines, but whether these differences lead to measurably different behaviors under explicit steering pressure is underexplored. Steering pressure refers to explicit instructions or prompts designed to shift a model's behavior, and behavioral steerability measures how much a model's responses change in response to such pressure. This study aims to fill that gap by systematically comparing six models.

<a id="item-11"></a>
## [NxN E-valuation: Hypothesis Certification via Conformal CRT Null](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06621) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces NxN E-valuation, an e-value-based algorithm that certifies hypotheses without constructing case-specific null hypotheses, using large datasets. It is particularly designed for LLM-based exploration systems to mitigate hallucination. This method offers a universally better alternative to circular verification and held-out testing for LLM outputs, addressing a critical issue of hallucination in exploration systems. It could improve the reliability of LLM-driven scientific discovery and data analysis. The algorithm exploits the naturally existing large training set, letting different samples serve as null hypotheses for one another, directly realizing a conditional randomization test (CRT). It requires that LLM generations are hypotheses that apply to each individual sample.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: E-values are a statistical tool for hypothesis testing, interpreted as evidence against the null hypothesis. Conformal inference provides a framework for constructing valid p-values or e-values under exchangeability assumptions. LLM hallucination refers to the generation of false or unsupported information, which is a major challenge in using LLMs for hypothesis generation.

## [Shape Your Feed: LLM Agentic System for Conversational Recommendation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06632) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Shape Your Feed (SYF), an LLM-based agentic recommendation framework that enables real-time, multimodal co-curation of content. It employs a three-tier architecture with Perception Flow, Serving Flow, and Self-Evolution Flow, achieving 98.85% accuracy in alignment scoring and improving feed relevance in online A/B tests. This framework addresses the gap between passive behavioral ranking and explicit user intent in industrial recommendation systems. By enabling users to steer their feed in real time through natural language and multimodal inputs, it could significantly enhance user experience and satisfaction, potentially influencing future recommendation system designs. SYF's Perception Flow captures intent from text, voice, and UI interactions; the Serving Flow performs real-time agentic re-ranking and pruning using a persistent Semantic Profile; the Self-Evolution Flow uses DPO and an LLM-as-a-Judge ensemble. Offline evaluations show 98.85% alignment accuracy, and online A/B tests on production traffic demonstrate improved feed relevance and user sentiment.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional recommendation systems rely on implicit behavioral signals like clicks and dwell time, which may not reflect explicit user interests. LLM-based agentic systems use large language models to understand natural language and perform actions, enabling more interactive and user-steerable recommendations. Multimodal co-curation involves combining various input modalities to collaboratively shape content.

## [TRACE: Multi-Layer Benchmark for Human-AI Coordination Under Drift](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06657) ⭐️ 8.0/10

TRACE introduces a multi-layer benchmark for diagnosing drift and failures in human-AI control loops, built from ALFRED traces with 1,918 drifted samples. It provides time-aligned, multi-layer traces labeled with drift type, affected layer, onset time, responsible actor, and causal mechanism. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in evaluating human-AI coordination under drift and failures, which is essential for AI safety and cyber-physical systems. It enables researchers to localize where coordination breaks down and to develop better monitoring and recovery strategies. The benchmark includes a leak-aware protocol that removes a near-perfect onset leak, and baseline results show drift identification and attribution well above random and majority baselines across classical, recurrent, and attention-based models. Affected layer macro-F1 is near 0.70, responsible actor near 0.85, and causal mechanism near 0.49, with heavy attention offering no advantage over simpler models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Cyber-physical and AI-assisted systems integrate human operators, AI decision modules, and automated controllers into a single control loop, where trustworthiness depends on the entire loop. Drift refers to deviations that can originate in any layer of the stack, and conventional single-modality monitoring cannot localize them. ALFRED is a benchmark for grounded instruction following in household tasks, providing a basis for generating realistic traces.

## [CellWorld: Latent Cell Prediction Boosts Spatial Transcriptomics Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06659) ⭐️ 8.0/10

CellWorld introduces a novel pretraining strategy for spatial transcriptomics foundation models, shifting from gene-level reconstruction to latent cell prediction. It achieves scalable performance improvements and better spatial transferability across multiple benchmarks. This approach could significantly improve the transferability and scalability of spatial transcriptomics models, which are crucial for understanding tissue architecture and disease mechanisms. It may set a new standard for pretraining in computational biology, benefiting researchers and clinical applications. CellWorld predicts latent representations of masked cells from visible spatial context and partial expression hints, avoiding direct reconstruction of assay-specific variation. Four variants (5.74M to 94.56M parameters) were pretrained on 46 million human cells, with CellWorld-Small outperforming all baselines on 11 linear-probe and 7 fine-tuned spatial benchmarks.

## [CGMas: Multi-Agent LLM Framework Automates Polymer Coarse-Grained MD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06694) ⭐️ 8.0/10

CGMas, a multi-agent LLM framework, automates the entire coarse-grained molecular dynamics pipeline for polymers, from topology construction to potential derivation and validation, completing all 27 homopolymer and copolymer tasks and reducing simulation time from 38-88 minutes to 1 minute. This framework addresses a significant bottleneck in polymer simulation by automating laborious bottom-up CG modeling, potentially accelerating materials discovery and research in polymer science. It demonstrates the promising integration of LLM-based multi-agent systems with computational chemistry, opening new avenues for automated scientific workflows. CGMas uses an LLM reasoning agent to infer AA topology from polymer name, with layered self-correction for physical errors in unsaturated, heteroatom-containing, and polar polymers. It matched AA density within 5% in 22 of 27 tasks, and the framework is designed to handle both homopolymers and copolymers.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Coarse-grained (CG) molecular dynamics extends polymer simulation beyond the scales accessible to all-atom (AA) methods, but bottom-up CG modeling is laborious because the CG resolution is a design choice and potentials must be derived anew for each polymer mapping. Multi-agent LLM frameworks involve multiple AI agents driven by large language models that collaborate to handle complex tasks, and Boltzmann inversion is a technique used to derive effective interaction potentials from target radial distribution functions.

## [WebRider: Intent Contracts for Live-Web Agent Policy Compliance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06704) ⭐️ 8.0/10

WebRider introduces intent contracts to formalize delegated policies for live-web assistance, and its audit reveals that a strong controller completes 99.2% of tasks but honors all policy constraints in only 38.8% of cases. The system employs a hierarchical architecture with a top-layer controller, a middle layer for guarded executable actions, and a tool layer, evaluated on RiderBench with 4,096 live-web contracts across 42 websites. This addresses a critical gap in live-web agent evaluation, where final-answer accuracy ignores policy constraints, potentially leading to unsafe or non-compliant agent behavior. By making the browsing path a first-class object, WebRider enables auditable, human-judgeable, and learnable systems, which is significant for the growing field of AI agents and web automation. The middle guarded interface also serves as a high-quality training signal; an 8B action-policy model trained through this interface outperforms executable-only baselines under a fixed controller. The benchmark audits both internal contract state and visible user experience to determine if a rollout preserved its policy and if steps were persona-consistent.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Delegating a web task involves transferring a policy that specifies what to verify, how to handle uncertainty, preferences, and when to stop. Current live-web agents are evaluated solely on final answers, ignoring these policy constraints. Intent contracts formalize the delegated policy as an operational record of goals, constraints, evidence obligations, answer form, and task-local persona controls. This concept is related to broader discussions on intent contracts in AI agents, where they are used to limit agent authority and ensure compliance.

## [MolBioKG: Grounding Out-of-Graph Molecules in Biomedical Knowledge Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06713) ⭐️ 8.0/10

MolBioKG introduces a two-layer system that grounds unseen molecules in biomedical knowledge graphs via multi-resolution structural anchoring, enabling link recovery and multi-hop reasoning without task-specific training. It connects an index of 2.74 million molecules to a 9.6-million-edge KG and features two inference mechanisms: static multi-anchor retrieval using Reciprocal Rank Fusion and Adapt-KG, a tool-using LLM policy. This addresses the cold-start problem in biomedical knowledge graphs, where unregistered molecules are disconnected, hindering drug discovery. By enabling out-of-graph generalization, MolBioKG could accelerate drug repurposing and target identification, impacting researchers and practitioners in computational drug discovery. MolBioKG outperforms strong baselines, raising Hits@10 from 0.585 to 0.876 in multi-hop reasoning and out-of-graph target recall from 0.145 to 0.269. Predictions retain traceable structural anchors and source-attributed KG evidence, ensuring interpretability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Biomedical knowledge graphs (KGs) are structured networks of biomedical entities and relationships, used to accelerate drug discovery. However, standard pipelines assume query molecules already exist as graph entities, leaving unregistered molecules disconnected. MolBioKG uses multi-resolution structural anchoring, representing molecules via scaffolds, fragments, functional groups, and fingerprints, to bridge this gap. Reciprocal Rank Fusion (RRF) is a method to combine rankings from multiple retrieval systems, and tool-using LLM policies enable adaptive traversal of the KG.

ReASearch is a new unified framework where a single tool-using agent autonomously manages evaluation, diagnosis, editing, and verification for optimizing prompts, programs, and ML workflows. It outperforms specialized systems on 14 diverse tasks, achieving gains of 2% to 40% over strong baselines. This work challenges the conventional reliance on explicit outer-loop controllers in optimization, showing that complex search behaviors can emerge from an agent's reasoning. It could lead to more flexible and general-purpose optimization systems that adapt to diverse domains without hand-designed heuristics. ReASearch uses a shared agent loop with domain-specific tools, allowing the same scaffold to optimize prompts, programs, and ML workflows. It incorporates persistent memory for long-horizon strategy refinement, and in some cases discovers solutions that improve on prior human best-known results.

## [New Dataset and Task for Pure Synthesis Fake News Video Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06732) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces PS-FNVD, the first pure synthesis fake news video dataset, and formulates T2V fake news detection as a ternary classification task (real, cheap fake, pure synthesis fake). It also proposes the R-T2V framework, which achieves state-of-the-art performance, outperforming the second-best baseline by 12.20 percentage points in accuracy and 8.46 in macro F1. This work addresses a novel and timely threat: text-to-video models can now synthesize fake news videos from scratch, bypassing traditional detectors that rely on detecting manipulated existing footage. The new dataset and ternary task formulation provide a foundation for developing more robust detection methods, crucial for AI safety and media forensics. PS-FNVD includes two types of fabricated content: Type 1 (fabricated events with aligned deception) and Type 2 (true events with false visual provenance), designed to prevent models from exploiting unimodal shortcuts. The R-T2V framework integrates high-level semantic logic with low-level physical generative traces through conditioned rationale generation and supervised fine-tuning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Text-to-video (T2V) generation models, such as Kandinsky Video and MiniMax H3, can create realistic videos from text prompts. Traditional fake news videos are often 'cheap fakes'—manipulated existing footage—while deepfakes use AI to alter faces or voices. The 'modality alignment trap' refers to a situation where a detector might rely on superficial alignment between text and video, rather than verifying the authenticity of the content itself.

## [IB-RL: Isolated Bilateral Reinforcement Learning for Strategic Dialogue Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06735) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Isolated Bilateral Reinforcement Learning (IB-RL), a method where two roles co-evolve through joint rollouts while each optimizes its own reward with fully independent advantages, action masks, and update paths. On Vehicle TeleSales, IB-RL achieves 89.6% Success@1, and on Deal-or-NoDeal, it reaches 98.4% agreement against DeepSeek V4 Pro, outperforming unilateral RL baselines. This work addresses the static-counterpart mismatch in RL for strategic dialogue, where training against a fixed counterpart leads to overfitting and poor generalization. By proposing a bilateral training paradigm, it could improve the robustness and adaptability of LLM-based agents in multi-agent interactive settings, impacting areas like negotiation, customer service, and game-playing AI. The method uses joint rollouts where both roles are trained simultaneously, but with strict per-agent isolation in advantages, action masks, and update paths to prevent information leakage. The paper evaluates frozen policies against fully independent held-out counterparts, demonstrating significant improvements over unilateral baselines in two domains.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning (RL) has been effective for improving large language models (LLMs) on tasks with stationary, verifiable rewards, such as math and code. However, strategic dialogue involves an adaptive counterpart, making the environment non-stationary. Traditional RL trains against a fixed counterpart, leading to the static-counterpart mismatch. IB-RL addresses this by co-evolving both roles, aiming to learn strategies that generalize across unseen counterparts.

## [LiFTER: Neuro-Symbolic Microscope for Dynamic Graph Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06765) ⭐️ 8.0/10

LiFTER (Link-Fact Temporal Rule Inducer) is a new neuro-symbolic predictor that preserves interactions as grounded temporal facts and applies executable temporal rules to pre-query facts, achieving competitive performance on four CTDG benchmarks. It enables inspection, recomputation, and intervention on the evidence and rules behind each prediction. This approach addresses the interpretability and intervention limitations of existing continuous-time dynamic graph models, which typically compress past interactions into opaque neural states. By making predictions verifiable and grounded, LiFTER could improve trust and usability in applications like social network analysis, financial fraud detection, and recommendation systems. LiFTER achieves the highest macro explanation accuracy and deletion fidelity across four CTDG benchmarks, and its independent execution reconstructs all logits for 19,664 test predictions with a maximum error of 0.0000131. The architecture also serves as a microscope to separate contributions of recurrence, history position, and transition, tracing them to individual facts.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Continuous-time dynamic graphs (CTDGs) model evolving relationships as timestamped events, and forecasting future links is crucial for many applications. Traditional neural models like graph neural networks (GNNs) and recurrent architectures compress past interactions into hidden states, which are effective but opaque. Neuro-symbolic methods combine neural learning with symbolic reasoning to enhance interpretability and allow intervention, which is the focus of LiFTER.

The paper introduces the Potential-aware Instance and Algorithm Co-evolution (PIAC) framework, which uses a novel reference-free metric called potential gain to generate hard instances and improve the generalization of LLM-constructed algorithm portfolios. PIAC achieves a 19.76% relative improvement for TSP Greedy Constructive portfolios over state-of-the-art baselines. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in LLM-based automatic portfolio construction for combinatorial optimization, where poor generalization in few-shot scenarios limits practical deployment. By removing the need for reference solutions and enhancing instance diversity, PIAC could enable more robust and adaptable algorithm portfolios across various optimization problems. PIAC instantiates on three algorithmic backbones: Greedy Constructive, Ant Colony Optimization, and Guided Local Search. It is evaluated on TSP and CVRP across six data distributions, consistently outperforming LLM-ACP baselines. The potential gain metric estimates generalization gain by perturbing generated algorithms and assessing their improvement potential on generated instances.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: LLM-ACP (Automatic Construction of Portfolios via Large Language Models) uses LLMs to build algorithm portfolios for combinatorial optimization problems, but suffers from poor generalization in few-shot scenarios. Instance and algorithm co-evolution frameworks address this by generating hard instances on which the current portfolio underperforms, but they rely on high-quality reference solutions and limited instance diversity. PIAC overcomes these limitations with a reference-free metric and LLM-evolved diverse instance mutators.

## [CEDAR: LLM-Driven MCTS for Goal-Directed Complex System Design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06871) ⭐️ 8.0/10

CEDAR introduces an autonomous method that combines Large Language Model (LLM) agents with Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS) to discover complex systems meeting user-specified behavioral goals. It represents systems as a restricted Python subset and uses an LLM Judge and Editor to iteratively evaluate and improve system variants. This work addresses a central open problem in artificial life—goal-directed design of complex systems—by automating a traditionally labor-intensive modeling workflow. It could significantly impact fields like systems biology, economic policy, and strategic decision-making by enabling broader adoption of complex systems modeling. CEDAR formalizes the process as an MCTS variant with an LLM-parameterized transition kernel and value function, preserving solution diversity while enabling goal-directed discovery. Its LLM-based interpretability reveals how structural changes drive emergent behavior, and it reduces human effort compared to existing approaches.

Traditional modeling languages like DYNAMO and STELLA require labor-intensive workflows, making goal-directed design challenging. Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS) is a heuristic search algorithm used in decision processes, often combined with neural networks for games, and here it is adapted with LLM agents to guide the search.

## [Edge-Conditioned Spectral Operators Improve Physics-Sensitive PDE Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06894) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces the Edge-Conditioned Spectral Operator (ESO), a novel framework that modulates global spectral mixing using local edge-wise variations, along with the Pairwise-Variation Modal Mixer (PVMM) and Physics-Aware Reweighting (PAR). ESO achieves state-of-the-art performance across nine PDE benchmarks, reducing errors near coefficient jumps and high-gradient structures. This work addresses a critical limitation of existing spectral neural operators, which rely on center-point representations and fail to capture physics-sensitive local structures. By incorporating edge information, ESO enhances the accuracy and applicability of neural operators for scientific computing, potentially benefiting fields like fluid dynamics and materials science. ESO incorporates the Pairwise-Variation Modal Mixer (PVMM) to inject local edge information into spectral mode selection, and a task-adaptive Physics-Aware Reweighting (PAR) that emphasizes physically important regions. The code is publicly available at https://github.com/Tanpig-X/ESO, and the paper is authored by Zhentao Tan and two others.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Neural operators are machine learning models that learn mappings between function spaces, commonly used to solve partial differential equations (PDEs). Spectral operators perform global mixing in the frequency domain, but they often overlook local variations critical to physical behavior, such as sharp changes in permeability in Darcy flow. ESO addresses this by using edge-wise variations to condition the spectral mixing, preserving global approximation while adapting to local structures.

## [Fast LapSum: Exact Differentiable Top-k at Million Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06912) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Fast LapSum introduces the first exact-budget soft top-k primitive with a linear-time GPU solver, processing 10^6, 10^7, and 10^8 scores in 0.41, 1.15, and 5.23 ms, respectively. It maintains an exact selection mass of k while remaining fully differentiable end-to-end. This breakthrough makes exact soft top-k practical for large-scale models, enabling efficient sparse routing, retrieval, and optimization over millions of coordinates. It addresses a key bottleneck in differentiable sparse computation, potentially accelerating applications like sparse adversarial examples and differentiable sparse coding. The solver combines a linear-time threshold computation with an analytical vector-Jacobian product, and for extreme scales uses probabilistic bracketing to sort only the uncertain middle band of kernel-noised scores. Unlike prior linear-time methods like DFTopK, Fast LapSum preserves an exact selection mass of k without relaxing the normalization constraint.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: The top-k operation is fundamental in sparse computation, but hard top-k blocks gradients, and existing soft relaxations are too costly for large-scale models. Fast LapSum builds on the LapSum-based soft top-k approach, which uses the Laplace CDF to produce soft weights, and optimizes it for GPU execution.

This study systematically investigates how prompt-conditioned emotions affect LLM-based price negotiation, revealing significant role-dependent effects on deal rates and prices across 36 emotion-pair settings and five LLMs. This research highlights the practical implications of emotional conditioning in AI agents, particularly for commerce and human-AI interaction, and underscores the need for careful design of emotion-aware agents to avoid unintended outcomes. The study used a controlled framework with buyer and seller agents assigned one of six emotional states, negotiating over 350 real consumer products under two budget conditions. Key findings include angry buyers almost never reaching agreement (0.39% deal rate) while happy buyers agree most often (28.91%) but obtain worse prices than fearful buyers.

## [SEE Benchmark Reveals MLLMs Fall Short in Real Scientific Discovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06931) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Science Edge Evaluation (SEE), a multimodal benchmark of expert-curated questions in chemistry, biology, and materials science, and evaluates 19 multimodal large language models (MLLMs). The best model achieves only 48.7% accuracy, and tool use improves this to 52.7%. This benchmark highlights a critical gap in current MLLMs' ability to perform evidence-bounded reasoning essential for real scientific discovery. It underscores that tool use alone does not guarantee reliable reasoning, guiding future research toward improving models' capacity to derive novel insights from experimental data. The evaluation includes 19 MLLMs, with general-purpose models outperforming science-specialized ones on average. In the visual-agent evaluation, tool use increased the best accuracy from 48.7% to 52.7%, but more information did not necessarily lead to reliable scientific reasoning, as models struggled to manage tool-derived information within the boundaries of original experimental evidence.

## [Aggregate Metrics Miss Pivotal Vote Benefits in LLM Judge Panels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06940) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.06940) shows that verification signals in LLM judge panels only improve accuracy on pivotal one-vote-margin decisions, with gains of +10.4 to +23.3 percentage points, while aggregate independence metrics show no change. The paper demonstrates that population-level dependence diagnostics and margin-stratified utility are complementary, not substitutes. This finding challenges the common use of aggregate independence metrics to evaluate LLM judge panels, suggesting they can mask where verification actually helps. It provides a practical rule for when to invoke verification signals, potentially improving evaluation efficiency and accuracy in code generation benchmarks and beyond. The paper uses elementary majority arithmetic to show that only one-vote-margin decisions can change with a single-ballot substitution. Across three code benchmarks and four panel sizes, the accuracy gain is entirely concentrated on pivotal queries, with a majority-side replacement rule raising overall accuracy from 82.44% to 85.62% on HumanEval+/MBPP+ while invoking the signal on only 16.2% of queries.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: LLM judge panels are a standard evaluation tool where multiple LLMs vote on output quality, but prior work found that panel errors are highly correlated, reducing effective information. Verification signals, such as executing a test suite, are a natural remedy, but aggregate metrics like effective-vote count showed no distinguishable change. This paper clarifies that aggregate dependence and conditional decision utility are different questions, and that the benefit of verification is concentrated on pivotal votes.

## [Biologically Plausible Learning Under Dale's Constraint](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06963) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a neural architecture that respects Dale's principle by using non-negative activations and fixed-sign synapses, while still enabling backpropagation-like learning through two complementary non-negative channels. The approach theoretically recovers the exact backpropagation update and empirically outperforms vanilla networks on Tiny ImageNet. This work bridges neuroscience and deep learning by addressing a fundamental constraint of biological neurons that most artificial models ignore. It could lead to more realistic neural computation models and inspire new learning algorithms that are both biologically plausible and practically effective. The architecture uses two interacting non-negative channels to represent positive and negative contributions, inspired by on-off representations in the brain. The learning rule is local and Hebbian, and the model achieves substantial gains over comparable vanilla networks on Tiny ImageNet.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Dale's principle states that a neuron releases the same neurotransmitter at all of its synapses, meaning it is either excitatory or inhibitory, not both, and synapses cannot change sign. Most artificial neural networks allow mixed-sign activations and weights, violating this biological constraint. Biologically plausible learning aims to explain how neural circuits can learn under real constraints, and this paper proposes a solution using non-negative channels.

This paper introduces column-tiled SVD, a method that extracts interpretable 'mechanism mounts' directly from linear layers of neural networks, each represented as a triple (trigger, write, strength). The method was evaluated on Gemma-2-2B using WikiText-2, achieving full A/B/C scores on residual writes and passing all 182/182 site-layer checks. This work offers an alternative to sparse autoencoders for mechanistic interpretability, directly extracting mechanisms from network weights rather than proxy dictionaries. It could provide more faithful and interpretable insights into LLM internals, potentially improving model auditing and safety research. The method evaluates seven linear maps in Gemma-2-2B, with residual writes (mlp.down, attn.o) receiving full A/B/C scores after post-sublayer RMSNorm and passing 52/52 site-layers, while other maps receive A/B only. The authors release library code, corpus builder, experiment entrypoint, and unit tests.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Mechanistic interpretability aims to reverse-engineer neural networks by understanding their internal algorithms and circuits. Sparse autoencoders are a common tool that learns sparse dictionaries to identify features, but these dictionaries are proxies and may not reflect the actual weights. Singular Value Decomposition (SVD) is a matrix factorization technique that can decompose weights into components, and tiled SVD applies this in a block-wise manner to handle large matrices efficiently.

The paper introduces an unsupervised finetuning framework for PDE foundation models that eliminates the need for ground-truth solution data, using a physics-based objective and a new Newton-Schulz orthogonalized LoRA variant (NSLoRA). This work addresses a critical limitation in adapting PDE foundation models to unseen equations, where dense solution data is often expensive or unavailable. The framework pretrains a neighborhood attention Transformer on diverse time-dependent PDEs, then finetunes via LoRA with a physics-based objective. NSLoRA mitigates rank collapse by orthogonalizing the low-rank updates, achieving performance comparable to supervised LoRA without ground-truth solutions.

## [BONSAI: Evolvability-Guided Tree Search for Skill Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07056) ⭐️ 8.0/10

BONSAI is a novel skill-optimization framework that uses evolvability-guided Monte Carlo tree search to improve text-based skills for frozen agents, outperforming fitness-only baselines. It lifts heldout accuracy by 23.13 points over skill-free agents and improves on GEPA and SkillOpt by 3.87 and 3.97 points respectively. This work addresses a key limitation in skill optimization—fitness-only selection can get stuck on narrow overfit spikes—by incorporating evolvability, a concept from biology. It could improve the efficiency and robustness of optimizing prompts and skills for large frozen models, impacting AI/ML research and applications. BONSAI grows skills as a Monte Carlo search tree where each child is a mutation of its parent, using an upper-confidence selection rule that blends a skill's own fitness with the fitness of its mutational neighborhood. The mean score beneath a node estimates evolvability at no extra cost, and the framework ships the single best-scoring document at no cost beyond the accept-if-better loop it replaces.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: A skill is a natural-language document that steers a frozen agent whose weights cannot be updated, so any capability the agent lacks must be supplied in prose. Optimizing a skill means optimizing text against a score, but a single score cannot distinguish a document on a narrow overfit spike from one on a broad plateau, even though only the latter can still be improved. Evolvability, the capacity of a region of document space to keep producing viable variation under further mutation, is treated by biology as separate from present fitness. BONSAI leverages this concept to guide search toward regions that keep improving.

## [PTQ4SNN: Membrane-Aware Post-Training Quantization for SNNs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07066) ⭐️ 8.0/10

PTQ4SNN introduces a post-training quantization framework that jointly quantizes weights and recurrent membrane states in spiking neural networks (SNNs) using only a small calibration set. It employs a channel-wise Unified Scale Bridge and mixed-precision bit allocation to enable low-bit deployment without retraining. This work addresses a critical gap in SNN deployment, as previous methods often left membrane states in floating point, limiting efficiency gains. By enabling low-bit membrane quantization, PTQ4SNN could significantly improve the practicality of SNNs on edge devices, impacting fields like neuromorphic computing and efficient AI inference. The framework operates on reusable projection-LIF pairs and supports both convolutional SNNs and spike-driven Transformers. Experiments on static and event-based classification and semantic segmentation show that PTQ4SNN preserves accuracy under W4 quantization and approximately 4-bit membrane precision.

**Background**: Spiking neural networks (SNNs) mimic biological neural networks by using discrete spikes as information carriers, enabling sparse and event-driven computation. Quantization is a common technique to compress neural networks by converting weights and activations to low-bit representations, but SNNs have recurrent membrane states that are difficult to quantize due to their varying distributions and sensitivity near firing thresholds. Post-training quantization (PTQ) is a method that quantizes a pre-trained model without retraining, using a small calibration set to determine quantization parameters.

## [DiDPO: Diff-in-Diff Policy Optimization for Fine-Grained Credit Assignment in Coding Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07147) ⭐️ 8.0/10

DiDPO introduces a critic-free reinforcement learning method that constructs fine-grained credit units from code diff structures, enabling more precise credit assignment in coding agent training. It outperforms strong agentic RL baselines by over 10% on Qwen2.5-7B-Coder and narrows the gap with larger models. This work addresses a critical limitation in RLVR for coding agents by providing a principled framework for fine-grained credit assignment, which could significantly improve the efficiency and effectiveness of training coding agents. It also open-sources verl-code, an agentic RL codebase, facilitating further research and adoption. DiDPO organizes multi-turn coding interactions into thought-action steps, discovers code diffs across sampled trajectories, and uses a 'groupability score' to split diffs into anchors that form advantage groups, projecting diff-level advantage back to individual tokens. Experiments on long-horizon coding and reasoning benchmarks show significant improvements.

**Background**: Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) uses execution feedback from compilation and tests as objective verification for training coding agents. However, coding actions often pack multiple changes into different regions of a code version, making it hard to assign credit to individual changes. Traditional methods rely on outcome or step-level rewards, which fail to capture the fine-grained structure of code diffs. DiDPO addresses this by leveraging the structure of code diffs to create fine-grained credit units.

Niyam-AI introduces a framework that locks permitted tools and constraints into an Intent Contract committed via SHA-256, and uses an isolated Judge model to validate every tool call, generating a zk-SNARK proof via EZKL before execution. In evaluations on 2,000 scenarios from Agent-SafetyBench, it achieves an F1 score of 88.5% with a 1.1% false-positive rate, significantly outperforming NeMo Guardrails, Llama Prompt Guard 2, and GPT-OSS-Safeguard. It could set a new standard for AI safety, enabling third parties to trust agent behavior without accessing internal model weights, and mitigating risks like prompt injection. Proof generation adds 2260.6 ± 218.4 ms per approved action, while verification takes 53.1 ± 11.8 ms. The authors note that the classifier was adapted to Agent-SafetyBench and evaluated against zero-shot baselines, a distinction discussed in Section IV.C.

EZKL is a library for generating zk-SNARK proofs for machine learning models, enabling verifiable inference.

## [EMAS: Evidence-Guided Evolution for Multi-Agent Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07196) ⭐️ 8.0/10

EMAS introduces an evidence-guided approach to revise multi-agent system (MAS) topology and prompts based on runtime experience, without updating LLM parameters. It achieves significant accuracy gains and cost reductions across benchmarks and LLMs. This addresses a key limitation in current MAS design where systems are deployed unchanged after initial design, ignoring valuable runtime experience. By enabling continuous, evidence-based evolution, EMAS can improve performance and efficiency, impacting AI/ML and software engineering practices. EMAS converts execution traces into structured diagnoses that specify a revision operation and target, generating candidate revisions only when the same diagnosis recurs. It applies revisions only if paired validation against the current MAS meets acceptance criteria, ensuring stability. On MBPP with Qwen3.6-27B, it raised accuracy from 55.09% to 89.12% while reducing token use per task by 62.2%.

EMAS leverages runtime experience to revise the system, similar to evolutionary algorithms that iteratively improve solutions.

## [AutoML Benchmark Flaws Exposed: Test-Set Leakage and Budget Overruns](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07303) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.07303) demonstrates that the Orcetra AutoML system's reported wins over FLAML and AutoGluon were inflated by protocol defects, including scoring candidates on the test split and failing to enforce time budgets during execution. After correcting these issues, Orcetra's win rate dropped from 59.4% to 34.3%, with no significant pairwise differences remaining. This work highlights critical methodological flaws in short-budget AutoML comparisons, which are common in tool READMEs and workshop papers. It underscores the importance of rigorous evaluation practices to ensure reproducibility and trust in AutoML benchmarks, affecting both developers and users who rely on these comparisons for tool selection. The study found that Orcetra consumed a median of 120 seconds against a 60-second budget, 2.24x the wall-clock time used by AutoGluon. The selection bias from test-set peeking grows with the number of candidates K but reaches only 0.27 accuracy points, about five times below the theoretical bound, because shared test rows cancel most of the noise.

## [FinRank: Evidence-Grounded Benchmark for Financial QA over SEC Filings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07400) ⭐️ 8.0/10

FinRank is a new benchmark for financial question answering over SEC filings, containing 1,185 manually authored question-answer records from 10-K and 10-Q filings of 22 companies. It introduces hand-curated hard negatives and defines separate tasks for passage retrieval, reranking, and hard-negative discrimination. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in financial QA evaluation by focusing on evidence grounding and provenance, which is essential for real-world applications where answers must be supported by the correct disclosure. It provides a rigorous test for retrieval and reranking systems, highlighting the limitations of current models and guiding future research. Baseline results show that even a 7B instruction-tuned embedder achieves only 44.8% Recall@10 on the pooled evidence corpus, and sub-billion-parameter encoders gain at most 3.5 points over BM25. A finance-adapted embedder trails BM25 by 9.7 points, and pairwise accuracy drops by 13.0-20.5 percentage points when random negatives are replaced with curated hard negatives.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Financial question answering over SEC filings is challenging because similar facts and disclosures recur across sections, reporting periods, and comparable firms, making it easy for models to retrieve plausible but incorrect evidence. Traditional benchmarks often evaluate only answer correctness, ignoring the provenance of the evidence. FinRank targets this provenance-sensitive retrieval problem by requiring systems to identify evidence for the intended entity, reporting period, and disclosure context.

ResidencyRL introduces a reinforcement learning method that trains clinical AI agents through simulated multi-turn patient encounters, achieving a 7.0% improvement in diagnostic accuracy under adversarial conditions and a 31% reduction in missed red flags. CoBa introduces a compute-balanced routing policy that allocates test-time compute between generation, verification, and stopping. It achieves 85.13% macro accuracy on 3,129 evaluations, matching a self-evaluation weighted-voting proxy (85.20%) while using 49.1% fewer parameter-weighted tokens. This work addresses a key challenge in LLM inference efficiency by optimizing compute allocation across multiple axes, rather than scaling a single dimension. It shows that cost-effective test-time scaling can match stronger baselines, potentially reducing inference costs for reasoning tasks. CoBa first obtains a small set of candidates, applies cheap verification broadly, and routes uncertain or high-value candidates to stronger verification. It also matches best-of-16 majority voting within 0.01 macro-accuracy points while using 58.9% fewer parameter-weighted tokens, though paired tests retain a small edge for best-of-16 at higher cost.

**Background**: Test-time scaling typically involves spending more compute on sampling, chain-of-thought, or stronger evaluators, but these choices compete under a fixed budget. The approach is evaluated on benchmarks like MATH-500, AIME 2024/2025, and AMC 2023, covering procedural symbolic reasoning.

A new arXiv paper demonstrates that encoding time-series data as 2D plots with Vision-Language Models (VLMs) reduces input tokens by 3.6-10.4x and inference energy by 1.8-2.5x across Llama-3.2-90B, Qwen2.5-VL-72B, and Pixtral-12B, while improving anomaly detection precision by 220.7% over text-only counterparts. This work addresses a critical inefficiency in AI inference, where over 90% of operational energy is consumed during inference and scales with token count. By making VLMs a viable, energy-efficient alternative for numerical time-series analysis, it paves the way for sustainable AI deployments in telecom and edge computing, potentially reducing operational costs and environmental impact. The study found that at 24 KPIs, text representations exceed the 128K context window of most production LLMs, making text-only processing infeasible without truncation, while visual representations remain within standard limits. Additionally, Pixtral-12B achieved a 20.6x improvement in J/F1 score at mean F1=0.82 on public benchmarks, and the energy savings translate to approximately 7.2 MJ/day at telecom edge deployments monitoring 200 cells per 15-minute interval.

## [TEPA: Revoking Stale Memories for Conflict-Robust Language Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07429) ⭐️ 8.0/10

TEPA introduces a revocable evidence-memory mechanism that makes validity an explicit state of memory, representing observations as keyed precedents and revoking active precedents when contradicted by fresh evidence. In controlled drift experiments, TEPA achieved a score of 0.950 compared to 0.210 for append-only and last-write-wins memory, and 0.309 for no memory. This work addresses the underexplored problem of memory pollution in language agents, where stale memories degrade performance when the world changes. By introducing lifecycle revocation as a core memory operation, TEPA could significantly improve the robustness and reliability of AI agents in dynamic environments, influencing future memory management designs. TEPA represents observations as keyed precedents and revokes active precedents when fresh evidence contradicts them under the same key, while preserving revoked history for audit. Boundary tests on multi-hop and very long-context MemoryAgentBench settings reveal retrieval-chain and context-selection bottlenecks beyond fact-level validity tracking.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Language agents rely on long-term memory to reuse past facts, preferences, and task experience. However, when the world changes, stale memories can remain retrievable and pollute the prompt, a failure mode known as memory pollution. TEPA addresses this by making validity an explicit state of memory and enabling revocation of outdated information.

## [Muon-Trained Transformers Show Post-Grokking Collapse](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07436) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new preprint (arXiv:2608.07436) reports that transformers trained with the Muon optimizer exhibit a post-grokking collapse, where solutions that initially generalize are later lost, across all nine configurations tested on modular addition. This failure is traced to instability at the representation-readout interface, a phenomenon not observed with AdamW. This finding highlights a critical failure mode in a popular optimizer, potentially affecting practitioners who use Muon for training transformers. It underscores the importance of optimizer dynamics in achieving stable generalization, and may prompt further research into optimizer-specific behaviors. The failure occurs when gradients drop to ~1e-6, with Muon showing negative step-size elasticity (-0.03) versus AdamW's +1.5, and Muon groups moving 8x faster per parameter. Freezing either the embeddings/readout or the hidden layers prevents collapse, and the issue persists across different moduli, widths, training fractions, and tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Grokking is a phenomenon where neural networks suddenly generalize long after overfitting the training data. The Muon optimizer, which uses orthogonalization and normalization, has been shown to accelerate grokking, but this paper reveals a potential instability. The representation-readout interface refers to the connection between the transformer's hidden layers and the final output layer, which is crucial for task performance.

Blast Radius, a predictive memory management layer, reduces token consumption by 17-26% in agentic coding through reversible context eviction and recurring dead matter identification. This innovation addresses the growing cost and token waste in agentic coding, making LLM applications more affordable and sustainable. Of 450 buried bodies, 378 were recurring dead matter and zero were recalled, demonstrating the effectiveness of the RDM identification.

The paper introduces Agolic, an agentic planning system that uses an LLM-based agent to plan and configure bounded symbolic execution (BSE) runs, improving branch coverage on C/C++ programs. In evaluations, Agolic extends branch coverage beyond continuous symbolic execution and covers more than 3x as many branches on average. This work demonstrates significant untapped potential in existing symbolic execution tools by reasoning about how they are used across runs, potentially leading to more effective software testing and program analysis. It combines LLM-based agentic planning with symbolic execution, a novel approach that could inspire further research and practical improvements in the field. The evaluation covers several C and C++ programs, and Agolic also covers more branches than each individual corpus from coverage-guided fuzzing and compiler-based concolic execution, reaching branches absent from all comparison corpora combined on six of seven programs.

**Background**: Symbolic execution is a program analysis technique that explores feasible program paths by treating inputs as symbolic values, but practical runs may exhaust resources before covering all behavior. Agentic planning is an AI paradigm where an agent decomposes complex goals into sub-tasks and plans actions autonomously, often using LLMs for reasoning. This paper combines these concepts to improve coverage by planning BSE runs across multiple executions.

## [New Diagnostic Ladder Separates Decision-Rule and Readout Gaps in Speech LMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06409) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a generation-aligned diagnostic ladder that decomposes answer accuracy in speech language models into endpoint, decision-rule, and readout-coverage gaps. Across five systems and two emotion corpora, state decoding outperforms generation by 27.8 accuracy points on average, and a label-free logit correction improves accuracy in every condition. This work provides a systematic framework for localizing performance losses in speech language models, distinguishing information availability from behavioral use. It has practical implications for improving model evaluation and guiding targeted interventions, benefiting researchers and practitioners working on paralinguistic tasks like emotion recognition. The diagnostic ladder compares emitted answers, option logits, an affine readout of those logits, and a linear readout of the hidden state at the same answer token. The decision-rule and readout-coverage gaps are positive in all ten conditions, and rank-matched comparisons show that emotion information outside the native readout generalizes to held-out speakers and survives acoustic controls.

**Background**: Speech language models are increasingly evaluated on paralinguistic tasks by the accuracy of prompted answers, but this accuracy conflates failures at different stages of the audio-to-answer computation. The proposed diagnostic ladder separates these stages, allowing researchers to identify whether errors stem from the decision rule (how logits are converted to answers) or from readout coverage (whether the relevant information is present in the hidden states). This approach builds on prior work on logit-based analysis and training-free logit correction methods.

ED-CSP is a new machine learning framework that predicts 3D crystal structures from chemical composition, atom count, and multiple detector-plane electron diffraction spot sets. It combines a relational set encoder, permutation-invariant multi-view aggregation, and a periodic flow generator to jointly predict lattice parameters and fractional atomic coordinates. This work addresses a challenging inverse problem in materials science, enabling structure determination from sparse electron diffraction data without relying on indexed reflections or structure libraries. It outperforms a state-of-the-art PXRD-based model on a benchmark, suggesting potential for accelerating materials discovery and analysis. The model was trained on ED-CS, a dataset of 4.85 million simulated multi-view ED crystal structures, deduplicated across seven materials repositories and filtered to exclude CHILI-100K overlaps. On 2,075 held-out CHILI-100K materials, ED-CSP achieves 57.49% MR@5, outperforming PXRDGen (52.92%), and scaling to one million structures raises MR@5 to 66.27%.

Traditional methods often require indexed reflections or rely on known structure libraries, whereas generative machine learning models can directly predict structures from raw data.

CyberForge is a new framework that synthesizes executable, repository-level security training data by injecting validated vulnerabilities into real C/C++ projects. It dynamically validates each instance by ensuring the injected build passes unit tests and that a proof-of-vulnerability triggers only on the injected build, resulting in a corpus of 1034 vulnerabilities across 80 projects and 63 weakness categories. This addresses a critical bottleneck in training defensive LLM agents for vulnerability discovery and patching, as reproducible security training data is scarce. By scaling beyond historical CVE data, CyberForge can significantly improve the performance of LLM-based security agents, with fine-tuning gains of +3.3 to +14.7 points on SEC-bench and generalization to other languages. CyberForge uses two complementary pipelines: fuzzer-guided injection and in-context agentic injection. The resulting corpus has edit locality similar to real CVE patches, and fine-tuning a 31B student model on this data allowed it to nearly match its GPT-5.4-mini teacher (72.7% vs 74.0%).

Traditional data augmentation relies on historical CVE data, which is limited in quantity and often lacks executable context. CyberForge overcomes this by synthesizing new vulnerabilities in real projects, ensuring they are validated and executable.

## [StepJack Benchmark Exposes Multi-Step Indirect Prompt Injection Risks in Computer-Use Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06477) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces StepJack, a benchmark with 480 test examples for multi-step indirect prompt injection attacks on computer-use agents (CUAs). It shows that these attacks increase attack success rates (ASR) on three of six state-of-the-art CUAs, with GPT-5.4-mini's ASR rising from 41.7% at single-step to 72.9% at three-step. This work highlights a significant security vulnerability in CUAs, which are increasingly used to automate tasks across web and desktop environments. By demonstrating that decomposing attacks into innocuous sub-steps can bypass existing defenses, it underscores the need for more robust safety measures in AI agents. The attack decomposition pipeline optimizes each sub-step for innocuousness while ensuring the original goal is achieved. Averaged over five CUAs that reliably follow the reference chain (excluding EvoCUA-32B), ASR rises from 31.3% at single-step to 36.9% at three-step. The dataset and code are available at https://github.com/BorealisAI/StepJack.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Computer-use agents (CUAs) are AI systems that can see and interact with software interfaces, such as browsers and desktop apps. Indirect prompt injection occurs when an LLM accepts input from external sources, like websites or files, which can contain adversarial instructions. Multi-step indirect prompt injection is a new attack class where the adversarial goal is decomposed into multiple innocuous-looking sub-steps distributed across a chain of pages.

## [LyEvO: Lyapunov-Guided Evolutionary Optimization for Safe Sim-to-Real Policy Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06481) ⭐️ 8.0/10

LyEvO introduces a novel framework that combines Lyapunov-based stability analysis, constrained evolutionary optimization, and statistical model checking (SMC) to jointly optimize and verify policies for safe sim-to-real transfer. It provides a deployment readiness criterion and demonstrates effectiveness on Cartpole and 3D Quadrotor benchmarks through simulations and real-world experiments. This work addresses a critical challenge in robotics: ensuring that policies trained in simulation are safe and robust when deployed in the real world. By providing a systematic verification and deployment readiness criterion, LyEvO could significantly improve the reliability of sim-to-real transfer, benefiting the reinforcement learning and control communities. LyEvO leverages prior knowledge of system dynamics to compute an initial candidate stability region via Lyapunov analysis, then iteratively optimizes and statistically verifies policies using operational scenarios drawn from this region, expanding the region based on verification outcomes. The framework is validated on Cartpole and 3D Quadrotor benchmarks, including targeted real-world experiments, demonstrating safe and robust transfer.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Sim-to-real transfer in reinforcement learning involves training policies in simulation and deploying them in the real world, but discrepancies such as friction and sensor noise can cause failures. Lyapunov stability analysis is a classical control theory method to assess the stability of dynamical systems near equilibrium points. Statistical model checking (SMC) is a verification technique that uses hypothesis testing to check probabilistic models, especially when they are large and complex. LyEvO integrates these concepts to jointly optimize and verify policies, providing a practical deployment readiness criterion.

The study uses the Big Five traits as a psychometric anchor and compares event-induced shifts against longitudinal human psychology data. Validation checks confirm that measured shifts exceed no-event retest noise, remain stable under paraphrased prompts, and persist across unrelated dialogue, but show limited convergence with scenario-based behavioral choices.

## [Agentic AI: Empowerment or Enclosure?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06510) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.06510) argues that the empowerment potential of agentic AI is shaped by political decisions embedded in technical arrangements, using case studies of ad blockers, recommender systems, robo-advisors, and email spam governance to show how these decisions often depoliticize technology and can undermine collective contestation. This analysis provides a novel interdisciplinary framework for understanding agentic AI governance, highlighting that technical choices like API protocols and default settings are political decisions with significant power implications. It offers valuable insights for AI governance and system design, potentially influencing how future agentic systems are built to either empower users or consolidate control. The paper identifies 'depoliticization' as a key concept, where technical decisions obscure their political nature, leading to situations where individual outcomes improve while collective contestation capacity declines, as seen in email spam governance. It applies this lens to current agentic AI governance, pointing to the Model Context Protocol and the Agentic AI Foundation as sites where these configurations are being settled before user and public influence wanes.

**Background**: Agentic AI refers to AI systems that act autonomously on behalf of users, pursuing goals over multiple steps without per-step human approval. The paper draws on political theory, particularly the concept of depoliticization, which in technology means separating political concerns from engineering work, treating technical decisions as purely technical rather than political. This background helps readers understand the paper's argument that technical arrangements in AI systems are not neutral but carry political implications.

## [TradeVerse: New Benchmark for Longitudinal Political Negotiation in Trade](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06549) ⭐️ 8.0/10

TradeVerse is a new benchmark built from WTO specific trade concerns, reconstructing 1,170 meeting minutes across 5 groups and 89 product groups, and defining three tasks: predicting HS codes, guessing the responding country, and generating the final statement. It is the first benchmark to test LLMs on longitudinal political trade negotiations. This benchmark addresses a gap in existing LLM benchmarks, which typically evaluate on isolated documents or single tasks, by focusing on longitudinal negotiation data where context accumulates over time. It provides a challenging testbed for LLMs in understanding complex institutional and political texts, potentially driving improvements in AI's ability to handle multi-turn reasoning and real-world negotiation scenarios. The dataset is derived from WTO specific trade concerns, with all labels recovered directly from proceedings without manual annotation. The three tasks include predicting harmonized system (HS) codes, identifying the responding country from anonymized content, and generating the final statement as the responding country, with experiments showing current LLMs struggle on these tasks.

This paper introduces a neural ODE-based regularization method that enforces latent state embeddings to follow consistent ODE flows, aligning representation learning with environment dynamics. Integrated into Actor-Critic algorithms, it yields major performance gains on Atari benchmarks for A2C and gridworld environments for PPO. This work addresses a fundamental misalignment between latent representations and environment dynamics in RL, which can hinder learning efficiency and performance. By providing a general regularization technique applicable to deep learning agents, it has the potential to improve a wide range of RL algorithms and advance representation learning in sequential decision-making. The method draws an analogy between MDP trajectories and ODE flows, modeling latent dynamics explicitly. It is broadly applicable to deep learning agents, with demonstrated effectiveness in A2C and PPO, achieving major performance gains on standard Atari benchmarks and gridworld environments.

## [Cryptanalytic Extraction of GLU Feed-Forward Blocks via Antipodal Separation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06631) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a constructive, multi-stage query-based method to extract bias-free GLU feed-forward blocks from modern language models, achieving sub-percent median validation error on real components such as Qwen, Llama, and Gemma. This work extends cryptanalytic model extraction to GLU-based architectures, which are prevalent in modern LLMs, potentially impacting model interpretability and security. It demonstrates that even complex two-branch structures can be recovered from black-box queries, raising concerns about model confidentiality. The method uses finite-difference curvature to identify gate-direction candidates and paired observations at x and -x to separate gate magnitude, orientation, and value-branch coupling. Experiments on high-precision targets, including six Qwen layers, an 8,192-unit Llama subproblem, and a full-dimensional Gemma block, all achieved sub-percent median validation error, though four finite-precision configurations remained below 5% median error but did not reproduce every stored weight.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Cryptanalytic model extraction treats neural network weight recovery as a cryptographic problem, where an attacker with black-box query access aims to reconstruct the model's parameters. Previous work successfully extracted ReLU networks, networks with componentwise activations like GELU or SiLU, and final projection matrices, but not GLU feed-forward blocks, which are common in modern LLMs. GLU blocks multiply an activated linear projection by a second learned linear projection within each hidden unit, creating a two-branch structure that complicates extraction. This paper addresses that gap with a novel antipodal separation technique.

## [Krum-Proxy Attack Bypasses Byzantine-Robust Aggregation in Federated Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06637) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces the Krum-Proxy attack, a selection-aware backdoor injection strategy that adaptively optimizes malicious updates to bypass Byzantine-robust aggregation methods like Krum and Multi-Krum in federated learning. This attack uses a two-stage optimization procedure to craft adversarial updates that lie in regions favored during aggregation. This attack highlights a critical vulnerability in distance-based robust aggregation methods, which are widely used to secure federated learning systems. It demonstrates that adaptive adversaries can consistently bypass these defenses, raising concerns about the security of federated learning in adversarial environments and motivating the development of more robust aggregation techniques. The Krum-Proxy attack uses a nearest-neighbor proxy, stochastic reference modeling, and anchor-guided alignment to optimize malicious updates, and includes a projection mechanism to keep them within realistic norm and variance bounds. Experiments on standard federated learning benchmarks show that it achieves higher attack success while preserving clean accuracy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Federated learning trains a global model by aggregating updates from multiple clients, but malicious clients can inject backdoors. Byzantine-robust aggregation methods like Krum and Multi-Krum are designed to mitigate such attacks by selecting updates closest to the majority, assuming benign updates form a compact cluster. However, these methods rely on geometric properties that can be exploited by adaptive adversaries.

## [MI-MIDI: Mechanistic Interpretability of Text-to-MIDI Generation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06638) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper applies mechanistic interpretability methods to two text-to-MIDI models, text2midi and MIDI-LLM, revealing architecture-dependent musical structure and demonstrating steering capabilities. It is the first systematic analysis of symbolic music generation models using probing, lenses, activation patching, and steering. This work extends mechanistic interpretability to symbolic music generation, an area previously focused on audio models, providing insights into how architecture shapes internal representations. The findings on linear decodability and steering could inform future model design and control, benefiting both interpretability and music generation research. The study uses linear probing, logit and tuned lenses, activation patching, and difference-in-means steering on text2midi and MIDI-LLM (a Llama 3.2 1B model extended with MIDI tokens). Results show that pitch, instrumentation, harmony, and texture are linearly decodable in both models, but text2midi refines predictions gradually while MIDI-LLM shows a sharp late rotation into musical vocabulary. Steering produces bidirectional changes in register and polyphony in both systems, and in tempo/energy in MIDI-LLM.

The logit lens and tuned lens are techniques to inspect intermediate representations of transformers, with the tuned lens providing a more reliable mapping.

A large-scale industrial study from Google analyzed 3.52 million C++ code changes from April 2025 to April 2026, revealing that AI-generated code has distinct quality issues, including higher coupling, allocation overheads, and a 5-8% increase in compute resource consumption. Targeted feedback reduced static analysis warnings by 11.1%. This study provides empirical evidence on the quality impact of AI-generated code in production at massive scale, addressing a critical concern for the software engineering industry. The findings highlight the need for observability and targeted feedback mechanisms to mitigate quality degradation, affecting developers, engineering managers, and AI tool vendors. The study tracked code changes in a brownfield codebase, finding that AI-generated C++ code relies more on explicit loops over optimized standard APIs, and exhibits higher interface and coupling burdens. The 11.1% reduction in warnings came from taxonomy-informed feedback provided to the models.
