## [SoRoMoX: JAX-Based Soft Robot Modeling with GPU Speed](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06650) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SoRoMoX introduces a fully numerical, JIT-compilable Python/JAX framework for soft robot modeling, implementing articulated, Piecewise Constant Strain, and Variable Strain models through a unified, control-ready interface. It is the first rod/strain-based soft robot modeling framework that runs directly on GPUs and is end-to-end differentiable with respect to states, inputs, and parameters. This framework significantly accelerates soft robot simulation and enables differentiable, GPU-parallel workflows that are crucial for control and learning applications. It bridges a critical gap in soft robotics, allowing advanced techniques like reinforcement learning and safety-constrained control to be applied more efficiently. Sequential CPU rollouts are up to 18.1x faster than state-of-the-art alternatives, while GPU-parallel rollouts increase throughput by up to 234.6x. The framework enables workflows such as static-equilibrium system identification with 66% lower marker RMSE, residual-force learning with a further 64% reduction, and computed-torque tracking with RMSE reduced by a factor of approximately 500 relative to model-free PD.

The paper introduces LivePlan, a mechanism that monitors and corrects programming agents in real time using a deterministic rule-based monitor to detect inefficiencies, and only invokes an advisor LLM when needed. Evaluated on SWE-bench Verified and Pro, it improves fix success rates by up to 15.2% (average 9.9%) over vanilla SWE-agent, with only $0.08 additional cost per instance. By decoupling judging from advising and using a deterministic monitor, it reduces costly LLM interventions, making long-horizon agent tasks more practical and cost-effective, which could accelerate adoption in real-world development workflows. LivePlan is implemented on top of SWE-agent and tested with five LLMs (three executors, two advisors). The deterministic monitor examines general trajectory signals without invoking an LLM, avoiding misleading re-planning and costly interventions. Additional solutions concentrate on medium and hard instances, with minimal regression on already successful runs and new successes on problems no baseline solves.

LivePlan introduces a decoupled design: a deterministic rule-based monitor detects issues cheaply, and an advisor LLM is only consulted when needed, providing high-level corrections.

## [PUSH: Scalable Long-Horizon Lifelong MAPF Planner](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06702) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces PUSH (Path Updates over Staggered Horizons), a novel lifelong multi-agent path finding (LMAPF) planner that coordinates thousands of agents in under a second while planning over multi-step horizons. It combines rule-based coordination from PIBT/EPIBT with windowed planning from RHCR and subset planning from TP, achieving high throughput on general maps. This work addresses a critical trade-off in LMAPF between scalability and long-horizon reasoning, which is essential for real-world applications like warehouse robotics and autonomous fleets. By enabling long-horizon planning at massive scale, PUSH could improve efficiency and reliability in large-scale multi-agent systems. PUSH plans only a subset of agents at each timestep using staggered planning windows, similar to TP, but without restrictive map assumptions. It integrates EPIBT-inspired priority inheritance, backtracking, and anytime improvements to maintain high throughput in congested environments. Empirical evaluations show PUSH scales to 10k agents while achieving significantly higher system throughput than all baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Lifelong Multi-Agent Path Finding (LMAPF) requires continuously generating collision-free paths for agents that receive new goals over time. Reactive methods like PIBT scale well but are myopic, while windowed methods like RHCR provide long-horizon planning but are computationally heavy. TP reduces complexity by planning subsets but only works on structured maps. PUSH aims to combine the strengths of these approaches.

## [Dueling World Models: Subtracting Distractors at Readout Time](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06706) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a simple read-time subtraction method based on dueling decomposition to cancel action-independent distractor effects in latent world models, improving action-conditioned predictions without extra losses. This provides a minimal, assumption-free solution to the action-blindness problem in world models, potentially improving model-based reinforcement learning across various domains. It works with frozen pretrained models, making it broadly applicable. The method subtracts the mean effect over actions from the prediction, canceling action-independent variation. It is proven exact for discrete and sampled action sets, with a measured boundary for distractors that track the action.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Latent world models predict future states from actions, but distractors (motion not controlled by the agent) can make predictions action-blind. Dueling decomposition, from dueling Q-learning, separates state value from action advantage, which this paper adapts to latent dynamics.

## [Hidden Gauge Controls Feature Specialization in ReLU Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06766) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper demonstrates that a positive-homogeneous scaling gauge, invisible to the initial predictor, can deterministically control which neuron learns a feature in overparameterized ReLU networks, leading to a sharp Θ(D²) separation in specialization time. This finding challenges the assumption that the initial function determines learning dynamics, offering new insights into feature learning and training dynamics in neural networks. It could inform initialization strategies and theoretical understanding of feature specialization. The paper uses a Gaussian teacher-student model, fixing the initial function while varying only the scaling gauge. It proves global selection and functional pruning, extends to visible perturbations and small-step full-batch gradient descent, and verifies predictions in population and finite-sample training.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: In overparameterized neural networks, multiple neurons may start with identical functional roles, but during training one may specialize to a feature while others become redundant. Positive-homogeneous functions, which scale linearly with input, are common in ReLU networks without bias terms. The scaling gauge refers to a reparameterization that changes neuron weights without altering the network's output function.

## [Genotypic Triggers: Backdoor Attacks on Antimicrobial Peptide Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06779) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces 'Genotypic Triggers,' a backdoor attack on generative antimicrobial peptide (AMP) models that increases predicted immunogenicity risk specifically for carriers of a targeted HLA allele. Across popular peptide generation models, the attack raised the predicted immunogenicity risk score for target-allele carriers by 743% on average, while non-carriers remained near baseline. This work exposes a critical blind spot in AI-driven drug discovery validation pipelines, where safety screens typically ignore genetic variability. It highlights a new security threat in personalized medicine, as backdoored models could produce peptides that pass conventional safety checks but pose adverse immune risks to specific genetic subgroups, potentially leading to harmful clinical outcomes. The attack shifts the generative distribution toward peptides with elevated predicted immunogenicity risk for carriers of a targeted HLA allele, while maintaining or improving primary desired properties such as antimicrobial potency and low general toxicity. The backdoored models' outputs thus pass conventional safety screens, making the attack stealthy and difficult to detect.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Large Language Models (LLMs) are increasingly used in drug discovery, particularly for designing antimicrobial peptides (AMPs). However, current validation pipelines overlook that certain drugs can pose health risks predominantly for individuals with specific genetic profiles, such as particular HLA alleles involved in immune presentation. Backdoor attacks are a known threat in machine learning, where models are manipulated to behave maliciously when triggered, but this is the first to target pharmacogenomic vulnerabilities in generative peptide models.

## [HLSmith: Expert-Guided LLM Framework for C/C++ to HLS Translation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06791) ⭐️ 8.0/10

HLSmith is a new framework that uses LLMs with an HLS optimization expertise library and a staged, feedback-driven orchestration flow to translate C/C++ programs into optimized FPGA accelerators. In evaluations on PolyBench, it achieved a geometric mean speedup of 4.24x over ChatHLS and produced functionally correct designs for all benchmarks, compared to ChatHLS's 57% valid-design rate. This framework addresses the high expertise barrier in FPGA development by automating the translation of C/C++ to high-performance HLS designs. It could significantly reduce development time and make FPGA acceleration more accessible to software engineers, potentially accelerating hardware design automation across many domains. HLSmith combines three components: an HLS optimization expertise library with guarded transformation recipes, a staged orchestration flow that guides agents through synthesis, bottleneck analysis, and optimization, and a tool-grounded model-adaptation pipeline that fine-tunes open-weight LLMs using trajectories from commercial frontier models. It achieved speedups up to 252x with commercial models and 138x with open-weight models.

**Background**: High-level synthesis (HLS) raises design abstraction by compiling C/C++ programs into register-transfer level (RTL) hardware, but it still requires hardware architecture design expertise. Large language models (LLMs) have shown strong software generation capabilities but lack the hardware intuition needed for HLS optimization. HLSmith encodes expert knowledge and orchestration to guide LLMs, addressing this gap.

## [LoRAScan Detects Backdoor Prompts in LoRA Adapters via Activation Spikes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06795) ⭐️ 8.0/10

LoRAScan is introduced as the first adapter-aware defense that detects and rejects trigger-bearing inputs at inference time without modifying adapter parameters. It identifies low-variance LoRA insertion sites that exhibit concentrated spikes in down-projection activations when a trigger is present, rejecting approximately 98.49% of malicious inputs with a small error rate on clean inputs. This addresses a critical security gap in the growing use of LoRA adapters, which are vulnerable to supply-chain backdoor attacks. By enabling safe use of potentially backdoored adapters, LoRAScan helps protect LLM deployments in real-world applications, especially where untrusted adapters are distributed. The method relies on the observation that about 5% of LoRA insertion sites remain stable across clean inputs but exhibit highly concentrated spikes in down-projection activations when a trigger is present. LoRAScan identifies these low-variance sites before deployment and monitors them during inference, outperforming existing defenses across standard LLM backdoor benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Low-rank adaptation (LoRA) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning technique that adds small trainable matrices to a frozen pre-trained model, enabling efficient specialization and distribution of large language models. However, untrusted LoRA adapters can be backdoored, causing the model to generate harmful content when a hidden trigger is present. Existing defenses either merge the adapter with the base model (diluting backdoor signals) or train a defensive adapter to repair a backdoored base model, but they do not address how to safely use a potentially backdoored adapter at inference time.

## [FutureBridge: Token Selection Beyond Local Preference in Collaborative Decoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06819) ⭐️ 8.0/10

FutureBridge introduces a token selection method for collaborative decoding that ranks joint LLM-SLM token candidates based on how well they support the SLM's subsequent reasoning, using counterfactual scores from a frozen SLM to train a lightweight reranker. On five mathematical reasoning benchmarks, it improves the Qwen3-1.7B SLM's Math Avg. by 35.1% relative to greedy SLM decoding. This addresses a key limitation of existing collaborative decoding methods that rely on the LLM's local preference, which may select tokens that are difficult for the SLM to build on. By considering future reasoning support, FutureBridge improves efficiency and performance in LLM-SLM collaboration, a relevant and active research area. During training, an answer-verified LLM trajectory supplies a fixed shared future, and a frozen SLM evaluates every candidate under this common context. At inference, FutureBridge uses the LLM only to expand the candidate pool, selects one token, and returns generation to the SLM without generating or appending a future suffix, averaging 4.8 LLM calls per token selection.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Collaborative decoding between large language models (LLMs) and small language models (SLMs) allows an LLM to assist an SLM when their predictions diverge. Existing methods either use LLM-generated intervention tokens or rank candidates with the LLM's next-token probabilities, both relying on the LLM's local preference. FutureBridge instead ranks candidates based on how well they support the SLM's subsequent reasoning, using counterfactual scores from a frozen SLM to train a lightweight reranker.

## [Data-Free Sparse Attention via Frozen Query-Key Geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06849) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Autonomy-of-Heads (AoH) introduces a data-free method to classify attention heads as retrieval or streaming based on the spectral geometry of query-key projections, specifically using the effective rank of the kernel attention operator M_h = W_K^h^T W_Q^h. This approach avoids runtime inputs and achieves 96.5% of Full Attention performance at 50% sparsity, reducing prefill and decode latency by up to 41.4% and 66.0%, and KV-cache memory by 50.0% at 256K tokens. This method could significantly reduce inference costs for long-context LLMs by enabling efficient sparse attention without needing calibration data or runtime attention scores. It offers a static, weight-based diagnosis that simplifies deployment and may complement existing dynamic methods like DuoAttention. AoH defines the kernel attention operator M_h = W_K^{h\top}W_Q^h and uses its effective rank as a weight-space measure: concentrated spectra indicate retrieval heads, diffuse spectra indicate streaming heads. It derives an efficient d_head-dimensional computation that avoids constructing the full d_model × d_model matrix, and experiments show strong performance across models at 50% sparsity.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Long-context LLM inference is bottlenecked by quadratic attention computation and growing KV-cache costs. Existing sparse attention methods often rely on runtime attention scores, observation windows, calibration prompts, or learned gates, making head diagnosis input-dependent and costly. Retrieval heads focus on specific historical tokens, while streaming heads attend to recent tokens; identifying them without data could enable static pruning of attention heads.

## [Text-Anchored Debiasing Improves Visual Counter-Commonsense Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06938) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper reveals that failures in visual counter-commonsense reasoning by multimodal large language models (MLLMs) stem from language priors, not visual perception, and introduces a text-anchored data construction pipeline with Fact-Frequency Distillation (FFD) and a post-training framework called TACT to debias the shared language decoder without visual training data. This challenges the common assumption that improving visual inputs is the key to better multimodal reasoning, potentially shifting research focus toward language decoder debiasing. It offers a cost-effective method that improves visual reasoning while preserving general capabilities, which could benefit the broader MLLM community. The empirical analysis shows that MLLMs already capture relevant visual evidence and the correct answer exists in their decoding space, but the decoder favors dominant language priors, especially for low-frequency factual scenarios. TACT routes evidence-following and prior-driven reasoning trajectories into different optimization stages, and it substantially improves performance on counter-commonsense visual benchmarks.

**Background**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) combine a vision encoder with a large language model to handle tasks involving both images and text. The proposed method, TACT, uses a text-anchored corpus to debias the decoder, demonstrating effective cross-modal transfer from text to vision.

## [State Encoding Choice Alters Synchronization in Language-Model Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06968) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study demonstrates that the choice of state encoding—whether low-order circular moments or histograms—determines whether language-model agents synchronize, even when the physical system is identical. The effect was replicated across GPT, Claude, and Gemini, with GPT showing synchronization in 6/6 seeds for moments and 0/6 for histograms. This finding challenges the assumption that state encodings are neutral interfaces, revealing that they form part of a model-dependent effective interaction law. It has significant implications for AI safety and multi-agent systems, as seemingly innocuous design choices can drastically alter collective behavior. The experiment involved a circular-synchronization task where each agent sees a summary of neighbors' relative phases and chooses to advance, stay, or retard. Replaying identical fields shifted each agent's probabilities far beyond within-encoding repeat variation, and in GPT, presentation alone shifted the operator even with moment values fixed.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Language-model agents interact with their environment through state encodings, which are typically treated as interchangeable. This study uses pretrained language models in a multi-agent synchronization experiment to test whether different encodings of the same physical state lead to different collective dynamics. The results suggest that the encoding itself is not neutral but actively shapes the agents' behavior.

The paper introduces PHASE-Tree, a multi-timescale character-state tree that models character evolution in long-horizon role-playing dialogue, and LongEvoRoleBench, a new benchmark for evaluating evolved-state generation. PHASE-Tree achieves state-of-the-art results on the benchmark, improving character-level, semantic, and embedding scores by 19.7%, 12.4%, and 15.1% respectively. This work addresses a critical gap in role-playing dialogue systems, which typically rely on static profiles and fail to model character evolution over time. By enabling localized updates and providing a dedicated benchmark, it paves the way for more dynamic and realistic character modeling in long-horizon dialogue systems. PHASE-Tree consists of an immutable identity root and mutable persona, session, and moment layers, allowing localized within- and cross-episode updates. The benchmark LongEvoRoleBench pairs four long-dialogue corpora for cross-episode evolution with four short-dialogue corpora for within-scene state-tracking checks, under a unified next-utterance protocol.

HarnessSafe is a new benchmark comprising 328 executable cases across seven persistent-carrier families, evaluated on seven mainstream agent harnesses. It introduces a Persistent-Risk Lifecycle and a multi-stage, trace-based evaluation to track how attacker-influenced content persists and triggers safety violations. Each case in HarnessSafe follows a five-element Persistent-Risk Lifecycle that preserves attack semantics across harness-specific adaptations. Experiments show that containment is carrier-specific and strongly depends on the harness-model configuration, with attack success rates failing to reflect distinct lifecycle progression patterns.

## [Fluid-DiT: Graph-Free Diffusion Transformer for Fluid Flow Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07161) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Fluid-DiT introduces a graph-free diffusion transformer that replaces graph message passing with attention-based denoising to model fluid flow distributions. It achieves higher R² correlations and lower Wasserstein distances than graph-based baselines on benchmarks like laminar cylinder wakes and turbulent 3D wing experiments. This work addresses scalability limitations of graph-based diffusion models in scientific computing, potentially enabling more efficient and accurate simulations of complex fluid dynamics. It could benefit fields like engineering design and climate modeling where high-fidelity simulations are computationally prohibitive. The framework uses a latent-space formulation that separates geometric fidelity from distributional learning, reducing high-frequency artifacts and accelerating sampling. It leverages the transformer's global receptive field to capture local and long-range correlations without hierarchical graph coarsening, and generalizes to unseen Reynolds numbers and geometries.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion models generate data by iteratively denoising random noise, and graph neural networks (GNNs) process data on unstructured meshes. Previous work like Diffusion Graph Networks (DGNs) combined these to sample equilibrium states of fluid flows, but suffered from hand-crafted constraints and limited receptive fields. Fluid-DiT replaces graph message passing with attention, which naturally handles global context and scales better.

## [REVEAL: Largest Generative Foundation Model for Endoscopy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07176) ⭐️ 8.0/10

REVEAL, a generative foundation model for endoscopy, is introduced, trained on GastroNet-5M, a new multicenter dataset of 5 million endoscopic frames. It aligns diffusion latents with domain-specific visual features using encoders pretrained on endoscopic data, and also serves as a powerful feature extractor. This is the largest generative foundation model for endoscopy, addressing the domain gap between natural and clinical images and computational costs of training large Diffusion Transformers. It offers an open, versatile backbone for building specialized clinical tools, potentially lowering the threshold for AI applications in gastroenterology. REVEAL uses encoders pretrained directly on the endoscopic distribution to align diffusion latents, preserving fine textures and anatomical structures. It achieves competitive or superior performance on classification benchmarks compared to EndoViT and Endo-FM, and maintains robust structural coherence in latent-space edits like inpainting and outpainting.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion models are generative models that learn to reverse a noise-adding process to create new data. Diffusion Transformers (DiT) use transformer architectures as the backbone, offering scalability but requiring significant computational resources. Representation alignment adjusts latent spaces to align semantically related samples, which can improve efficiency and performance in specialized domains like medical imaging.

## [Causal Data Management Ecosystem Proposed for Agentic AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07214) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper proposes a Causal World System (CWS), a shared, persistent, queryable causal layer for data integration and causal reasoning in AI ecosystems, addressing confounded decisions in agentic AI. This matters because agentic AI systems acting autonomously need causal reasoning to anticipate consequences, not just correlate data, to be trustworthy. The proposal could influence future data management and AI system design, improving reliability in decision-making. The CWS is designed as a shared, persistent, queryable layer that integrates heterogeneous data sources and supports prescriptive and counterfactual analysis. The paper highlights that integration alone is insufficient; an explicit causal layer is necessary to distinguish drivers from correlates.

## [EliSeg: Report-Grounded Abnormality Segmentation without Target Oracles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07299) ⭐️ 8.0/10

EliSeg introduces an actor-verify-revise framework that constructs segmentation targets directly from unfiltered radiology reports, eliminating the need for predefined target identities, prompts, points, or bounding boxes. Experiments on MIMIC-CXR-ILS show it outperforms direct segmentation and extract-then-segment cascades, with effective suppression of ineligible mentions. This work addresses a critical gap in medical image segmentation by removing the reliance on target oracles, which are often unavailable in real-world clinical settings. It could enable more autonomous and accurate abnormality detection from radiology reports, potentially improving clinical AI applications and reducing manual annotation efforts. The framework uses a grammar-constrained Actor to propose target slots and masks, an independent text-only Verifier to reconstruct the eligible finding inventory, and a Revision step that selectively re-executes the Actor when target structures disagree. The method also demonstrates effective transfer to an external dataset (CheXlocalize), and code is publicly available.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Radiology reports describe clinical observations but do not specify executable segmentation targets, often containing present, negated, prior, uncertain, or irrelevant findings. Existing segmentation methods typically bypass this ambiguity by receiving a target identity or spatial prompt before inference, acting as a hidden target oracle. EliSeg aims to handle this ambiguity directly by determining target eligibility, cardinality, and finding-to-mask correspondence from the unfiltered report.

## [Logit Lens Reveals LVLM Hallucination Is About What, Not How Much, Attention](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07302) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper challenges the prevailing assumption that object hallucination in Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs) stems from insufficient visual attention. Instead, it shows that both real and hallucinated objects receive equally strong attention, and proposes using Logit Lens to decode visual features, identifying two distinct hallucination mechanisms: visual uncertainty and contextual prior. This work provides a novel diagnostic method and a training-free mitigation framework that achieves state-of-the-art results on multiple hallucination benchmarks. It could significantly improve the reliability of LVLMs in real-world applications and guide future research on interpretability and hallucination mitigation. The proposed Detect-Mitigate framework includes a Logit-Lens Consistency Check for detection, High-Attention Regions Masking (HARM) for visual uncertainty hallucinations, and Visual Evidence Enhanced Decoding (VEED) for contextual prior hallucinations. The paper is accepted at CVPR 2026 and code will be available.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs) extend large language models to process both text and images, but they often generate objects not present in the input image, a phenomenon known as object hallucination. Prior work attributed this to insufficient visual attention, but this paper shows that the issue is not the amount of attention but the semantic content of attended regions. Logit Lens is an interpretability technique that decodes intermediate representations into vocabulary tokens, allowing inspection of what the model 'sees' in high-attention areas.

## [Assurance Closure: A New Architecture for AI-Native Agile Development](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07317) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper identifies six gaps in achieving assurance closure for AI-native agile software development and proposes a high-level architecture with six corresponding capabilities built on a shared semantic assurance layer. It also formulates four research questions to guide future work. This work addresses a critical gap in AI-native software development by moving beyond code generation to systematic assurance, which is essential for trusting AI agents in large-scale engineering. It provides a roadmap for making assurance reasoning machine-operable, potentially enabling safer and more reliable AI-driven development processes. The six gaps include establishing what must be true, obtaining appropriate evidence, judging evidence credibility, preserving validity through change, and using uncertainty to bound agent authority. The proposed architecture leverages existing mechanisms from formal methods, testing, simulation, assurance cases, digital twins, and runtime assurance, integrated via a shared semantic assurance layer.

Assurance closure is the concept that a system can establish what must be true, gather and judge evidence, preserve validity through change, and use uncertainty to bound agent authority. This paper builds on existing work in formal methods, testing, and runtime assurance to address residual gaps in making assurance reasoning machine-operable.

## [PACE: Primitive-Aware Code Evolution for Automated Algorithm Design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07395) ⭐️ 8.0/10

PACE introduces Executable Algorithmic Primitives (EAPs) to decouple local logic from complete programs, enabling code-level transfer and retention of useful algorithmic components in LLM-based automated algorithm design. The method uses primitive-aware operators and Thompson sampling to guide evolution, and experiments on four tasks show it discovers competitive algorithms while preserving valuable components. This work addresses a fundamental limitation of whole-program evolution, where useful local logic is often lost when a program is discarded. By enabling structural retention and transfer of algorithmic components, PACE could significantly improve the efficiency and effectiveness of LLM-based automated algorithm design, impacting fields such as optimization, machine learning, and software engineering. PACE maintains a dynamic set of EAPs and uses primitive-aware operators that structurally guarantee retention and cross-program transfer. It leverages Thompson sampling based on parent-relative performance improvements to guide primitive selection without requiring extra evaluation datasets. Experiments were conducted on four tasks, demonstrating the method's effectiveness.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Large Language Model (LLM)-based automated algorithm design typically evolves algorithms as complete, indivisible programs, which couples useful local logic to the host program and makes it difficult to assess individual components. Executable Algorithmic Primitives (EAPs) are persistent, executable units that represent local logic, allowing for code-level transfer. Thompson sampling is a Bayesian heuristic for balancing exploration and exploitation in decision-making, often used in multi-armed bandit problems.

## [SABRE: Scalable Automated Pipeline for VLM Stress Testing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07435) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SABRE introduces a scalable, automated pipeline that converts a Test Primer into structured specifications, images, and question-answer pairs for stress-testing vision-language models (VLMs). It includes SABRE-Prior, a stress test with 600 images and 1,000 questions, and pilots for counting and spatial tasks. This addresses a critical bottleneck in VLM evaluation by automating stress-test generation, which is typically costly and slow. It enables efficient identification of model weaknesses, potentially accelerating VLM improvement and providing a reusable framework for the research community. SABRE-Prior tests whether VLMs follow visual evidence over world priors, covering Context, Texture, Attribute, and Language Elicitation categories. Across six VLMs, macro-average accuracy ranged from 17.8% to 31.3% (mean 22.6%), and a real-image Attribute control was comparably difficult for the Filtering VLM.

## [CreativeInstruct: Teaching LLMs to Balance Creativity and Quality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07460) ⭐️ 8.0/10

CreativeInstruct introduces a scalable instruction-tuning method that teaches LLMs to balance creative, base-model-like generations with the quality of post-trained models by learning to inject special [StartCreativity] spans. It also proposes a structural diversity metric based on graph edit distance to capture narrative-level variation. This addresses a significant problem in LLM post-training: the trade-off between quality and diversity/creativity. It could impact creative tasks like story generation and reinforcement learning, where diversity is implicitly required, and offers a scalable solution without needing multiple models at inference. On narrative generation, CreativeInstruct matches or exceeds the diversity of multi-model baselines and distilled variants without sacrificing quality. Human evaluation found annotators rated CreativeInstruct generations as more creative in 70.3% of cases, and GRPO applied to a CreativeInstruct checkpoint improved by ~4% on AMC and ~5% points on MATH over the post-trained checkpoint.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Post-training of large language models (LLMs) typically improves their capabilities but reduces output diversity and creativity, which is harmful for tasks that require creativity, such as story generation, and for reinforcement learning (RL) where exploration benefits from diverse outputs. Instruction tuning is a common post-training method that teaches models to follow instructions, but it often leads to homogenized outputs. The proposed method uses special tokens to control creativity and a graph-edit-distance-based metric to measure structural diversity in narratives.

## [Boundary Density Likelihood Boosts Event Detection Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.12792) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Boundary Density Likelihood (BDL), a new training objective that directly supervises event detection models on annotated event timestamps, using a Poisson objective to estimate expected event mass. In a prespecified five-fold nested sleep study, BDL-Hard improved pooled out-of-fold mAP from 0.586 to 0.705 over interval segmentation, a gain of 11.9 percentage points. This work challenges the common practice of training sequence models for samplewise segmentation and converting predictions to events post hoc, showing that directly optimizing for the evaluated output can yield substantial gains. The findings could influence how event detection models are trained across domains like sleep analysis, seizure detection, and other time-series applications. A matched boundary-BCE detector achieved 0.702 mAP, indicating that direct boundary supervision and event decoding account for most of the gain, with a smaller contribution from the Poisson objective. The same trend appeared with an offline convolutional model, but causal, Transformer, and patient-grouped seizure experiments left broader generalization unresolved.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Event detection involves converting long recordings into a sparse set of ranked timestamps, but many sequence models are trained for samplewise segmentation and only convert predicted states into events after training. BDL assigns one unit of target mass to each annotated event, preserves that mass through smoothing and temporal downsampling, and uses a Poisson objective to estimate expected event mass in each output bin, with local peaks becoming ranked detections.

The proposed algorithm enables AI agents to build models of their interlocutors and predict their next actions and mental states, validated across five social reasoning benchmarks.

## [Counterfactual Simulation Training Boosts Chain-of-Thought Faithfulness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.20710) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces Counterfactual Simulation Training (CST), a novel method that trains LLMs to produce chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning that enables accurate prediction over counterfactual inputs. Applied to models up to 235B parameters, CST improves monitor accuracy by 35 accuracy points on cue-based counterfactuals and simulatability by 2 points on generic counterfactuals. Chain-of-thought faithfulness is critical for LLM interpretability and safety, as unfaithful reasoning can mislead users and auditors. CST offers a scalable training approach that significantly enhances faithfulness, with direct applications in monitoring for spurious features, reward hacking, and sycophancy, thereby improving trust in AI systems. CST outperforms prompting baselines, and rewriting unfaithful CoTs with an LLM is 5x more efficient than RL alone. Notably, faithfulness improvements do not generalize to dissuading cues (as opposed to persuading cues), and larger models do not show more faithful CoT out of the box but benefit more from CST.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Chain-of-thought (CoT) prompting is a technique where LLMs provide step-by-step reasoning before answering, which helps users understand the model's decision process. However, CoT can be unfaithful, meaning the stated reasoning may not reflect the model's actual internal computations. Counterfactual simulation involves predicting how a model would respond if its input were altered, which can be used to test whether the CoT reasoning is consistent with the model's behavior. CST trains models to generate CoTs that enable accurate counterfactual predictions, thereby improving faithfulness.

## [AutoMOOSE: Agentic AI Automates Phase-Field Simulations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.20986) ⭐️ 8.0/10

AutoMOOSE, an open-source multi-agent framework, automates phase-field simulations from natural-language prompts, validated on grain growth and spinodal decomposition benchmarks. It uses six specialized agents to generate, execute, analyze, and adversarially test simulations, achieving high success rates and physical accuracy. This work significantly lowers the barrier to using complex multiphysics simulation tools like MOOSE, enabling researchers without deep expertise to conduct phase-field simulations. It demonstrates the practical application of multi-agent AI to accelerate materials science research, potentially speeding up discovery and design of new materials. On a 25-task grain-growth benchmark, AutoMOOSE generated valid inputs for all tasks, completed 19 simulations with measurable coarsening, and delivered 15 results satisfying Burke-Turnbull kinetics (R^2 ≥ 0.90) and passing Skeptic falsification. An ensemble of 1000 simulations recovered the activation energy within 1% (Q∞ = 0.228 eV vs 0.230 eV), and a Fe-Cr spinodal decomposition simulation limited mass drift to 6.3×10^-6.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Phase-field modeling is a computational method that simulates microstructural evolution by coupling thermodynamics and kinetics. MOOSE (Multiphysics Object-Oriented Simulation Environment) is an open-source finite element framework for multiphysics simulations, but it requires significant expertise to set up and run. Allen-Cahn and Cahn-Hilliard equations are fundamental models for non-conserved and conserved phase transformations, respectively.

## [INTRYGUE: Mechanistic Gating for RAG Uncertainty Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.21607) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces INTRYGUE, a method that gates predictive entropy using induction head activations to improve uncertainty estimation in retrieval-augmented generation. It reveals a mechanistic paradox where induction heads inflate entropy, causing false uncertainty, and proposes a solution that matches or outperforms baselines across four benchmarks and six LLMs. This work addresses a critical limitation in RAG systems: hallucination detection. By combining predictive uncertainty with interpretable internal signals, INTRYGUE offers a more reliable way to assess output confidence, which is essential for deploying LLMs in high-stakes applications. INTRYGUE was evaluated on four RAG benchmarks and six open-source LLMs ranging from 4B to 13B parameters. The method consistently matches or outperforms a wide range of UQ baselines, demonstrating that hallucination detection benefits from combining predictive uncertainty with internal context-utilization signals.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) enhances factual reliability by incorporating external knowledge, but it does not eliminate hallucinations. Uncertainty quantification (UQ) methods, such as entropy-based approaches, are used to detect when models may be wrong, but they can fail in RAG due to mechanistic interactions. Induction heads are attention mechanisms that copy patterns from context, and their activation can interfere with entropy signals, leading to false uncertainty.

## [AI Alignment's Fantasia Problem: Short-Circuiting User Agency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.21827) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2604.21827) introduces the concept of 'Fantasia interactions,' where instruction-tuned AI systems jump to final outputs, bypassing users' cognitive refinement processes. The authors argue this demands a rethinking of alignment research to optimize cognitive responsibility allocation. This challenges the current alignment paradigm, highlighting a significant gap in AI's understanding of human cognition and agency. It could influence future alignment research and the design of AI assistants, affecting how users interact with and trust AI systems. The paper identifies that even when AI systems infer implicit intent, they lack understanding of human cognitive processes, leading to suboptimal outcomes or increased user revision time. It outlines a research agenda for training and evaluating models to support intent refinement rather than just final outputs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: AI alignment is a research field focused on ensuring AI systems behave in accordance with human intentions and values. Instruction-tuned models are trained to follow user prompts, but this paper argues they often fail to support the user's cognitive journey from abstract goals to concrete outcomes, a phenomenon likened to the 'Fantasia' scene where brooms multiply uncontrollably.

## [DATAREEL: New Benchmark for Automated Data Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.25220) ⭐️ 8.0/10

DATAREEL is a new benchmark for automated data-driven video story generation, containing 328 real-world data reels. It evaluates vision-language models on generating executable animation code with synchronized subtitles from data tables and communicative intents. This benchmark addresses the lack of standardized evaluation for automated data video generation, a challenging multimodal task. It reveals a significant capability gap between open-weight and proprietary models, highlighting areas for improvement in VLM-based data storytelling. Evaluating eight proprietary and open-weight VLMs, the benchmark found open-weight models have execution failure rates up to 39.8%, while proprietary models perform better overall. Even the best models often generate static charts, desynchronized subtitles, unstable layouts, and poor style adherence.

## [In-Context Examples Suppress Scientific Knowledge Recall in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.27540) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper demonstrates that adding in-context examples to LLMs can suppress their reliance on pretrained scientific knowledge, leading to a shift toward empirical pattern fitting, even when examples are generated by the same formula. This finding challenges the assumption that in-context examples reinforce knowledge-driven reasoning, and has significant implications for prompt engineering and model evaluation in scientific tasks. It could influence future research on in-context learning and reasoning in LLMs. The study covers 60 latent structure recovery tasks across five scientific domains, 6,000 trials, and four models. The displacement is consistent across domains, but its accuracy consequences vary: it can lower accuracy, leave it unchanged, or appear to improve it, depending on the replaced strategy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: In-context learning (ICL) is a technique where LLMs are given examples or instructions within the prompt to guide responses without retraining. Latent structure recovery refers to uncovering hidden relationships from data, which is central to scientific reasoning. This paper investigates how ICL affects the use of pretrained scientific knowledge.

## [LOCA: Minimal Local Causal Explanations for LLM Jailbreak Success](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.00123) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces LOCA, a method that provides minimal, local, causal explanations for why specific jailbreak prompts succeed on LLMs. It identifies a minimal set of interpretable intermediate representation changes that can induce refusal on an otherwise successful jailbreak request, requiring on average six changes compared to prior methods that often fail even after 20. This work addresses a critical gap in AI safety by moving from global to local explanations of jailbreak success, which is essential for understanding and mitigating vulnerabilities in future autonomous models. It provides a more precise tool for mechanistic interpretability, potentially leading to more robust safety training and evaluation. LOCA is evaluated on harmful original-jailbreak pairs from a large jailbreak benchmark across Gemma, Llama, and Qwen chat models. The method successfully induces refusal with an average of six interpretable changes, while prior methods routinely fail to achieve refusal even after 20 changes. Code is publicly available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Safety-trained LLMs can be induced to answer harmful requests through jailbreak prompts, but the underlying mechanisms are poorly understood. Prior work used intermediate representations to identify directions that causally encode concepts like harmfulness and refusal, providing global explanations for all jailbreaks. However, different strategies and request categories may involve different concepts, necessitating local explanations. LOCA addresses this by identifying minimal causal changes in intermediate representations that can flip a successful jailbreak into a refusal.

## [ARIADNE: Training-Free Semantic Adapter Routing via Task Embeddings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19079) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ARIADNE introduces a training-free semantic adapter routing method that selects the most appropriate PEFT adapter for a query using task embeddings, achieving 97.4% of Oracle performance on 23 tasks and scaling to 44 adapters with 89.7% selection accuracy, without accessing adapter weights. A companion method, GRACE, recovers fine-tuning data from adapter output logits via contrastive decoding diffing, enabling routing when training data is unavailable. This work addresses a critical challenge in PEFT ecosystems where many task-specific adapters share a single backbone, making efficient adapter selection essential for deployment. By eliminating the need for training data or adapter weights, ARIADNE and GRACE enable practical routing for adapters from public hubs or third-party providers, potentially simplifying model serving and improving inference efficiency. ARIADNE reframes adapter selection as a classification problem, representing adapters by task embeddings and routing an unlabeled query to the nearest adapter in the encoder's latent space. GRACE uses a modified contrastive decoding diffing (CDD) procedure to synthesize training data from adapter output logits, and across three backbones (Llama-3.2-1B, Qwen2.5-3B, Qwen2.5-32B), it recovers 72–100% of Oracle task accuracy and matches or exceeds ARROW on 48 of 69 task/backbone combinations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) allows adapting large language models to specific tasks by training a small number of additional parameters, such as adapters, while keeping the backbone frozen. This leads to ecosystems where a single backbone is paired with many task-specific adapters, and routing is the process of selecting the most appropriate adapter for a given user query. Existing routing methods often require access to adapter weights or supervised training data, which may not be available when adapters are shared publicly. Task embeddings are vector representations that capture the semantic essence of a task, enabling similarity-based matching between queries and adapters.

## [SenWorld: Digital-Twin Simulation for Privacy-Safe Evaluation Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.19949) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SenWorld introduces a physically grounded, deterministic, event-sourced digital-twin simulation that generates context-rich evaluation data for smartphone personal assistants, with ground truth fixed by construction. The method was evaluated with 16 personas in Beijing, producing data that closely matches real-user benchmarks in category distribution and daily rhythm. This addresses the critical challenge of obtaining privacy-preserving, reproducible evaluation data for smartphone assistants, which is essential for advancing AI/ML evaluation in systems research. By providing a deterministic, ground-truth-by-construction approach, SenWorld enables reliable benchmarking without exposing sensitive real-device traces. The generated data closely matches the held-out real-user benchmark in category distribution (Jensen-Shannon divergence 0.070) and daily rhythm of communication records (JSD below 0.1), though generated records remain shorter than real ones. Projected into 717 evaluation cases, the data exposed 78 failures in a production smartphone assistant, all confirmed as assistant-side retrieval errors via snapshot pointers, with no LLM judge involved.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Smartphone personal assistants reason over longitudinal personal data, but evaluating them requires context-rich data with known correct answers, and real device traces are too privacy-sensitive to share. Digital-twin simulations create virtual replicas of real-world environments, and event-sourced architectures store state changes as a sequence of events, enabling deterministic replay and auditing. Ground truth in evaluation data refers to the correct answers or labels used to assess model performance, which are typically obtained via manual annotation or LLM judges, but SenWorld fixes them by construction.

## [Property-Driven Causal Abstractions for MDPs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26787) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a novel property-driven causal abstraction technique for factored Markov Decision Processes (MDPs), which leverages causal relations over state variable predicates to reduce state space while preserving properties. The method is theoretically and empirically compared across MDPs, interval MDPs, and stochastic games, showing that it can produce small abstractions enabling near-optimal policies for original MDPs. This work addresses the scalability challenge in MDP reasoning by providing a principled abstraction method that preserves key properties, potentially enabling more efficient planning and verification in large-scale decision-making systems. It bridges causality and formal methods, offering a new tool for AI planning and reinforcement learning communities. The approach identifies states that share the same reasons for fulfilling or violating a given abstraction property, using causal relations over state variable predicates. The evaluation on standard benchmarks demonstrates that the causal abstractions often generalize to related large-scale MDP models, and the paper includes comparisons across different model types such as interval MDPs and stochastic games.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Markov Decision Processes (MDPs) are mathematical frameworks for modeling decision-making in stochastic environments, commonly specified over factored state spaces with state variables. The exponential growth in state count makes many reasoning tasks computationally challenging, and abstraction techniques aim to reduce the state space while preserving relevant properties. Causal abstraction, a recent concept, uses causal relationships among variables to guide state aggregation, offering a more principled way to reduce complexity.

## [Shadow Evaluations Reveal AI Agents Fail Open-Ended Research](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.27191) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study introduces 'shadow evaluations,' a method where AI agents tackle the central research question of unpublished papers and are graded by the original authors. Testing on two NeurIPS 2026 submissions, frontier agents completed all engineering tasks but failed to make substantial research progress, leading to unambiguous rejection. This provides early empirical evidence on whether AI agents can automate open-ended AI research, a key assumption behind forecasts of explosive AI progress. The findings suggest that while agents can handle engineering, they struggle with critical research aspects, which could temper expectations for AI-driven R&D automation. The study identified five recurring failure modes: poor judgment about publishable research standards, uncreative responses to design shortcomings, ineffective backtracking from dead ends, poor resource awareness, and instruction drift. A robustness check with a second model and scaffold reproduced these failures, and the authors released expert reviews, survey responses, agent repositories, and logs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Current evaluations of AI agents for research either test narrow, verifiable tasks, which exclude open-ended research, or submit AI-generated papers to blind peer review, which is overstretched and stochastic. Shadow evaluations offer a third way by having agents work on the central research question of a high-quality unpublished paper, with the original authors grading the output. This method aims to measure progress towards AI R&D automation more reliably.

## [H+ Embedding: Multi-Granularity Retriever with Context-Dependent Phrases](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00065) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces H+ Embedding, a unified multi-granularity retriever that predicts variable-length phrase partitions and uses weighted MaxSim interaction. It outperforms global retrieval by 6.91 macro nDCG@10 across 16 tasks and nearly matches token-level retrieval while using 13.7% fewer document vectors. This work addresses a critical gap in information retrieval by introducing context-dependent phrases as a retrieval unit, offering a practical intermediate between global and token-level representations. It has significant implications for terminology-intensive domains like medical retrieval, where preserving multi-word entities is crucial. The method predicts variable-length phrase partitions, preserves uncovered tokens as singletons, and applies importance-guided unit selection with weighted MaxSim interaction. It outperforms content-independent grouping rules under moderate vector budgets, demonstrating its efficiency.

**Background**: Traditional dense retrieval uses single-vector representations that may over-compress local signals, while late interaction methods like ColBERT retain token-level vectors at high storage and scoring costs. Context-dependent phrases offer a middle ground by grouping related tokens into meaningful units, which is especially beneficial in fields like medicine where multi-word terms are common.

## [LoCA: Forward-Only LLM Tuning via One-Shot Calibration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03020) ⭐️ 8.0/10

LoCA introduces a two-stage fine-tuning method that uses a single probe backward pass to fit low-rank credit assignment maps, enabling forward-only adaptation without repeated backpropagation. It achieves lower GPU peak memory and CPU memory usage compared to LoRA across Qwen2.5 models (0.5B to 14B). This method significantly reduces hardware requirements and memory usage during fine-tuning, making LLM adaptation more accessible and practical, especially in resource-constrained environments. It also opens up new possibilities for forward-only training paradigms that could benefit edge deployment and on-device learning. LoCA fits low-rank maps at each transformer block from the final prediction error to local hidden-state corrections, then uses these maps to form blockwise regression targets and fits low-rank adapters with closed-form ridge solves. In 16 of 25 task-scale comparisons, LoCA achieves lower evaluation cross-entropy than LoRA, with 26-29% lower GPU peak and 36-52% lower CPU steady-state memory after calibration.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Parameter-efficient fine-tuning methods like LoRA reduce trainable parameters but still require repeated end-to-end backpropagation through the frozen backbone, which demands backward-capable hardware and activation storage. LoCA aims to replace this repeated backward chain with a one-time calibration, amortizing global credit assignment into a single probe pass. This aligns with the growing interest in backpropagation-free training to reduce computational and memory costs.

## [CIPO: Evidence-Oriented RL Framework Reduces Confirmation Bias in Search Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06128) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose Contextual Information Policy Optimization (CIPO), a reinforcement learning framework that explicitly aligns post-retrieval actions with retrieved evidence. It assigns dense, turn-level credit to evidence-grounded reasoning and combines it with a global outcome reward, requiring no human annotations or additional reward models. This addresses a critical limitation in search agents: confirmation bias and inefficient evidence use. By promoting evidence-grounded reasoning, CIPO could improve the reliability of LLM-based search agents in knowledge-intensive tasks, with potential impact on AI/ML and systems research. CIPO does not require human process annotations or an additional reward model. Experiments on seven in-domain and out-of-domain benchmarks show that CIPO reduces prior-driven reasoning and achieves excellent performance on most tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Search agents extend large language models (LLMs) by enabling them to acquire and use external evidence during multi-step reasoning. Existing methods often reward final-answer correctness or intermediate progress, but fail to assess whether post-retrieval actions are grounded in retrieved evidence, leading to confirmation bias. CIPO addresses this by providing dense, turn-level rewards for evidence use, encouraging agents to let retrieved facts guide or revise their reasoning.

## [READ: Agentic Retrieval Replaces Black-Box Top-K for Financial Documents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06305) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces READ (Reliable Embedding-free Agentic Document-search), an agentic retrieval method that replaces black-box top-k retrieval with three deterministic operations—normalized lexical search, structural navigation, and bounded span reads—exposed over the Model Context Protocol. On 51 verified questions from a 780-page financial report, READ achieves 58.8% accuracy versus 15.7% for dense retrieval, a statistically significant improvement. This work challenges the dominant chunk-embed-top-k paradigm in retrieval-augmented generation (RAG), showing that for table-heavy documents like financial statements, this design is structurally flawed. By proposing an interpretable, agentic alternative with measurable gains, it could influence how RAG systems are built for structured documents, improving accuracy and auditability in high-stakes domains. A table-aware chunker, built as a steelman, fixes the unit problem but still leaves 27-30% of numeric chunks without a fiscal-year header at every chunk size. An agent given the same loop but a top-k tool reaches only 27.5%, indicating the gain comes from the interface, not iteration; BM25 is statistically indistinguishable from READ, separating embedding-based from embedding-free retrieval.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) typically involves chunking documents, embedding them, and retrieving top-k chunks by similarity. However, for financial documents with dense tables, chunking can separate numbers from their units or fiscal-year headers, causing errors. Agentic retrieval is an emerging approach where an AI agent performs multi-step retrieval operations, often using tools, to answer complex queries. The Model Context Protocol (MCP) is a standard for exposing tools to AI agents, enabling replayable and auditable trajectories.

## [Optimal Transport and Causal Inference: A Unified Review](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.07811) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This review paper (arXiv:2503.07811) systematically uncovers the deep, often implicit connections between optimal transport theory and causal inference with observational data, aiming to unify language and notation across statistics, mathematics, and econometrics. It highlights how foundational causal models have relied on optimal transport principles for decades and proposes novel research directions at this intersection. This synthesis bridges two major fields, potentially catalyzing new methodological advances in causal inference by leveraging the powerful framework of optimal transport. It could impact researchers in AI/ML, statistics, and econometrics who work with observational data, offering a unified perspective and opening up new avenues for identifying causal effects. The paper is a review rather than a breakthrough, but it provides a novel synthesis by showing that optimal transport is not just a tool but a foundation for model assumptions in causal inference. It emphasizes implicit uses in existing models and suggests future work in both areas derived from this realization.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Optimal transport theory, originating from Monge and Kantorovich formulations, concerns the cost-efficient reallocation of probability measures and has applications across science. Causal inference with observational data aims to identify cause-and-effect relationships from non-experimental data, often relying on assumptions like unconfoundedness. The paper argues that many causal models implicitly use optimal transport principles, and making these connections explicit can unify and advance both fields.

## [Surrogate Model Usefulness in Configuration Tuning Beyond Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21945) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces the first systematic exploration and theory for evaluating surrogate models in configuration tuning beyond accuracy, using fitness landscape analysis. It also proposes Model4Tune, an automated predictive tool that estimates the best model-tuner pairs for unforeseen systems, outperforming random guessing in 79%-82% of cases. This work addresses a critical gap in configuration tuning by challenging the assumption that more accurate surrogate models are always better. It provides a new perspective and practical tool that could significantly reduce the engineering effort required for software performance tuning, benefiting both researchers and practitioners. The theory is based on fitness landscape analysis, and the empirical study involves up to 27,000 cases. Model4Tune is one of the first tools of its kind, and its performance is validated against random guessing, showing significant improvement in 79%-82% of cases.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Configuration tuning is the process of adjusting software parameters to optimize performance metrics like latency. Surrogate models are used to approximate expensive system measurements, and traditionally, model accuracy is considered the primary criterion for selection. However, prior work has shown that accuracy can be misleading, prompting the need for alternative evaluation methods. Fitness landscape analysis is a technique that studies the structure of the search space, which can provide insights into model usefulness beyond simple accuracy.

<a id="item-112"></a>
## [PTDI: Provable Training Data Identification with False Discovery Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.09717) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces PTDI, a distribution-free method for provable training data identification that controls the false identification rate using conformal p-values and a Jackknife-corrected Beta boundary estimator, followed by the Benjamini-Hochberg procedure. This work addresses a critical gap in training data identification by providing statistical guarantees, which is essential for copyright litigation, privacy auditing, and fair evaluation of large language models. It offers a more reliable alternative to existing instance-wise methods that lack error control. PTDI computes conformal p-values for each data point using known unseen data, then uses a novel Jackknife-corrected Beta boundary (JKBB) estimator to estimate the training-data proportion of the test set, scaling the p-values accordingly. The method applies the Benjamini-Hochberg procedure to the scaled p-values to select a subset with provable false identification rate control.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Training data identification aims to determine whether a specific data point was used in training a model, which is important for legal and privacy reasons. Traditional methods often treat this as an instance-wise classification problem without controlling the error rate of the identified set, leading to unreliable results. Conformal prediction provides a framework for distribution-free uncertainty quantification, and the Benjamini-Hochberg procedure controls the false discovery rate in multiple testing.

## [Theoretical Analysis of Layer Normalization Placement in Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.09904) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper provides a principled theoretical study of how layer normalization placement affects forward and backward stability in Transformers, deriving explicit bounds on hidden state growth and analyzing gradient backpropagation dynamics. It also offers guidance on scaling residual steps to improve training stability and performance. Training deep Transformers is often unstable, and layer normalization placement has been largely ad-hoc. This work provides a theoretical foundation that can guide architecture design, potentially improving training stability and performance across various Transformer-based models. The paper distinguishes between forward stability (growth of hidden states) and backward stability (gradient propagation), and shows how different layer normalization placements lead to regular or pathological training dynamics. Numerical experiments corroborate the theoretical findings, and the framework can be used to sanity-check new architectural modifications.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Layer normalization is a standard component in Transformers that normalizes activations to have zero mean and unit variance, improving training stability. Its placement—before (pre-norm) or after (post-norm) the residual connection—affects optimization behavior. Forward and backward stability are concepts from numerical analysis that describe how errors propagate through computations, and here they are applied to neural network training dynamics.

## [Intelligence per Watt: A New Metric for Local AI Efficiency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.07885) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces 'intelligence per watt' (IPW), a unified metric measuring task accuracy per unit of power for local LLM inference, and evaluates 20+ models across 8 hardware accelerators using 1M real-world queries. As cloud AI demand strains centralized infrastructure, IPW provides a critical benchmark for assessing whether local inference can viably redistribute demand, potentially reducing costs and energy consumption while improving privacy and latency. The study finds local LMs answer 88.7% of queries correctly, IPW improved 5.3x from 2023-2025, and local accelerators achieve at least 1.4x lower IPW than cloud accelerators running identical models, indicating significant optimization headroom.

A new arXiv paper (2603.15506) argues that current time-series forecasting benchmarks, dominated by strong seasonality, unfairly favor deep learning models and mask the competitive performance of classical statistical methods. The authors call for retiring or augmenting these benchmarks and mandating classical baselines in all deep learning submissions. This critique challenges the perceived superiority of deep learning in time-series forecasting, potentially reshaping how the community evaluates models and allocates research effort. If adopted, it could lead to more rigorous benchmarking and prevent wasted computational resources on models that offer no real advantage over simpler methods. The paper highlights that standard datasets often exhibit dominant autocorrelation and seasonal cycles, which simple linear or statistical models can capture effectively. ## [Unified Multi-Tier KV Cache Management Boosts GPU Inference Efficiency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.26968) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper proposes a unified multi-tier KV cache management system that addresses three inefficiencies in large-scale GPU inference: lack of unified sizing across attention architectures (especially MLA), confinement to a single memory tier, and reactive eviction policies. The system achieves a 7.4x batch size gain for DeepSeek-V3 (MLA) and extends effective KV cache capacity from 40 GB to over 38 TB per node. KV cache management is a critical bottleneck in LLM serving, and this unified approach could significantly reduce memory over-provisioning and improve throughput and cost-efficiency. It has the potential to benefit large-scale inference deployments, especially those using MLA-based models like DeepSeek-V3. The system includes an architecture-variant-aware sizing engine, a six-tier memory hierarchy (GPU HBM, CPU DRAM, CXL, NVMe via GPUDirect Storage, RDMA fabric, parallel filesystems), and a Bayesian reuse predictor with Beta conjugate priors for eviction and prefetching. Component-level validation on trace replay shows 70-84% cache hit rates, but cluster-scale projections are analytical without error bars.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: KV cache stores key-value pairs from attention layers to avoid recomputation during LLM inference, but it consumes significant GPU memory. Multi-head latent attention (MLA) compresses KV pairs to reduce memory, but general-purpose frameworks often lack support, leading to over-provisioning. Multi-tier memory hierarchies, including CPU DRAM and NVMe, can expand cache capacity but require efficient management.

A new study evaluates four dependency parsing architectures across twelve languages and finds that the Biaffine LSTM consistently outperforms transformer models in low-resource settings, with transformers only regaining advantage as training data increases. This challenges the common assumption that transformers are always superior for NLP tasks, providing practical guidance for developing syntactic tools in low-resource languages where annotated data is scarce. The study compares Biaffine LSTM, Stack-Pointer Network, AfroXLMR-large, and RemBERT across twelve typologically diverse languages, focusing on low-resource African languages. Morphological complexity, measured via MATTR, is identified as a significant secondary predictor of transformers' relative disadvantage after controlling for corpus size.

The Biaffine LSTM, a simpler architecture, uses bidirectional LSTMs and a biaffine attention mechanism to predict dependency arcs and labels. MATTR (Moving Average Type-Token Ratio) is a measure of lexical diversity that correlates with morphological complexity.

## [Input-Side Watermark Detects Verbatim LLM Copy-Paste in Education](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.16336) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces SteganoPrompt, an educator-controlled input-side watermarking tool that embeds hidden instructions in assignment prompts, causing LLMs to produce detectable signatures in their responses. This enables reliable detection of verbatim copy-paste without requiring cooperation from model providers. This approach addresses the practical problem of detecting verbatim LLM use in academic settings, offering a more reliable alternative to post-hoc AI detectors that often penalize non-native English writers. It empowers educators with a tool they control directly, potentially improving academic integrity enforcement. SteganoPrompt encodes arbitrary printable-ASCII payloads into the deprecated Unicode Tags block (U+E0000–U+E007F), which is visually identical to the original text and survives common copy-paste channels. The tool includes a built-in academic-integrity reminder for students and is released under the MIT license.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) have made it easy for students to generate essays and answers, leading to concerns about academic integrity. Existing detection methods include post-hoc AI-text detectors, which are unreliable and biased, and output-side watermarks, which require model provider cooperation. Input-side watermarking embeds signals in the prompt itself, allowing educators to detect verbatim copy-paste without external dependencies.

Multi-Legal-Bench is introduced as the first cross-jurisdictional legal benchmark, evaluating identical tasks across six countries (Ukraine, France, Netherlands, Poland, Czech Republic, Lithuania) and four language families. It assesses 7 frontier LLMs under zero-shot and 3-shot prompting, plus 4 smaller models, revealing uneven few-shot gains and cross-lingual transfer patterns. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in Legal NLP by enabling cross-lingual and cross-jurisdictional comparison of LLM performance on legal tasks. The benchmark defines five tasks (court-type classification, judgment form classification, case-outcome prediction, legal norm extraction, cause category prediction) mapped to structured metadata, forming a sparse 5x6 matrix with 20 of 30 cells filled. Results show that few-shot gains track headroom rather than language, and cross-lingual transfer does not follow language proximity (e.g., UA->FR transfers better than UA->PL). Tokenizer fertility does not significantly predict accuracy (r=-0.14, p=0.24).

The paper proposes evaluating certified training methods via Pareto front comparisons over the natural-certified accuracy trade-off, using automated multi-objective hyperparameter optimization to identify Pareto-optimal configurations. This approach uncovers undertuning in previous configurations and establishes a new state of the art. This matters because the common practice of reporting a single configuration can mislead conclusions about overall performance and prevent unbiased assessments of the state of the art. By providing a fairer, method-agnostic evaluation framework, it could reshape how certified training methods are compared and drive more robust progress in adversarial robustness research. The paper presents the first comprehensive multi-objective comparison of certified training approaches, showing that prior advancements are less pronounced than assumed and revealing previously unreported performance complementarities. The method uses efficient automated multi-objective hyperparameter optimization to identify Pareto-optimal configurations for each method.

## [Compositional Framework for Behavioral Semantics in RL State Abstraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25357) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a unified compositional framework for specifying behavioral semantics in reinforcement learning, based on local one-step descriptions of system dynamics. It establishes results showing how behavioral structures can be safely transferred between abstract and concrete systems, and constructs quantitative metrics from logical behavioral semantics with soundness guarantees. This work addresses a fundamental gap in reinforcement learning theory by providing a general principle for determining which behavioral structures are provably preserved under state abstraction. It offers reusable definition and proof principles that could influence future research and applications in complex structured systems. The framework is compositional, allowing behavioral semantics to be built from local one-step descriptions. It also demonstrates how to construct quantitative metrics from logical behavioral semantics with soundness guarantees, and the paper is a preprint (arXiv:2606.25357v2) that has not yet undergone peer review.

## [MultiView-Bench: Benchmarking VLMs' 3D World-Centric Integration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08970) ⭐️ 8.0/10

MultiView-Bench is a new diagnostic benchmark that evaluates vision-language models' ability to integrate multi-view observations into a coherent 3D world-centric representation. Systematic evaluation of frontier VLMs reveals consistent failure modes, and the proposed ViewNavigator method improves base models by 12.3–20.0 percentage points under a six-image cap. This benchmark addresses a critical but under-tested capability in VLMs—multi-view integration into 3D world-centric models—which is essential for downstream tasks like mechanical part assembly. By exposing consistent failure modes and biases, it guides future research and model development toward more robust spatial understanding. The benchmark requires models to decouple object positioning from transient perspectives and ground them in a fixed global coordinate system, unlike existing datasets that focus on pixel-level mapping or camera-relative navigation. It identifies biases such as struggles with unconventional axis directions and sensitivity to object colorways and texture variations; budget-extended gains for ViewNavigator reach 27 percentage points for GPT-5.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) typically excel at single-image perception but often lack the ability to integrate information across multiple viewpoints into a coherent 3D world-centric (allocentric) representation. This capability is distinct from egocentric reasoning, which is relative to the observer's viewpoint, and is crucial for tasks requiring holistic scene understanding, such as robotics and assembly. Existing benchmarks largely test single- or limited-view perception, leaving this cognitive ability untested.
