## [Physics-Inspired Digital Twin of Cortical Dynamics via Port-Hamiltonian GNN](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10439) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a novel digital twin of cortical dynamics using a band-stratified metriplectic port-Hamiltonian graph neural network, trained on EEG data from the PhysioNet Motor Movement/Imagery database. The model ensures passivity and energy balance by construction, and achieves a held-out reconstruction error of 'FitTestMSE'. This work bridges physics-based modeling and deep learning for brain-computer interfaces, offering theoretical guarantees like passivity and energy balance that are crucial for reliable closed-loop neuromodulation. It provides a structure-preserving substrate for designing and testing interventions, potentially advancing computational neuroscience and BCI applications. The model pairs each channel's instantaneous phase with its angular frequency, and stored energy decomposes over five canonical frequency bands. A phase-locking prior gates the learned connectome, and the metriplectic formulation places the twin at a non-equilibrium steady state sustained by a metabolic port. Free-running tests show it reproduces near-critical avalanche branching (σ≈1) but not the aperiodic 1/f slope or long-range temporal correlations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Port-Hamiltonian systems are a framework for modeling dynamical systems with energy storage, dissipation, and interconnection, ensuring passivity and energy balance. Graph neural networks (GNNs) operate on graph-structured data, making them suitable for modeling brain networks from EEG. The PhysioNet EEG Motor Movement/Imagery database contains over 1500 EEG recordings from 109 volunteers, commonly used for BCI research. This work combines these concepts to create a physics-informed digital twin of cortical dynamics.

## [Counterfactual Shapley Credit Assignment Improves RL Credit Attribution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16999) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces Counterfactual Shapley Credit Assignment, a causal-theory-based framework that uses Counterfactual Shapley Values (φ-values) to redistribute environmental rewards, enhancing credit assignment in reinforcement learning under sparse causality, high stochasticity, and delayed rewards. It also proposes a consistent estimator and a new policy gradient method, φ-PPO, combined with Prioritized Trajectory Replay (PTR). This work addresses fundamental limitations in credit assignment, a core challenge in reinforcement learning, by isolating true causal drivers from spurious correlations and environmental stochasticity. It offers theoretical guarantees and demonstrates superior sample efficiency in challenging environments, potentially advancing RL applications in complex, real-world scenarios. The framework preserves the optimal policy while redistributing rewards, and the consistent estimator computes φ-values efficiently. Empirical results show that φ-values align precisely with ground truth causes of task rewards, outperforming prior state-of-the-art methods that fail to converge in certain environments.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: The credit assignment problem (CAP) in reinforcement learning involves determining which actions are responsible for received rewards, especially when rewards are delayed or influenced by environmental stochasticity. Traditional methods rely on temporal contiguity or hindsight-conditioned reward reweighting, but often fail to distinguish between an agent's skill and luck. Shapley values, from cooperative game theory, provide a principled way to attribute credit fairly among contributors, and counterfactual reasoning helps estimate what would have happened under alternative actions.

A new arXiv preprint (2607.20255) analyzes how LLM-driven autonomous agents introduce indeterminacy in actions, impact, and user population, complicating ethics and safety in offensive security. The paper provides stratified recommendations for stakeholders. This work addresses a timely and critical gap in AI ethics and cybersecurity, as LLM-driven agents are rapidly being adopted for offensive security. It highlights how existing dual-use frameworks fail to address the unique challenges posed by these agents, potentially influencing future policy and safety practices. The paper identifies three independent dimensions of indeterminacy: non-deterministic policies that resist explanation, open-ended impact due to model agency and opaque supply chains, and an indeterminate user population with a lowered skill floor. It argues that these properties, combined with cost asymmetry, enable the industrialization of offensive capability, favoring attackers in the short term.

## [IFCLoRA: Topology-Aware Rank Allocation for Efficient LLM Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22251) ⭐️ 8.0/10

IFCLoRA introduces a topology-aware method for pre-fine-tuning rank allocation and adapter initialization in LoRA, using intervention tracing to construct a task-conditioned information-flow graph and compute Information-Flow Centrality (IFC) scores. On GSM8K, it achieves the highest mean accuracy among compared PEFT methods, exceeding standard LoRA by 4.75 percentage points on LLaMA-3.1-8B. This work addresses a key limitation in existing adaptive LoRA methods by using global information-flow topology for rank allocation, which could improve parameter-efficient fine-tuning efficiency and effectiveness. It provides a structural prior that is task-conditioned and non-uniform, potentially benefiting low-budget fine-tuning scenarios across various models and tasks. IFCLoRA requires a one-time offline calibration stage using a small calibration set, and it reuses response vectors from tracing to construct a function-preserving flow-response subspace initialization. The resulting rank allocations are non-uniform and vary across tasks and base models, and the method achieves comparable fine-tuning time and peak memory to standard LoRA.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) technique that freezes the base model and injects trainable low-rank matrices, with effectiveness depending on rank allocation. Existing adaptive methods derive ranks from local statistics like gradients or activations, which may not capture each module's global role in information propagation. Intervention tracing is a technique used to analyze information flow in neural networks, often represented as graphs where nodes correspond to representations and edges to computations.

Researchers introduced a unified software tool that performs stochastic numerical validation of deep learning operators in a single pass, detecting and monitoring sources of numerical instability during training and inference. Numerical instability can silently degrade model accuracy and reliability, yet it is often overlooked. This tool provides automated detection and monitoring, which could help developers build more stable and efficient deep learning systems. The tool applies the CESTAC method to supported exposed operations and uses an operator-level data-perturbation approximation for GEMM-like kernels. It verified effectiveness by detecting injected numerical instabilities across various tasks.

## [Topology-Aware Data Movement for Disaggregated GPU Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28633) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper proposes a topology-aware transfer orchestrator for disaggregated LLM inference that discovers interconnect hierarchy at startup and selects optimal transport per transfer, hiding 76-100% of KV cache transfer latency behind computation. This addresses a critical unsolved problem in disaggregated LLM inference, where KV cache transfer between prefill and decode GPU pools can become a bottleneck. By exploiting interconnect hierarchy, it can significantly improve efficiency and reduce latency, impacting the design of future AI infrastructure. The paper reports that bandwidth between two GPUs varies by 72x depending on physical relationship (900 GB/s via NVLink 4.0, 50 GB/s via InfiniBand, 12.5 GB/s via TCP). It introduces three mechanisms: pipelined layer-by-layer transfer, NVLink domain-aware placement for MoE models, and CXL 3.0 memory expanders as a shared overflow tier.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Disaggregated LLM inference separates prefill and decode phases onto different GPU pools to improve utilization, but requires transferring the KV cache between them. For a 70B model, this transfer is 1.3 GB per request, exceeding 100 GB/s aggregate at production scale. Existing systems like DistServe, Splitwise, and Mooncake use uniform RDMA, ignoring the large bandwidth differences in the interconnect hierarchy.

## [Theory Shows When Activation Patching and Weight Ablation Agree](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03620) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper presents a theoretical framework proving exact conditions under which activation patching and weight-space ablation yield equivalent causal conclusions in neural networks. It derives three exact results for an idealized residual-stream model and validates them on synthetic transformers. This work provides a rigorous foundation for interpretability methods, clarifying when two widely used causal-claim techniques agree or diverge. It could impact AI safety research by guiding researchers on which method to trust for validating causal attributions in neural networks. The paper proves that deleting carriers collapses a matched input pair iff removal is symmetric and leaves no outside contrast, with exact error forms. It also shows patching moves readout by contrast while ablation moves by absolute level, and derives a first-order interaction formula for attention heads with a second-order remainder.

**Background**: Activation patching swaps internal activations to test causal influence, while weight-space ablation removes parameters to observe effects. ## [Tail-Call Optimization in C: A Recent 2025 Addition](https://lwn.net/Articles/1034703/) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Tail-call optimization (TCO) has been recently added to C compilers, marking a notable change in 2025. This development allows C compilers to optimize tail-recursive calls, reusing stack frames and preventing stack overflow. This addition is significant because C is a widely-used systems programming language, and TCO can improve performance and enable deeper recursion in certain applications. It also brings C closer to functional languages that have long supported TCO, potentially influencing coding practices and compiler design. The LWN article notes that in 1994, C compilers did not perform tail-call optimization for typical usage, highlighting how recent this feature is. The discussion also mentions that TCO was added and then removed from JavaScript, illustrating the complexity and trade-offs involved in implementing TCO.

hackernews · prakashqwerty · Aug 10, 11:34 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49242297)

**Background**: Tail-call optimization is a compiler technique that optimizes recursive function calls where the recursive call is the last operation before returning. Instead of allocating a new stack frame, the compiler reuses the current frame, allowing for unbounded recursion without stack overflow. This is a standard feature in many functional languages like ML and Haskell, but has been historically absent from C due to its low-level nature and the need for predictable stack behavior.

## [Parametron: 1950s Japanese Magnetic Logic Computer Technology](https://ethw.org/Milestones:Parametron,_1954) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The article highlights the parametron, a logic element invented by Eiichi Goto in 1954, which was used in early Japanese computers like the NEAC-1101 and PC-1, offering an alternative to vacuum tubes and transistors. This technology is historically significant as it represents a forgotten path in computing history, and its modern descendant, the quantum flux parametron, could influence future quantum computing architectures. The NEAC-1101, completed in 1958, used 3,600 parametrons and supported decimal 7-digit floating-point operations. The parametron was based on magnetic core logic, which represented binary states through magnetization direction.

**Background**: In the 1950s, computing technology was transitioning from vacuum tubes to transistors, but various alternative technologies were explored. Magnetic logic, including parametrons and transfluxors, used ferrite cores' non-linear properties for digital logic. The quantum flux parametron, invented by Eiichi Goto, is a superconducting logic device based on Josephson junctions, offering potential for ultra-high-speed, low-power computation.

## [SQLite Text Revision History Compression Prototype](https://simonwillison.net/2026/Aug/9/sqlite-text-history-prototype/#atom-everything) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Simon Willison prototyped storing text revision histories in SQLite by compressing a JSON array of all previous versions with zlib or zstd, achieving 20.4 MB of raw text compressed to 80.3 KB. He used GPT-5.6 Sol Pro to build the prototype, which also suggested chunking history into multiple rows to avoid recompressing the entire array on each edit. This approach offers a simple and efficient way to store revision histories in relational databases, potentially reducing storage overhead significantly for applications that track document edits. It could influence how developers design versioning systems, especially in lightweight or embedded database contexts like SQLite. The prototype simulated 1,000 revisions, compressing a JSON array of full text versions with Zstandard. To avoid decompressing and recompressing the entire array on every edit, the history is split into multiple rows, each containing at most 128 revisions or 3 MB of uncompressed JSON.

rss · Simon Willison · Aug 9, 22:05

**Background**: Storing revision histories in relational databases is challenging because naive approaches store a full copy of each version, leading to high storage costs. Compression algorithms like zlib and zstd are designed to reduce redundancy in data, making them effective for compressing arrays of similar text versions. SQLite is a popular embedded database that supports BLOB columns, which can store compressed binary data.

## [EntropyMoE: Entropy-Aware Sparse Routing for Byte-Patch LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06398) ⭐️ 7.0/10

EntropyMoE introduces a Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) architecture for byte-patch large language models, replacing dense feed-forward layers with Top-K expert layers. The router selects experts based on patch entropy, achieving the lowest held-out bits-per-byte among matched baselines while maintaining comparable downstream accuracy. This work addresses a key limitation in tokenizer-free LLMs by enabling adaptive computation based on patch complexity, potentially improving efficiency and scalability. It extends MoE modeling beyond tokenizer-based representations, opening new avenues for efficient byte-level language modeling. The router uses patch entropy as the sole input for expert selection, while patch length contributes only to workload accounting. Experiments show EntropyMoE achieves the lowest held-out bits-per-byte among dense and sparse baselines, with comparable downstream accuracy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Byte-level LLMs group bytes into dynamic patches to avoid tokenization, but existing architectures apply uniform dense computation to all patches. Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) layers use a router to select top-k experts per input, enabling sparse conditional computation. Patch entropy, derived from byte-level prediction uncertainty, has been used in prior work like Byte Latent Transformer for patch construction.

## [LUCID: LLM-Guided Interpretable Unsupervised Community Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06402) ⭐️ 7.0/10

LUCID is a novel community detection method that leverages large language models (LLMs) to guide an unsupervised, training-free, and interpretable four-stage pipeline, inspired by phase-transition kinetics. It achieves state-of-the-art performance on real-world datasets, outperforming leading unsupervised and semi-supervised baselines. This work addresses key limitations of existing community detection methods, which either struggle with complex graph structures or rely on labeled data and lack interpretability. By combining LLM reasoning with a structured pipeline, LUCID offers a promising direction for interpretable and label-free graph analytics, potentially impacting fields like social network analysis and biological network mining. The LUCID pipeline consists of four stages: Local-View Community Initialization (using k-ego contexts and unsupervised node roles), Multi-factor Community Merge (iterative merging via LLM-induced rules), Multi-grain Community Refinement (parallel coarse-to-fine refinement), and Global-view Community Selection (based on topological compactness and boundary clarity). The method is training-free and fully unsupervised, relying on LLM-induced formal rules to translate implicit knowledge into explicit logical structures.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Community detection is a fundamental task in graph analytics that aims to identify cohesive groups of entities with similar behaviors or interests. Traditional methods often rely on objective functions or deep learning, but they face challenges such as handling complex structures, requiring labeled data, and lacking interpretability. Large language models (LLMs) have strong reasoning capabilities and world knowledge, making them promising for interpretable, label-free community detection. The proposed LUCID method is inspired by phase-transition kinetics, where complex structures emerge through initialization, merging, refinement, and selection.

The paper introduces ADIAS, a framework for automated full-code agent design that shifts from candidate-centric to issue-centric optimization, using a persistent issue state to guide repair. It outperforms the strongest baseline by 25.2% on average across five interactive benchmarks. This work addresses inefficiencies in existing automated agent design methods, potentially improving the efficiency and effectiveness of AI agent development. It offers a new perspective that could influence future research and tools in the AI/ML and software engineering communities. ADIAS maintains a persistent issue state with stable issue identities, lifecycle status, supporting evidence, and intervention-outcome histories. Controlled ablations show that removing this state or reverting to candidate-centric policies causes performance drops of up to 40.7%.

The paper introduces C4, a cognition-inspired framework for evaluating cross-concept creativity in multimodal large language models (MLLMs), using Chinese idioms (Chengyu). It includes C4-Eval, a dataset of 184 synthetic and 37 human-created items, and tests ten MLLMs across five task settings, finding that the best closed models achieve only around 50% accuracy. This work addresses a critical gap in evaluating creative capabilities of MLLMs, which are important for design, communication, and human-AI collaboration. The framework provides a structured, culturally specific benchmark that could drive improvements in MLLM creative reasoning and inspire further research in cross-concept understanding. The C4 framework uses a manually annotated and third-party-reviewed cross-concept network, with difficulty indexed by bridge count and depth. The evaluation includes 884 primary answer-recovery cases, and results show that candidate constraints improve accuracy sharply, while bridge hints and explanation requests provide only modest gains.

The C4 framework operationalizes item construction as cross-concept encoding and model inference as cross-concept decoding, providing a structured way to generate and evaluate creative tasks.

## [KnowPlan: Knowledge-Driven AI Agents for Smart Degree Pathway Planning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06530) ⭐️ 7.0/10

KnowPlan is a new AI agent framework that separates curriculum extraction from degree planning, using an extraction-first boundary to reconstruct university curricula and then optimize student-specific pathways. It achieves 96.2% inventory recall and 88.7% masked-source recovery while reducing source access by 47%, and maintains 100% hard feasibility with improved utility. This approach addresses a real problem in higher education by decoupling extraction and planning, preventing failure modes from masking each other. It could significantly improve the accuracy and efficiency of degree planning tools, benefiting students and institutions. CatalogBrowse uses a lower-confidence expected marginal gain scoring and a span-constrained clause-to-AST model fallback, terminating on a closure certificate. DegreeMap compiles the extracted documents into a typed requirement hypergraph and optimizes lexicographically with CP-SAT, ensuring certifiability within solver budget.

## [TaskSense: Task-Centric World Models with Stochastic Spatial Attention](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06544) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TaskSense introduces a task-centric world modeling framework that uses a differentiable stochastic spatial attention mechanism, conditioned on the previous latent state, to focus on task-relevant regions before latent encoding. It also incorporates an auxiliary inverse-dynamics objective and reconstructs only attended regions, outperforming DreamerV3 on the Distracting Control Suite. This work addresses a critical limitation in world models for visual control: the mismatch between full-observation reconstruction and control objectives, which degrades performance under visual distractions. By improving robustness to distractors, TaskSense could enhance the reliability of model-based reinforcement learning in real-world applications like robotics and autonomous driving. The stochastic spatial attention is differentiable and conditioned on the previous latent state, and the decoder is conditioned on the sampled attention map to ensure consistent reconstruction. TaskSense maintains competitive performance on the DeepMind Control Suite while consistently outperforming DreamerV3 on the Distracting Control Suite, with qualitative analysis confirming that learned attention localizes control-relevant regions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: World models for visual control learn compact latent states by reconstructing observations, which often preserves task-irrelevant information like background clutter. This can dilute learning signals for control-relevant features, especially under visual distractions. TaskSense builds on prior work like DreamerV3 and addresses this by enforcing task relevance before latent encoding.

## [AgentPatch: Training-Free Repair for Merging Agentic Multimodal LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06699) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AgentPatch introduces a training-free, coarse-to-fine repair framework to merge agentic multimodal large language models (MLLMs), addressing asymmetric capability preservation and behavior-critical forgetting. It produces a single static checkpoint without routing or ensembles, improving performance across six agentic and multimodal benchmarks. This work tackles a novel and practical challenge in consolidating specialized agentic MLLMs into a generalist, which is crucial for advancing autonomous AI systems. By offering a training-free solution, it lowers the barrier for model merging and could accelerate the deployment of versatile multimodal agents in real-world applications. AgentPatch selects a stable merged backbone, restores diluted weak-task-specific signals via Weak-Task Unique Residual Recovery, and applies an Agent-Guided Behavior-Critical Patch to recover decisive behaviors under explicit capability protection. The framework is evaluated on six benchmarks, showing improved weak-task recovery while preserving complementary search and agentic visual processing capabilities.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Model merging is an efficient technique that combines multiple models trained on different data or tasks into a single model without requiring raw training data or expensive computation. Agentic multimodal LLMs extend traditional MLLMs by adding planning, tool use, and interaction capabilities, but they are often specialized for particular tools or environments, making consolidation challenging. AgentPatch addresses the specific issues of asymmetric capability preservation and behavior-critical forgetting that arise during merging.

## [bioMoR: First Biology-Guided Mixture-of-Recursions for Genomic Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06727) ⭐️ 7.0/10

bioMoR is the first framework to apply Mixture-of-Recursions (MoR) to gene-level and pathway-level learning, integrating biological knowledge through graph-based information sharing, structural bias in self-attention, and a graph-aware router. It improves average macro-F1 by 8.2 percentage points and balanced accuracy by 7.1 percentage points over the strongest biology-agnostic MoR baseline while using 75% fewer parameters and up to 58% fewer FLOPs than a non-recursive Transformer. This work addresses the critical challenge of efficiency in high-dimensional omics analysis, where only a subset of genes or pathways requires deep computation. By integrating biological knowledge into the MoR architecture, bioMoR not only improves performance but also provides biological interpretability, potentially accelerating genomics research and enabling more efficient models for large-scale genomic data. bioMoR identifies three locations for integrating structured biological knowledge within an MoR backbone: graph-based information sharing refines token embeddings, a structural bias guides self-attention toward biologically related tokens, and a graph-aware router uses neighborhood information to determine each token's recursion depth. The model was evaluated across eight benchmarks spanning diverse omics data types under a unified five-fold cross-validation protocol, and the selected marker genes or pathways provide biological interpretability while token-specific recursion depths reveal computation allocation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Transformer models for high-dimensional omics analysis process thousands of genes or pathways, but only a subset requires deep computation. Mixture-of-Recursions (MoR) improves efficiency through adaptive token-choice or expert-choice routing, allowing the model to allocate computation dynamically. bioMoR extends MoR by incorporating biological knowledge, such as gene interaction networks, to guide the routing and attention mechanisms, making the model more efficient and interpretable for genomic data.

Capek 0.5 introduces an execution-centric capability taxonomy for embodied vision-language models, organizing capabilities into four families: Spatial Reasoning, Temporal Understanding, Action Guidance, and State Verification. ## [Surg-UniWorld: Unified Surgical World Model with Multimodal Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06770) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Surg-UniWorld introduces a unified surgical world model that uses hierarchical anchors and multimodal control experts to generate realistic instrument-tissue interactions. It also constructs the Cholec80-SurgWAM benchmark for controllable surgical video generation. This work addresses a critical gap in controllable surgical simulation by providing a unified multimodal control paradigm, which can enhance surgical AI training and simulation. It outperforms existing methods in generation quality, temporal consistency, and multimodal controllability, potentially impacting medical imaging and robotic surgery. The model uses a Hierarchical Surgical Anchor from first-frame appearance and semantic masks, Anchor-Relative Modality Experts for edge, depth, and optical flow, and a Multimodal Control Expert for stage-wise composition. It leverages the Wan2.2 video diffusion backbone and introduces the Cholec80-SurgWAM benchmark.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: World models are generative models that simulate environments, and in surgery, they can synthesize realistic instrument-tissue interactions for training AI systems. Existing methods lack a unified multimodal control paradigm, and direct fusion of heterogeneous visual conditions often causes distortions. Surg-UniWorld addresses this by using hierarchical anchors and modality experts to preserve scene identity and control generation.

<a id="item-155"></a>
## [Gated-BEPO: Confidence-Gated Bellman Credit Assignment for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06861) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Gated-BEPO introduces a novel credit assignment method for large language model (LLM) agents, deriving step-level credit from empirical rollout graphs using a mean-backup Bellman fixed point and a confidence gate to selectively fuse episode- and step-level credit. Experiments on WebShop, ALFWorld, and visual Sokoban show consistent improvements across language and vision-language models. This work addresses a key limitation in training LLM agents for long-horizon tasks: existing methods either propagate rewards uniformly or rely on fixed-weight fusion, failing to distinguish useful actions in failed trajectories. By providing a theoretically grounded and adaptive credit assignment, Gated-BEPO could improve the efficiency and effectiveness of reinforcement learning for LLM agents, potentially benefiting applications in autonomous task completion and interactive environments. The method constructs an empirical graph for each rollout group, estimating node values via a mean-backup Bellman fixed point that reflects the current policy's action distribution. It then accumulates temporal-difference residuals using generalized advantage estimation (GAE) to obtain step-level Bellman advantages, and a confidence gate incorporates Bellman credit only at states with multiple observed successors, otherwise using episode-level credit. Diagnostic ablations confirm the effectiveness of Bellman fixed-point value estimation and show that step-level credit should be incorporated selectively rather than uniformly.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Training large language model (LLM) agents in long-horizon environments requires assigning credit from sparse terminal outcomes to individual actions. Existing critic-free methods propagate trajectory-level rewards uniformly across steps, while recent approaches construct step-level groups by matching repeated states and compare actions within each group. The Bellman equation is a fundamental concept in reinforcement learning that relates the value of a state to the values of successor states, and the mean-backup Bellman fixed point is a variant that averages over possible next states according to the policy's action distribution.

The benchmark captures diverse attribution settings, including local and long-range attribution as well as structured attribution chains. Reference baselines based on incremental trajectory contribution and component-level leave-one-out perturbation show substantial performance differences across these settings, providing an initial characterization of the benchmark's challenges.

## [ReGraph: Structured Recipe Graphs from Food Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06917) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ReGraph introduces a large-scale recipe graph dataset that represents cooking processes as structured graphs, along with Recipe Reasoning Chain-of-Thought (RR-CoT) traces, and proposes a two-stage Recipe Graph Learning (RGL) framework to generate these graphs from food images using LMMs. This work addresses a gap in evaluating LMMs for procedural knowledge by making cooking processes explicit and compositional, revealing that current models often produce plausible text but lack recoverable structure. It provides a benchmark and method that could improve multimodal learning and knowledge representation in the culinary domain. The ReGraph dataset includes entities for ingredients, actions, and tools, with attributes for ingredient states and typed relations for procedural ordering. Experiments show that existing approaches achieve competitive text scores but limited structure alignment, while RGL consistently improves entity and relation generation across two LMM backbones, with ingredient-state capture remaining the most challenging.

**Background**: Large Multimodal Models (LMMs) process and generate content across modalities like vision and language, and have been applied to recipe generation from food images. However, cooking is a structured process with ordered actions and state changes, which free-form text often leaves implicit. Graph representations make this procedural knowledge explicit and compositional, providing a structured basis for evaluation. ReGraph leverages this by creating a dataset and framework to generate recipe graphs, aiming to assess and improve process-level understanding in LMMs.

## [TRIBE: Predicting Team Performance via Communication Behavior Ensembles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06926) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TRIBE is a novel domain-independent approach that uses communication behavior ensembles to categorize teams into performance-predictive behavioral tribes, enabling early prediction of team performance as early as 10% into a task. It was tested on four diverse datasets and compared with Llama, achieving significant speedup with performance improvement. This work provides a scalable, domain-independent tool for predicting team performance from communication patterns, which is crucial for designing autonomous agents that assist human teams. It also reveals how AI agents alter team behavioral trajectories, offering insights for human-AI teaming and timely interventions. The prediction strength varies by the degree of behavioral freedom allowed by the task structure. Temporal analysis shows that AI agents significantly alter team behavioral trajectories, while human advisors align with natural dynamics, and teams maintain behavioral flexibility throughout collaboration.

## [Modality Transfer: Key to Autonomous GIS Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06948) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a novel modality transfer task for Large Multimodal Models (LMMs), requiring them to describe an image of colored squares in a grid and then regenerate the image from that description. The authors evaluate recent OpenAI LMMs and find they still struggle with this task, highlighting a critical bottleneck in geospatial understanding. This work addresses a prerequisite for autonomous GIS agents, which need to seamlessly switch between text and image modalities in spatial workflows. By identifying the limitations of current LMMs in modality transfer, it guides future research toward robust multi-modal alignment, potentially accelerating the development of fully automated GIS analysis pipelines. The task involves two steps: first, an LMM describes an input image of colored squares in a regular grid; second, a new LMM instance regenerates the image from the textual description. The study uses spatial information theory to quantify modality transfer capability, and results show that recent OpenAI LMMs still struggle with this simple spatial grid task.

Autonomous GIS agents aim to automate geospatial data retrieval, analysis, and map making, requiring seamless modality transfer. This paper highlights the need for rigorous multi-modal alignment to achieve robust geospatial understanding in LMMs.

## [Multi-Agent Triage Pipelines Don't Reduce LLM Bias, Study Finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06949) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new simulation study on GPT-4o-mini found that distributing triage decisions across a nine-agent pipeline with an independent audit does not reduce demographic bias compared to a single LLM (6.9% vs. 6.1%, p = 0.498). The study also found that audit capacity constraints cause 30.0% of biased outcomes to go undetected, rising to 43.8% when the auditor is overloaded. This challenges the common assumption that adding oversight layers to LLM pipelines automatically catches bias, which is critical for real-world deployments in healthcare, disaster response, and other high-stakes resource allocation. The findings highlight the need for better audit mechanisms and capacity planning, not just more agents. The study used a synthetic disaster-triage simulator with 192 episodes (2,304 resolved case pairs) and three pressure dimensions. The effect of audit capacity on catching bias was driven almost entirely by coverage (dropping from 100.0% to 65.6% under load, p < 0.001), not by degraded judgment on reviewed cases. Reordering the audit queue by estimated risk recovered most lost coverage (65.6% to 91.7%, p = 0.028).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) have been shown to exhibit demographic bias in resource-allocation decisions. Real-world deployments often use multi-agent pipelines with review steps intended to catch such failures. This study simulates a disaster-triage scenario where patients arrive needing scarce hospital beds, and an LLM-based pipeline decides who gets treated. The pipeline includes assessment, allocation, and independent audit roles, and the study compares it to a single-agent control.

## [LLMs Show Systematic Critical Acclaim Bias in Film Preferences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06955) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new study of eight large language models from four families (Anthropic, OpenAI, Alibaba, and Mistral) reveals that they consistently prefer critically acclaimed films over commercially successful ones, based on 20,000 pairwise comparisons per model analyzed with Bradley-Terry estimation. This finding highlights a systematic cultural bias in LLMs that could affect how they generate recommendations or evaluations in real-world applications. Understanding this bias is crucial for AI alignment and for ensuring that LLMs do not inadvertently reproduce elite cultural hierarchies. The study used a 200-film benchmark divided into critically acclaimed, commercially successful, and dual-legitimacy films. Nested OLS regression analyses showed that evaluative orientation, public visibility, and popular reception distinctly explain preferences, and adjusting for public visibility reversed preferences for dual-legitimacy films over critical acclaim-only films.

## [CAi Copilot: Intent-Driven Agentic Workflows for Molecular Design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06961) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CAi Copilot introduces an intent-driven agentic workflow system that coordinates specialized molecular design tools into adaptive, traceable runs. In benchmarks across 45 tasks, it achieved an outcome score of 84.59, outperforming the next-best result by 18.07 points. This addresses a critical bottleneck in computational drug discovery: the manual coordination of disparate tools. By automating intent-to-evidence workflows, it could significantly reduce operational workload and accelerate early-stage molecular design. The system comprises three layers: a Research Interface Layer that translates intent into executable plans, an Agent Reasoning Layer that guides runs using interim results, and an Execution Substrate providing molecular tools and backend services. The paper also notes limitations in long-horizon execution.

## [FedLBW: Loss-Based Weighting for Robust Federated Learning in Wireless Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07007) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FedLBW introduces a novel aggregation strategy for federated learning that weights client updates by the inverse of their validation loss on a server-side proxy dataset, instead of dataset size. Experiments show up to 7.6% higher accuracy on CIFAR-10 in extreme non-IID settings and improved resilience to client dropouts. This approach addresses key challenges in federated learning over wireless networks, where non-IID data and client dropouts are prevalent. By prioritizing reliable updates, FedLBW can improve model accuracy and convergence speed, making FL more practical for real-world wireless applications. FedLBW was evaluated on FashionMNIST, CIFAR-10, and CIFAR-100 using CNN, ResNet-18, and ResNet-34 models, respectively. It outperformed baselines like FedAvg, FedAvgM, FedProx, FedNova, FedLAW, and FedDkw, and showed exceptional resilience to increasing dropout probabilities.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Federated learning (FL) enables collaborative model training across distributed clients without sharing raw data. Traditional aggregation methods like FedAvg weight updates by dataset size, which can be biased under non-IID data and sensitive to client dropouts. FedLBW addresses these issues by using validation loss on a proxy dataset to weight updates, prioritizing more reliable models.

## [ReQuant: Backprop-Free Fixed-Grid Refinement for PTQ](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07019) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ReQuant introduces a backpropagation-free fixed-grid refinement stage for post-training quantization (PTQ) that iteratively improves discrete weight assignments while preserving the quantized format. It is agnostic to the PTQ initializer and can be applied as a plug-and-play post-processing step to existing PTQ pipelines. This work addresses a common limitation in PTQ where quantized weights are treated as final, offering a complementary optimization stage that can improve model efficiency without backpropagation. It is particularly beneficial for low-bit-width quantization and simple initializers, potentially enhancing LLM deployment on resource-constrained devices. ReQuant iteratively revisits discrete weight assignments on a fixed quantization grid, accepting only updates that strictly reduce the mean squared reconstruction error. Experiments across diverse model families, bit-widths, and downstream tasks show consistent improvements, with ReQuant able to refine a simple round-to-nearest initialization to approach or surpass GPTAQ under the same format.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Post-training quantization (PTQ) is a widely used technique to reduce the memory and computational cost of large language models by converting weights to lower-bit integers. Existing PTQ methods typically use heuristic rules or greedy optimization to obtain an initial quantized model, and the resulting integer assignments are usually considered final. ReQuant introduces a refinement stage that treats these assignments as a feasible starting point and continues optimizing them without backpropagation, which is computationally efficient and complementary to existing approaches.

DocMemo introduces a novel framework that formulates long-document reasoning as dynamic evidence exploration, maintaining a tri-level memory system (Document Schema Memory, Page Belief Memory, and Question Episodic Memory) to refine page selection via Bayesian belief updating and Thompson sampling. DocMemo uses Bayesian page belief updating with Thompson sampling, spatial proximity propagation, and structure-aware adaptive-granularity evidence access, supplementing page-level evidence with fine-grained visual regions. The tri-level memory captures structural priors, dynamic relevance estimation, and query-specific reasoning trajectories, enabling continuous refinement of cross-round page selection.

## [MemWM: Memory-Augmented World Model Boosts Text-Based Agent Planning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07107) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MemWM introduces a memory-augmented text-based world model that uses a curated memory bank of transition rules, state caches, and hard-to-predict facts to condition next-state prediction. It improves Structured State Fidelity (SSF) by up to 206.3% over SFT and achieves up to 65.4% relative gain in downstream task success across ALFWorld, WebShop, and ScienceWorld. This work addresses a known limitation of world models—fluent but factually inaccurate next-state predictions—by leveraging memory, which is crucial for reliable planning in text-based agents. The significant improvements in state fidelity and task success suggest that memory augmentation could become a standard component in future world model designs, impacting AI research and applications in interactive environments. MemWM uses a policy-side world skill mechanism that retrieves task-level skills and step-wise corrective guidance while keeping the policy model frozen. Sensitivity analyses show that retrieved memory improves task success and efficiency under different memory and action-budget settings, and the paper introduces SSF as a primary metric to evaluate factual state preservation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

## [MARL-Centered Reference Architecture for LLM Augmentation in Smart Manufacturing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07148) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a MARL-centered reference architecture and taxonomy for augmenting multiagent reinforcement learning with large language models in smart manufacturing, addressing six coupled demands of adaptive control. It introduces a three-layer architecture and a conditional capability profile to evaluate LLM roles. This work provides a structured framework for integrating LLMs with MARL, a timely and complex problem, offering valuable guidance for researchers and practitioners. It clarifies where LLMs can augment versus replace traditional MARL, impacting the design of future smart manufacturing systems. The taxonomy organizes literature through four LLM attachment points: policy, reward design, communication, and hierarchical planning. The paper concludes that conventional MARL is better for frequent, structured, decentralized coordination, while LLMs are promising for semantic interpretation, reward drafting, and slower supervisory planning, but not yet for strict real-time safety-critical control.

## [Agent Memory Distillation Boosts Small LLM Tool Use](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07169) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Agent Memory Distillation (AMD), a training-free framework, transfers hierarchical memory from a large teacher agent (GPT-5-mini) to small student agents (4B-8B parameters), improving tool-use accuracy by up to 27.2 percentage points on AppWorld without additional training. This method addresses the challenge of making small language models effective tool users, which is crucial for deploying efficient and cost-effective AI agents in real-world applications. By enabling smaller models to leverage teacher knowledge, AMD could reduce reliance on large, expensive models while maintaining competitive performance. AMD constructs three memory types: Workflow, Subtask, and Function, injected proactively or reactively. Evaluations on three benchmarks (AppWorld, BFCL V3, ToolSandbox) show average gains of 27.2, 11.2, and 3.4 percentage points, with Subtask memory contributing most and 4B-sized students benefiting most.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents often use memory systems to improve performance, but small models struggle to generate successful trajectories on their own. Memory distillation is a technique to transfer knowledge from larger models to smaller ones, and hierarchical memory organizes information at different levels of abstraction, such as task-level strategies and concrete examples.

## [Causal Identifiability Conditions for Probabilistic Logic Programs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07230) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper derives conditions under which the causal order in acyclic probabilistic logic programs is uniquely determined by the learned probability distribution, incorporating relational symmetry constraints. This provides a method for verifying when a learned program supports well-defined intervention semantics. This addresses a significant open problem in probabilistic logic programming and statistical relational AI, bridging probabilistic and causal reasoning. It enables more reliable interventional reasoning in learned models, which is crucial for AI/ML applications requiring causal inference. The method exploits the relationship between acyclic probabilistic logic programs and Bayesian networks, using the distribution to determine causal order. It incorporates constraints from relational structure by considering prescribed sets of causal symmetries induced by the underlying relational vocabulary.

Causal identifiability asks whether a causal structure can be uniquely determined from observational data, a key question in causal inference.

## [WNM-3D: Generative World Navigation Model with 3D Scene Conditioning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07267) ⭐️ 7.0/10

WNM-3D introduces a generative world-action model for continuous vision-language navigation that conditions future-view and action generation on geometry-aware 3D scene representations. It uses a frozen feed-forward geometry encoder and a trainable 3D Scene-to-Token Adapter to provide shared geometric context for both future-view and action generation. This work addresses a significant gap in existing world-action models for navigation, which typically rely on 2D image histories and lack explicit 3D scene understanding. By integrating 3D scene conditioning, WNM-3D improves closed-loop navigation performance, potentially advancing embodied AI and robotics applications. WNM-3D is trained using supervised world-action fine-tuning on A*-generated demonstrations, DAgger-style adaptation, and DanceGRPO-based closed-loop policy optimization. Experiments on GN-Bench show that WNM-3D outperforms strong VLM-based navigation policies and its 2D-conditioned counterpart, achieving higher flow-action consistency and lower visual-motion error on a fixed near-goal evaluation set.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language navigation (VLN) is an embodied AI task where an agent follows natural language instructions to navigate through environments. Generative world-action models (WAMs) jointly predict future observations and actions, but existing WAMs for continuous VLN do not condition generation on geometry-aware 3D scene representations. WNM-3D addresses this by extracting geometry-aware representations from monocular RGB history and using them to condition a diffusion transformer.

This paper introduces a novel method to mitigate scoring bias in LLM-as-a-Judge by measuring and rectifying latent numerical bias via random number generation. The method was tested on four tasks and outperformed baselines including previous calibration methods. Scoring bias in LLM evaluators can lead to unreliable evaluation results, affecting model development and comparison. This method provides a practical way to improve the fairness and accuracy of LLM-based evaluation, which is crucial for the growing use of LLMs as judges in NLP research and applications. The method instructs an LLM to generate random numbers and measures the deviation from a uniform distribution to identify latent numerical bias. It then rectifies token generation probabilities during evaluation, and the experiments show that bias varies across LLMs, tasks, and score ranges, highlighting the need for task-specific measurement.

## [Evaluating XAI Support from Hierarchical RL Policy in Human-Agent Collaboration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06381) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This study provides the first systematic evaluation of XAI explanations generated from an intrinsically explainable learned policy in the Overcooked-AI benchmark, using the HA^2 architecture and a novel trigger-based system to deliver text or audio explanations. The between-subjects experiment (n=38) found no significant performance effects, but audio explanations significantly reduced participants' working-alliance bond with the agent. This work addresses a gap in XAI research by moving beyond hand-crafted policies and custom environments to a state-of-the-art benchmark, enhancing generalizability. The finding that audio explanations negatively affect the human-agent alliance highlights the importance of matching explanation modality to the policy's capacity, which could inform the design of more effective collaborative XAI systems. The experiment used the Hierarchical Ad Hoc Agents (HA^2) architecture in Overcooked-AI, generating real-time explanations from hierarchical subtask selections. The study found no significant performance effects, though participants with explanations showed trends toward faster performance improvement; notably, audio explanations caused a significant reduction in working-alliance bond, an effect not seen with text.

The working alliance is a concept from psychotherapy that measures the collaborative bond between a helper and a helpee, and has been applied to human-agent interaction. ## [TEXAS: Task-Expert-Aware Supervision for MoE LLM Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06396) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TEXAS introduces a novel method that identifies task-relevant experts by comparing activations on correctly and incorrectly solved instances, then allocates token-level supervision during fine-tuning. It achieves best or tied-best performance in 17 of 18 settings across three MoE models and six benchmarks, improving over the strongest baseline by 1.3–1.5 points on average. This method addresses key limitations in MoE LLM adaptation by using correctness-conditioned signals rather than aggregate routing statistics, and by leveraging token-level supervision. It offers a more effective and efficient way to fine-tune large MoE models, potentially benefiting researchers and practitioners in model adaptation and efficiency. TEXAS retains experts that are more strongly activated on successful instances and upweights answer tokens in failed instances when they activate these experts. It does not restrict adaptation to a fixed expert subset or impose an explicit target routing distribution, leveraging existing routing behavior.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) language models route each token through a small subset of experts, enabling sparse activation and conditional computation. Fine-tuning adapts a pre-trained model to a specific task, and routing patterns can help identify task-relevant experts. However, prior methods often rely on aggregate routing statistics that reflect usage rather than task success, and they underexplore token-level supervision.

This paper addresses the challenge of reconstructing original explanations from such transformed proofs.

## [WorldMark: Plug-and-Play World Knowledge Interface for LLM Watermarking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06416) ⭐️ 7.0/10

WorldMark introduces a plug-and-play interface that uses a World Knowledge Memory (WKM) graph to compute token-level knowledge saliency scores and adjust watermark strength via Asymmetric Knowledge Modulation (AKM), improving detection robustness in open-ended text generation without retraining the backbone model. This work addresses a key limitation of existing watermarking methods that rely solely on local token statistics, which may be insufficient for robust signal placement in open-ended generation. By leveraging world knowledge, WorldMark enhances provenance tracking for LLM outputs, which is critical for AI security and content authenticity, and it does so without adding detector-side complexity. On the C4 benchmark, the complete WorldMark interface improves clean and attacked detection across three adaptive-strength host watermark variants while slightly reducing perplexity. It requires no additional detector-side model or parameter and introduces negligible overhead under the primary protocol.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Large language model watermarking embeds statistically detectable signals during decoding to trace text provenance. Existing methods fall into logits-based, sampling-based, entropy-aware, and adaptive-strength families, but they all place signals based on local token statistics. WorldMark introduces a memory graph that organizes semantic and episodic knowledge, converting retrieved knowledge into saliency scores to modulate watermark strength, addressing the insufficiency of local statistics in open-ended generation.

## [CertBind: Certifiable Composition of Frozen Multimodal Encoders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06516) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CertBind introduces a multiscale theoretical framework for certifiable composition of frozen multimodal encoders, providing finite-sample guarantees for retrieval decisions. It evaluates a shared C-MCR route where native CLIP R@1 dropped from 0.524 to 0.290, but the production fallback recovered 0.963 ± 0.002 of clean retrieval with a no-harm value of 1.000. This work extends multimodal composability from connected representations to certifiable task decisions, addressing a critical gap in reliable AI deployment. It could significantly impact retrieval systems and other AI applications by providing statistical guarantees, enhancing trustworthiness in high-stakes environments. CertBind operates at four scales: node (native anchors establish task identification boundaries), edge (contract-aware conformal ranks provide family-wise error control), path (overlap-aware budget yields finite-sample recovery radius), and query (covered top-k candidate set becomes a point certificate when size equals k). It classifies routes as Direct, Recovery, Certified, or Abstain based on these guarantees.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Lightweight connectors allow frozen multimodal encoders to be composed at the representation level, but deployment exposes issues at the task decision level. Conformal prediction is a technique for uncertainty quantification that provides statistically valid prediction regions assuming exchangeability of data. CertBind leverages these concepts to provide certifiable guarantees for retrieval decisions in multimodal systems.

## [LLM Outputs Show Model-Specific Idiolects, Not a Single AI Language](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06589) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper argues that LLM outputs exhibit model-specific linguistic signatures akin to human idiolects, challenging the notion of a collective 'AI language'. It provides empirical evidence from two datasets (2024 and 2026) showing both generational shifts and unique profiles per model. This perspective could reshape how researchers analyze and detect LLM-generated text, with implications for forensic linguistics and usage-based language studies. It also highlights the need to consider model-specific variation in AI text analysis. The study uses stylometric principal component analysis and computational descriptors on texts from six models in each cohort. Contraction frequencies varied dramatically, from over 1,200 to over 30,000 per million words within the 2026 cohort.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Stylometric analysis uses computational methods to quantify writing style, often employing principal component analysis to identify authorial fingerprints. The concept of idiolect refers to an individual's unique way of speaking or writing, which this paper applies to LLMs as distinct linguistic entities.

## [SLED: Scalable Location Encoding via Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06612) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SLED introduces a distillation-based location encoder that uses geospatial location as a binding modality to pretrain with any modality of geospatial data, achieving performance with batch sizes as small as 128. It eliminates the need for spatiotemporal coregistration and scales efficiently to multiple modalities. This addresses the computational and scalability limitations of current CLIP-style location encoders, which require large batch sizes and suffer from false negatives. SLED's lightweight and modular design could enable broader adoption of location encoders in Earth observation and geospatial AI applications, reducing pretraining costs and improving flexibility. SLED was pretrained on Sentinel-1, Sentinel-2, and Landsat imagery, and evaluated on 19 human-centric benchmark tasks. Both unimodal and multimodal SLED models match or outperform existing approaches, and the framework supports flexible incorporation of multiple modalities without spatiotemporal coregistration.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Location encoders compress Earth observation data into location-specific embeddings, but current state-of-the-art methods rely on CLIP-style contrastive learning, which requires large batch sizes (16K-32K) and scales poorly with additional modalities. Knowledge distillation is a technique that transfers knowledge from a large teacher model to a smaller student model, enabling efficient training and deployment. SLED leverages this concept to create a scalable and modular location encoder.

## [3D Medical Foundation Models Show Artifact-Dependent Robustness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06613) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A controlled study evaluated the robustness of five pretrained 3D medical foundation models to MRI artifacts, revealing that 3DINO is the most stable while BrainIAC is highly sensitive. The study used BraTS-Africa data with seven artifacts and multiple metrics. This work addresses a critical gap in understanding how 3D medical foundation models behave under real-world MRI artifacts, which is essential for their safe deployment in clinical settings. The findings caution that larger-scale or domain-specific pretraining does not guarantee artifact invariance. The study generated seven frequency- and image-domain artifacts at five corruption settings, assessing robustness via linear CKA, RankMe, and UMAP, plus segmentation consistency. CKA decreased substantially under many conditions while RankMe remained stable, indicating artifacts distort representation geometry without causing dimensional collapse.

## [Factorized Hypothesis Search Bridges Retrieval Readiness Gap](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06614) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Factorized Hypothesis Search (FHS), a novel method that maintains multiple partial interpretations over named semantic dimensions to address the retrieval readiness gap in large-taxonomy retrieval. FHS achieves state-of-the-art Recall@1, MRR, and final accuracy on financial taxonomy tagging and CodiEsp clinical coding tasks. This work addresses a significant limitation in retrieval systems where input evidence is indirect, such as table cells, which often fail to retrieve the correct taxonomy code. By improving head-ranking performance, FHS could enhance practical applications like automated clinical coding and financial tagging, potentially reducing manual effort and errors. FHS operates in four main stages: hypothesis generation, structured query rendering, multi-hypothesis retrieval, and dimension-level candidate verification. Replacing the factorized hypothesis path with a free-text ensemble causes the largest drop in head-ranking performance, while sequential refinement provides no additional gain over FHS's strong parallel first round.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Large-taxonomy retrieval typically assumes the input already expresses the target concept, but in many real-world scenarios, the input is indirect evidence like a table cell whose meaning depends on context. This mismatch is termed the 'retrieval readiness gap.' FHS addresses this by maintaining multiple partial interpretations over semantic dimensions, enabling more effective retrieval and verification.

## [Policy-Masked Private Experts: Auditable Reversible Access Control for Sparse MoE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06690) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Policy-Masked Private Experts, a method that freezes a pretrained sparse Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) model, trains a disjoint expert branch, and selects public or private experts before top-k routing to enforce auditable and reversible capability access control. Tests on Qwen3-30B-A3B and DeepSeek-V2-Lite show zero unauthorized private expert execution across 64 adversarial scenarios and 96 deny/fail-closed events. This work addresses a novel systems-level security question: controlling which trained parameters are reachable during inference, rather than merely regulating model behavior. It could enable more granular and reversible access control for large language models, with implications for privacy, compliance, and safe deployment of MoE-based systems. The method uses a trusted computing base (TCB) and ensures that unauthorized requests execute no private experts, while the public model's fingerprint remains unchanged. Empirical results show exact recovery of routed private rows and allow-deny-allow recovery, with improvements in tool use accuracy (e.g., 5.0 pp and 21.3 pp on Qwen benchmarks) and a parameter-matched LoRA baseline leaving 1,225 adapter calls under deny.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models use sparse activation, where a router selects a subset of experts (via top-k routing) for each token, improving efficiency. Traditional access control in LLMs typically regulates outputs or behavior but leaves the same computation available to all requests. This paper explores a different approach: controlling which expert parameters are reachable during the forward pass, leveraging the sparse structure of MoE models.

## [HYMELL: Hybrid Framework for Accurate LLM Inference Latency and Energy Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06723) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces HYMELL, a hybrid three-level framework that combines analytical modeling with machine learning to estimate LLM inference latency and energy. Evaluated on NVIDIA H100, it achieves less than 5% error for LLaMA 3 8B in both prefill and decode phases. Accurate estimation of LLM inference costs is crucial for sustainable AI deployment and hardware-aware design. HYMELL enables fast, hardware-free design space exploration and energy-efficient optimization, potentially reducing the environmental impact of large-scale LLM serving. HYMELL models execution through a three-level hierarchy: analytical estimation of primitive operations, ML prediction of higher-level components, and an end-to-end model for system-level overheads. It supports diverse architectures including dense and MoE FFNs, as well as MHA and GQA attention mechanisms.

## [KReF: Training-Free Retrieval Framework for Long-Term Time-Series Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06748) ⭐️ 7.0/10

KReF is a training-free retrieval framework that constructs predictive distributions for long-term time-series forecasting by retrieving similar historical lookback-future pairs. It achieves the lowest CRPS in all 12 dataset-embedding settings and the lowest IS90 in 9 settings across six benchmarks and four horizons. This work challenges the reliance on trained models in probabilistic long-term forecasting, offering a lightweight alternative that matches or surpasses trained baselines on some datasets. It introduces retrieval as an underexplored inductive bias, potentially impacting the efficiency and interpretability of forecasting systems. KReF embeds lookbacks using handcrafted statistics or frozen random Fourier features, and uses similarity weights to define predictive masses, quantiles, CRPS, and a weighted-mean point forecast. It also constructs a probability-integral-transform map from the query lookback and applies validation-selected expansion and shrinkage rates to adapt interval boundaries.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Long-term time-series forecasting (LTSF) often relies on trained deep learning models, which require significant computational resources. Training-free conformal methods provide uncertainty intervals but typically depend on a pre-existing point forecaster and may suffer from delayed feedback at long horizons. Retrieval-based methods, which leverage historical data directly, have been less explored in this context.

## [Progressive Content Refinement with Decaying Reward Joint LinUCB](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06750) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a novel contextual bandit algorithm that explicitly models reward decay using an Expectation-Maximization (EM) algorithm, jointly estimating arm-specific and decay parameters. By embedding prompts as arms, it enables joint learning of arm values, distinguishing it from the traditional disjoint LinUCB framework, and achieves significant performance gains on Sentiment Reversal and GSM8K benchmarks. This work addresses the over-exploitation problem in iterative LLM refinement, where repeated use of the same prompts leads to diminishing rewards. By incorporating reward decay, it improves the efficiency and effectiveness of prompt optimization, which is crucial for applications like automated content refinement and LLM-based decision-making. The algorithm uses an EM-based approach to estimate both arm-specific parameters and decay parameters simultaneously, which is a departure from standard LinUCB that assumes static rewards. The experimental results on Sentiment Reversal and GSM8K show significant gains over strong baselines, and an ablation study confirms that reward decay modeling is crucial for mitigating over-exploitation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Contextual bandit algorithms are a class of reinforcement learning methods that balance exploration and exploitation when selecting among multiple options (arms) based on context. LinUCB is a popular contextual bandit algorithm that models the expected reward of each arm as a linear function of context features. In iterative LLM refinement, prompts are treated as arms, and the goal is to select the best prompt for a given context. However, traditional bandit approaches often ignore the saturation effect, where repeated use of the same prompt yields diminishing returns.

## [Atelier: Shortcut-Aware Control-State Planning for Artist-Grounded Image Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06751) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced Atelier, a shortcut-aware control-state planning framework for artist-grounded text-to-image generation, along with the ArtIntentBench benchmark covering Van Gogh and Qi Baishi. Atelier separates scene anchors, preserve/transform decisions, style-regime hypotheses, role-bound artist evidence, and shortcut-avoidance constraints to improve style fidelity and reduce shortcut substitution. This work addresses a critical limitation in text-to-image generation where models often rely on canonical shortcuts (e.g., recurring motifs) instead of preserving the user's intended scene. By improving artist-level style fidelity and reducing shortcut substitution, Atelier could enhance creative tools for artists and designers, and the new benchmark provides a standardized evaluation for future research. Atelier grounds control states using artist-level knowledge and local patch references, compiles backend-aware generation plans, and iteratively refines candidates through global and local authenticity feedback. The ArtIntentBench benchmark covers artwork re-rendering, period/style-controlled generation, historically unseen subjects, shortcut auditing, and human preference evaluation, and experiments show improvements over prompt-engineered, retrieval-augmented, and general-purpose agent baselines.

Atelier introduces a planning framework that explicitly separates different aspects of artistic intent and uses evidence-grounded controls to guide generation.

This framework addresses a practical gap between task-specific fine-tuning and business metric alignment, potentially improving production recommendation systems. Linear probing (LP) is a technique where only the output head is trained while the backbone is frozen, and full fine-tuning (FFT) updates the entire model. ## [GeneGeoFlow: Control-Anchored Flow Matching for Virtual Cell Perturbation Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06824) ⭐️ 7.0/10

GeneGeoFlow is a new method that conditions a control-anchored residual flow on gene-wise geometry derived from biological networks to predict single-cell transcriptional responses to unseen perturbations. It achieves Pearson Delta scores of 0.8979 on the Norman additive benchmark and 0.9088 on five held-out drug combinations in the fixed ComboSciPlex test split. This work addresses a key limitation in existing graph-based perturbation models by separating stable gene relationships from intervention-specific responses, potentially improving the accuracy of virtual cell models for drug discovery and systems biology. The high performance on benchmarks suggests it could become a valuable tool for predicting cellular responses to novel perturbations. GeneGeoFlow derives multi-scale spectral coordinates from Gene Ontology and control-derived coexpression networks, and uses a perturbation-conditioned, gene-wise gating module to select relevant structural scales and network sources. It employs condition-wise optimal transport for training and a Delta-correlation objective to align predicted and observed expression-shift directions, without explicitly propagating target-derived signals along the graph.

**Background**: Virtual cell modeling aims to predict single-cell transcriptional responses to perturbations, using biological networks as priors. Existing graph-based models often conflate stable gene relationships with perturbation-response pathways, leading to suboptimal predictions. GeneGeoFlow introduces a novel approach by conditioning a flow matching model on gene geometry, which captures stable relationships separately from intervention-specific responses.

## [NysHD: Bridging Hyperdimensional Computing and Kernel Methods via Nyström Approximation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06860) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces NysHD, a method that uses the Nyström approximation to convert any positive-semidefinite similarity function into a hyperdimensional computing (HDC) mapping, providing a theoretical link between HDC and kernel methods. Empirical results show average classification accuracy improvements of 11% on graph datasets and 17% on string datasets compared to existing HDC encoding methods. This work expands the applicability of hyperdimensional computing by enabling the import of a vast literature on kernel design into the HDC setting, potentially improving performance on a wider range of machine learning tasks. It also strengthens the theoretical foundation of HDC, which is valued for its energy-efficient and highly parallel hardware implementations. NysHD provides a simple recipe to turn any user-defined positive-semidefinite similarity function into an equivalent HDC mapping, leveraging the Nyström method for low-rank kernel approximation. The empirical evaluation covers graph and string datasets, showing significant accuracy gains over existing HDC encoding methods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Hyperdimensional computing (HDC) is a computational paradigm inspired by cognitive science, representing data as high-dimensional random vectors and enabling efficient implementations on hardware like FPGAs and processing-in-memory architectures. Kernel methods, such as support vector machines, project data into high-dimensional feature spaces using a kernel function, but often suffer from scalability issues due to the kernel matrix. The Nyström method is a well-known technique for approximating the kernel matrix via low-rank approximations, making kernel methods more scalable. This paper bridges these two fields by using the Nyström method to construct HDC mappings from kernel functions.

FedVAR introduces a weakly-supervised federated learning framework specifically designed for fine-grained video anomaly recognition, addressing semantic misalignment across distributed clients. It leverages Vision-Language Models (VLMs) and a prototype-based alignment mechanism to create a shared semantic anchor, enabling consistent representation of normality and robust prompt-learning of anomaly direction vectors. FedVAR uses prototype-based alignment to re-center and align visual and textual feature spaces across clients, enforcing consistent normality representation with minimal communication overhead. This paper introduces a method to georeference non-gazetteer place names (NGPs) by inverting spatial relations from repeated occurrences in biological specimen records from the Allan Herbarium. It compares deterministic, probabilistic, and LLM-based approaches, with probabilistic inference achieving the highest accuracy (median error 1.43 km). This work addresses the challenge of georeferencing historical or vernacular place names that are absent from standard gazetteers, which is crucial for biodiversity informatics and historical GIS research. The comparative analysis provides insights into the strengths and limitations of traditional modeling versus LLMs for spatial inference, informing future tool development. The study uses a pseudo-NGP benchmark, where probabilistic inference achieved a median error of 1.43 km and A@1 km of 36%, while the LLM-based method had a median error of 1.80 km and A@1 km of 31%. The approach leverages repeated occurrences of the same place name across records with different locations and spatial relation terms to derive constraints.

**Background**: Georeferencing is the process of assigning geographic coordinates to a location description, essential for mapping species distributions in biodiversity databases. A gazetteer is a geographical dictionary of place names, and non-gazetteer place names are historical or vernacular names not listed in standard gazetteers. Biological specimen records often contain textual locality descriptions that lack precise coordinates, making georeferencing challenging.

## [ZCA Whitening Improves WEAT Bias Measurement Reliability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06908) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes using ZCA whitening as a geometric pre-processing step for the Word Embedding Association Test (WEAT) to restore isotropy in embedding spaces. Evaluated on 70 model-task combinations across seven models and ten WEAT suites, the method substantially reduces anisotropy and changes significance status for over 30% of WEAT results. This work addresses a critical methodological flaw in WEAT, a widely used bias measurement tool in AI fairness and computational social science, by ensuring the isotropy assumption holds. It suggests that previously reported bias measurements in anisotropic spaces may be unreliable, prompting re-evaluation and more accurate bias assessments across language models. ZCA whitening transforms the covariance matrix of the embedding space into the identity matrix while minimizing perturbation to original vectors. The study found that after calibration, effect sizes shift in both directions depending on bias category, indicating that uncalibrated measurements can both overestimate and underestimate associations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: WEAT, introduced by Caliskan et al. in 2017, measures bias in word embeddings by comparing cosine similarities between target and attribute word sets, inspired by the Implicit Association Test (IAT). It assumes the embedding space is isotropic, but many language models produce anisotropic spaces, undermining the validity of cosine similarity as a semantic association measure. ZCA whitening is a standard technique that decorrelates features while preserving the original data structure as much as possible.
