## [MaskFlow: Precise, Consistent, Seamless Regional Image Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06929) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MaskFlow introduces a training framework for regional image editing that uses mask-conditioned flow matching and a Soft-Poisson de-seaming module to achieve precise localization, consistent background preservation, and seamless boundary transitions. It also presents MEData, a mask-based image editing dataset created via a data synthesis pipeline. This work addresses a challenging problem in image editing: achieving reliable regional control with natural integration. By improving precision and seamlessness, it could enhance applications like photo editing, content creation, and infographic design, and its dataset may facilitate further research. The method incorporates the mask into the probability path and flow-matching objective, coordinating generation within the editable region with source preservation outside it. The Soft-Poisson de-seaming module refines the predicted vector field during both training and sampling, and experiments show consistent improvements over competing methods on natural scenes and infographic images.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Regional image editing aims to modify specific areas of an image while keeping the rest unchanged. Flow matching is a generative modeling technique that learns a time-dependent vector field to transform a simple distribution into a complex one, and it has been used in various image generation tasks. Mask-conditioned flow matching extends this by conditioning on a mask to control the editing region.

## [Ask-E: Benchmarking and Training Models on Calibrated Question Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06933) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Ask-E is a new environment that benchmarks and trains models on generating questions calibrated to specific skill levels, defined by the capabilities of two existing language models. The paper shows that even frontier models achieve below 50% calibration, and training on this environment improves downstream math benchmarks. This work introduces a novel approach to AI evaluation and training by leveraging the insight that generating calibrated problems requires capability beyond the target level. It could influence future research on model self-improvement and evaluation, providing a new way to measure and enhance model competence without relying on external data or rewards. The benchmark defines target skill levels as ranges bounded by the capabilities of two solver models, and a question is successfully calibrated if exactly one of the two models can solve it. The paper reports that frontier models achieve below 50% calibration, and training on Ask-E improves performance on downstream math benchmarks even without new math data, interaction with stronger models, or correctness-based reward.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional AI training and evaluation focus on answering questions, but generating questions calibrated to a specific difficulty level is a distinct and demanding task. The key insight is that to generate problems at a given frontier, a model must possess capability beyond that frontier, which can be leveraged for training and benchmarking. Ask-E uses two existing language models to define difficulty ranges, providing a concrete and scalable way to measure calibration.

## [Explicit Stance Labels Improve Memory Compression Retention](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06953) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper demonstrates that explicitly labeling epistemic stance as a field, rather than a bracketed aside, significantly improves its retention in compressed agent memory systems. Across 60 claims, the labeled format raised retention by about 15 points on two models, with a pre-registered replication confirming the effect. This finding is significant for AI agent memory systems, as it provides a practical, model-agnostic guideline for improving information retention during compression. It highlights that how information is formatted matters as much as its length, which could influence the design of memory compression algorithms in production systems. The study used matched notes with identical claims and stances, differing only in stance placement, and a blind reader scored the results. The labeled format improved retention by 15 points on two models (37 vs 2 and 30 vs 8; permutation p=0.00005), and a pre-registered replication on Haiku gave +15.6 points (38 vs 1). Ablations showed labels help on both models (+9.7 and +12.8), while length helps on neither; wording as a full sentence was the largest component on one model (+12.5) but negligible on the other (+0.6).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Agent memory systems compress stored information to manage token limits and costs, but compression tends to drop qualifiers like epistemic stance, which indicates the certainty or source of a claim. This paper investigates how formatting affects the retention of such stance information, using rigorous methods like pre-registration and permutation tests to ensure reliability. The findings suggest that making stance explicit as a labeled field is more effective than merely making it longer.

## [GPTKB 2.0: Disambiguated LLM Knowledge Base with Web Demo](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06992) ⭐️ 7.0/10

GPTKB 2.0 is a large-scale knowledge base derived from LLMs, containing 38.4M triples over 1.6M canonical entities, with 207.6K relations and 66K classes. It introduces context-guided entity disambiguation during recursive construction, separating homonyms and merging synonyms, and offers an interactive web interface for browsing, querying, and auditing provenance. This work addresses a key limitation of prior LLM-derived knowledge bases that rely on surface strings, improving entity disambiguation at scale. It provides a practical tool for researchers and practitioners to explore and audit LLM-generated knowledge, enhancing trust and usability in AI/ML and knowledge graph applications. The demo supports SPARQL queries, natural-language questions translated to SPARQL, and entity linking from user-provided text. The full KB is downloadable for offline use, and provenance auditing includes surface forms, candidate matches, source triples, and disambiguation decisions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Knowledge bases store structured facts, often represented as triples (subject, predicate, object). LLM-derived knowledge bases automatically extract such triples from large language models, but often identify entities by surface strings, leading to ambiguity. Entity disambiguation resolves this by using context to distinguish homonyms and merge synonyms, improving accuracy. SPARQL is the standard query language for RDF data, enabling structured queries over knowledge graphs.

## [PILOT Framework Decouples Intention from Trajectory in World Action Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06994) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces PILOT (Physical Inference for Latent Optimized Trajectories), a framework that uses a Representational Deduction (RD) mechanism to decouple high-level intention from low-level trajectory generation in World Action Models. This is achieved by integrating motion chain-of-thought (CoT) guidance as a native model capability. This work addresses a key limitation in World Action Models by reducing representational entanglement, potentially improving success rates and generalization in complex robotic manipulation tasks. It also offers a few-shot fine-tuning strategy for real-robot deployment, which could accelerate the adoption of WAMs in embodied AI and robotics. The RD mechanism encourages the action branch to explicitly model potential state transition tokens, which are retained as CoT in the reasoning space to guide fine-grained motion trajectory. Experiments show that RD significantly improves success rate and generalization, and the abundant state transition supervision signals alleviate sparse supervision in action generation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: World Action Models (WAMs) aim to build unified architectures that understand world state evolution and guide generative motion planning. However, existing visual branches often predict static visual observations rather than reflecting potential transition information, leading to representational entanglement between high-level physical condition evolution and low-level action trajectory generation. The PILOT framework introduces Representational Deduction to bridge this gap by integrating motion chain-of-thought guidance.

## [Hybrid LLM-Wikidata Pipeline for Multilingual HR Skill Knowledge Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07023) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a hybrid knowledge graph generation pipeline that combines top-down and bottom-up methods, grounding LLMs in Wikidata and using an agentic reflexion pattern to handle multilingual, unstandardized skill declarations for HR platforms. The system operates across five stages, including entity reconciliation, multilingual canonicalization, active curation, deduplication, and iterative recovery of unmapped concepts. This approach addresses a persistent challenge in HR technology—organizing thousands of unstandardized, multilingual skill declarations—which directly impacts talent matching accuracy. By dynamically creating new nodes for unrecognized skills while anchoring recognized ones to stable KG entities, it offers a scalable, explicable, and self-healing framework that could be applied beyond HR to other domains with noisy, evolving terminology. The pipeline is executed across five stages: entity reconciliation, multilingual canonicalization, active curation, deduplication, and iterative recovery of unmapped concepts. It supports five European languages and leverages Wikidata's multilingual knowledge graph for grounding, while the agentic reflexion pattern enables the LLM to synthesize emerging concepts and associated metadata.

**Background**: Knowledge graph generation is a core component in the GenAI ecosystem, providing reliable context for AI systems. Traditional approaches are either rigid top-down methods that rely on predefined ontologies or fragmented bottom-up methods that extract entities from scratch. The agentic reflexion pattern is a design pattern where an AI model generates, critiques, and refines its outputs through iterative self-assessment, improving quality over time. This paper combines these concepts to create a hybrid pipeline that adapts to noisy, evolving data.

## [Accounting Graph Transformer Improves Multi-KPI Forecasting for Small Businesses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07037) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces the Accounting Graph Transformer (AGT), a novel model that leverages accounting relations to jointly forecast 13 financial KPIs over 12 months for small businesses with limited history. AGT outperforms LightGBM, achieving a sample-weighted KPI-macro MAE of 0.6990 versus 0.7378 for LightGBM across 11,993 forecast origins. This work addresses an underexplored problem: multi-KPI forecasting for small businesses with short accounting histories, which is crucial for planning and risk management. The model's ability to outperform strong baselines like LightGBM suggests practical value for financial analytics and could inspire further research into graph-based forecasting. AGT represents each ledger series as a masked token, uses typed attention on a fixed accounting-relation graph, and fuses target-specific context with a gated three-month recency path. The model has 5.3M parameters and produces 156 aligned forecasts without company-specific fitting, achieving best results on all 13 KPIs against LightGBM, TimeMixer, and SOFTS in the matched seed-42 comparison.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Small businesses often have only 12-24 months of accounting history, making traditional forecasting methods less effective. Graph transformers combine transformer architectures with graph neural networks to model relational data, and this paper applies them to accounting data by leveraging the inherent relationships between financial metrics. The KPI-macro MAE metric averages MAE across KPIs, providing a balanced measure of forecasting accuracy.

## [LLM-as-a-Judge for Reverse Engineering Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07038) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper presents the first systematic study of using LLM-as-a-Judge to evaluate human-oriented binary reverse engineering (HOBRE) outputs, introducing BinJudgeBench, an expert-annotated, reference-free benchmark, and BinJudge, a routing-based judge that adaptively selects optimal configurations. This work addresses a critical gap in evaluating HOBRE outputs, where existing metrics are either costly or unreliable. By demonstrating that LLM-as-a-Judge can achieve high correlation with human judgment and proposing a cost-effective routing mechanism, it could significantly improve the scalability and fidelity of reverse engineering tool evaluation. BinJudgeBench covers three tasks: function name recovery, binary code summarization, and decompilation optimization. The LLM-as-a-Judge achieves an average correlation of 63.20% with human judgment, outperforming traditional metrics at 35.04%, and BinJudge improves correlation by 4.5%-24.7% while reducing API cost to 0.06x-0.84x of static best configurations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Human-Oriented Binary Reverse Engineering (HOBRE) aims to transform decompiled pseudocode into a more human-friendly representation, reducing cognitive burden. Traditional evaluation methods either require executable test cases and runtime environments, which are often unavailable, or rely on source code references that are inaccessible and fail to capture semantically equivalent but lexically diverse outputs. LLM-as-a-Judge is an evaluation method that uses a large language model to automatically score outputs against defined criteria, which is naturally suited to HOBRE evaluation.

## [AutoIntervene: Calibrated Intervention for Action-Chunking Policies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07065) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AutoIntervene introduces an online framework that selectively transfers control between an action-chunking policy and an operator during deployment, using a visual-action support memory to evaluate proposed chunks and calibrate switching thresholds from empirical quantiles. Experiments on real-world bimanual manipulation tasks show higher post-adaptation task success and lower operator-control time than manual intervention. This framework addresses a critical robustness issue in action-chunking visuomotor policies, which can produce smooth but inconsistent actions when the robot drifts out of the demonstration distribution. By enabling calibrated, phase-aware intervention, AutoIntervene could improve the reliability and safety of imitation learning policies in real-world deployment, reducing the need for manual oversight. The framework uses phase-local support for policy-to-operator transfer within the current task phase, and global support for returning to policy control after operator recovery. Switching thresholds are calibrated from empirical quantiles of evaluation-level scores on held-out expert demonstrations, avoiding manual tuning, and intervention segments are retained for corrective supervision in subsequent policy updates.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Action-chunking visuomotor policies learn from demonstrations and predict short action sequences rather than single-step commands, improving temporal consistency. However, perception errors and execution drift can move the robot outside the demonstration distribution, leading to inconsistent actions. AutoIntervene builds on this by introducing an online intervention mechanism that uses a support memory of successful executions to decide when to hand control to an operator.

It combines support-aware 3D traversability mapping, stair-specific perception, and direction-aware stair traversal, coordinated by a unified navigation policy. ## [ROSER Framework Unlocks RL Component Synergy for Sample-Efficient Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07086) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper systematically investigates the interdependencies of reinforcement learning (RL) algorithmic components, revealing that naively stacking state-of-the-art techniques can lead to compounded non-stationarity and performance degradation. It introduces ROSER, a framework that coordinates model-based representation, optimization stability, and experience replay, achieving a 17.60% improvement over naive stacking on continuous-control benchmarks. This work addresses a critical gap in RL research by moving beyond isolated component improvements to a holistic system design perspective. The findings and ROSER framework provide actionable insights for building sample-efficient agents, which is essential for real-world applications where data is scarce or expensive. The study finds that component efficacy is highly task-dependent, and naive stacking often triggers emergent challenges like compounded non-stationarity. ROSER integrates representative techniques from three critical dimensions—Model-based Representation, Optimization Stability, and Experience Replay—and consistently outperforms vanilla baselines across diverse continuous-control benchmarks.

**Background**: Reinforcement learning systems are more complex than other machine learning paradigms due to tightly coupled factors, requiring joint design of many components. This paper systematically investigates these interactions and proposes a principled coordination framework.

This paper evaluates the transferability of stochastic cortical self-reconstruction (SCSR), originally trained on UK Biobank data, to an independent Chinese cohort for detecting gray matter atrophy in Alzheimer's disease. It compares four training strategies and two reconstruction backbones, finding that a fine-tuned Spherical UNet achieves the highest discriminative performance (average pairwise AUC = 0.848). This work addresses a critical challenge in neuroimaging: the cross-population generalization of a novel method for detecting subtle cortical atrophy. Demonstrating robust transferability from UK Biobank to a Chinese cohort is essential for clinical applicability and global Alzheimer's disease research. The study compares direct application, fine-tuning, training from scratch, and joint training on UKB and Chinese data, using both a multilayer perceptron (MLP) and a Spherical UNet (SUNet) as reconstruction backbones. The fine-tuned SUNet achieved the highest average pairwise AUC of 0.848, and reconstruction errors remained low across the lifespan even when the training population had a narrower age distribution.

The paper introduces GABL, a geometry-aware bronchoscope localization framework that fuses preoperative structural priors with intraoperative video to estimate 6-DoF camera poses. It achieves an 8.37% reduction in translation error and a 31.76% reduction in rotation error over prior state-of-the-art, with a 4x inference speedup to 33.6 FPS. This advancement could significantly improve the accuracy and real-time performance of bronchoscopy navigation, potentially enhancing clinical outcomes for procedures like biopsy and tumor resection. It addresses a critical need for millimeter-level precision in confined anatomical structures, which is a challenging problem in medical imaging. The framework employs a graph-guided coarse-to-fine localization scheme to leverage structural priors and a Transformer-based tracking model with a novel RGB-depth matching objective to mitigate pose jitter. Extensive experiments demonstrate robust real-time performance at 33.6 FPS, making it suitable for intraoperative guidance.

Small Language Models (SLMs) are compact AI models (0.5B-14B parameters) that can run on edge devices, making them suitable for onboard processing. Multi-agent systems involve multiple AI agents collaborating to perform tasks, as used here for ground-based command generation.

This paper introduces a method to measure concept content in text by reading the internal activations of frozen, out-of-the-box LLMs, using Recursive Feature Machine (RFM) and linear probing. On a human-annotated ESG dataset, the best linear probe achieves accuracy within 0.6 percentage points of a fine-tuned domain classifier without any task-specific fine-tuning. This work challenges the reliance on surface-level text measures for concept content, offering a way to tap into LLMs' internal knowledge without fine-tuning. It has potential applications in finance and other domains where measuring concept intensity is crucial, and it highlights the gap between what LLMs know internally and what they express. The study compares RFM concept vectors and linear probes against embedding and surface baselines, as well as the model's own answer. The linear probe outperforms the model's own answer in eleven of twelve comparisons, while RFM provides a continuous score for concept presence, though its validation awaits graded labels.

**Background**: Traditional methods for measuring concept content in text rely on surface features like word counts or embedding similarities, which capture the words used rather than the reader's judgment. Recent research shows that LLMs encode more information internally than they express in outputs. Linear probing involves training a simple classifier on LLM hidden states to detect properties, while RFM is a kernel-based algorithm that iteratively reweights features using the average gradient outer product (AGOP) to build interpretable models.

## [TOFD: A Unified Defense Framework Against Poisoning Attacks in Split Federated Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07274) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces TOFD, a three-stage framework for proactive detection and robust optimization against poisoning attacks in Split Federated Learning (SFL). It combines target inference, sample purification, and decoupling optimization to defend against a wide range of attacks. SFL is increasingly used for privacy-preserving collaborative learning, but its split architecture introduces unique vulnerabilities. TOFD provides a unified defense that exploits the split paradigm, potentially improving security and trust in SFL deployments. TOFD operates in three stages: Target Inference using class-specific Margin Perturbation (MP), Sample Purification via cross-class min-max normalization, and Decoupling Optimization with an adversarial guidance model. The paper provides theoretical convergence guarantees and demonstrates superior robustness on five datasets with low computational overhead.

Poisoning attacks in federated learning involve malicious clients tampering with training data or model updates to degrade performance or introduce backdoors. Existing defenses often fail to exploit the split architecture, limiting their effectiveness.

## [Finite E-Group of Nilpotency Class Three Exists](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07275) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The authors prove that a specific finite 3-group of order 3^84, previously studied by Abdollahi et al., is an E-group, thereby answering Caranti's question about the existence of finite E-groups of nilpotency class three. This result resolves a long-standing open question in group theory, showing that finite E-groups can have nilpotency class three. It provides a concrete example and a novel linear algebra argument that may inspire further research on endomorphisms and automorphisms of groups. The proof reduces the tensor rigidity to a finite calculation on the 9841 points of PG(8,3). The group P has V=P/Φ(P)≅F_3^9, and the nine power relations determine a linear map q:V→Λ^2 V; the authors show q has no nonzero proper subspace U satisfying q(U)⊆Λ^2 U.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: In group theory, an E-group is a group where every element commutes with all its endomorphic images. Nilpotency class is a measure of how close a group is to being abelian; class three means the group is non-abelian but its commutator subgroup is contained in the center. Caranti's question asked whether finite E-groups could have nilpotency class three, which was previously unknown.

The paper introduces H2AL, a novel framework that applies hyperbolic geometry to registration-based few-shot medical image segmentation, incorporating a Hyperbolic Hierarchy-aware Infusion (H2I) module and a gradient aggregation-based joint optimization algorithm. Experiments on two anatomical regions with five settings demonstrate improved registration and segmentation performance. This work addresses a key limitation of existing RFMIS methods that operate in Euclidean space, which neglect anatomical hierarchies. By modeling hierarchies in hyperbolic space, H2AL can improve pseudo-label quality and segmentation accuracy, potentially benefiting medical image analysis where annotated data is scarce. The H2I module uses transformation-guided supervised hyperbolic contrastive learning to learn hierarchy-aware representations, which are then injected into Euclidean space via a gated infusion block. The joint optimization algorithm aggregates gradients from registration and segmentation decoders to update the shared encoder, promoting collaborative learning. The code is publicly available on GitHub.

**Background**: Registration-based few-shot medical image segmentation (RFMIS) generates pseudo-labels for unlabeled images by warping labeled images through registration. Traditional methods perform pixel-level optimization in Euclidean space, treating anatomical structures as flat and disjoint, which neglects inherent hierarchies. Hyperbolic space is well-suited for modeling hierarchical data, as it can embed tree-like structures with low distortion. This paper leverages that property to improve RFMIS.

<a id="item-226"></a>
## [New Metric SA-PPG Exposes Overestimated Benchmark Contamination Mitigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07341) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes SA-PPG, a stratified per-question probability-based metric for evaluating benchmark contamination mitigation in LLMs, and introduces RailCap, a decoding-time mitigation strategy. Experiments show that previous mitigation strategies' restoration is substantially overestimated under SA-PPG, while RailCap achieves the lowest SA-PPG. Benchmark contamination is a critical issue in LLM evaluation, as it inflates scores and masks true capabilities. The proposed metric provides a more accurate assessment, which could lead to more reliable model comparisons and better mitigation strategies, benefiting the AI/ML community and downstream applications. SA-PPG estimates each question's solve probability by sampling, differences it against the clean model per question, and aggregates within groups defined by the clean model's solve probability. RailCap caps the next trajectory token to the runner-up whenever a sample falls back onto the greedy trajectory, accumulating suppression until the response distribution becomes sufficiently dispersed.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Benchmark contamination occurs when test data from public benchmarks leaks into pretraining corpora, inflating evaluation scores once memorized. Contamination mitigation evaluation intervenes in the decoding process to suppress memorization and restore a contaminated model's genuine capability, but the prevailing metric, G-AP (Gap of Aggregate Performance), is flawed because discrete correct/incorrect readouts cannot characterize per-question performance, averaging before differencing lets over- and under-suppression cancel out, and uniform per-question weighting invites strategies to push solve probabilities onto the clean model's high-frequency values.

This paper introduces BAMM, a 40-hour dataset of real-world television recordings, and evaluates CNN-based AI-generated music detectors under three scenarios, showing significant performance degradation on real broadcast data. This work highlights a critical domain gap in AI-generated music detection, showing that current detectors trained on clean or synthetic data are unreliable for real-world broadcast monitoring, which is essential for transparency and fair compensation in media. Both clean-trained and broadcast-trained CNN variants achieve near-perfect performance on Clean Foreground Music (CFM) but degrade substantially under synthetic broadcast conditions, and further degrade on Real TV Broadcast (RTB) with substantial score overlap between AI-generated and human-made music.

This study validates a fully automated pipeline using nnU-Net deep learning segmentation and geometric modeling to compute femoroacetabular impingement (FAI) angles from zero echo time (ZTE) MRI, achieving excellent agreement with expert manual measurements for most angles. This advancement could reduce reliance on CT for FAI assessment, eliminating ionizing radiation exposure while maintaining diagnostic accuracy. It also demonstrates the potential of deep learning to automate complex morphometric measurements in musculoskeletal imaging, potentially improving clinical workflow and accessibility. The study included 73 participants (135 hips), with nnU-Net trained on 100 hips and tested on 35. Dice scores exceeded 0.96 for bone segmentation, and model-rater agreement was excellent for acetabular version, coronal center-edge, and Tonnis angles (ICC 0.92-0.96), but only fair for alpha and femoral neck-shaft angles (0.45 and 0.55).

## [GeoDistill-Refine: Silhouette-First Distillation for Spacecraft Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07405) ⭐️ 7.0/10

GeoDistill-Refine is a two-stage framework that distills SAM 3 pseudo-masks into a compact segmentation network using a silhouette-first strategy and sample-level gating. On the SpaceSense-Bench HJM lockbox set, it improves Image IoU by 0.0456 and Boundary F1 by 0.1380 over a plain pseudo-label student. This work addresses the challenge of annotation-free spacecraft segmentation, which is critical for autonomous on-orbit operations. By improving boundary quality and foreground precision, it could enhance the reliability of spacecraft perception systems in real-world missions. The framework fuses six fixed prompts via an unweighted 50% vote to stabilize teacher outputs, and the student is refined with signed-distance-field, skeleton, and area objectives. The deployed TinyUNet has only 0.263M parameters and runs at ~1.1 ms per image on an RTX 4090, with SAM 3 pseudo-mask construction and auxiliary branches used only during training.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Foundation segmentation models like SAM can provide supervision for spacecraft imagery without manual masks, but their predictions vary with textual prompts and may contain geometric errors. Knowledge distillation transfers knowledge from a large teacher model to a compact student network, and pseudo-masks are generated labels used for training. SpaceSense-Bench is a benchmark for spacecraft perception, providing simulated multi-modal data for component-level semantic understanding.

## [Taxonomy-Driven Mapping of Open-Source AI Risk Tools](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07446) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a structured protocol that maps 21 open-source LLM evaluation and security tools to the 32 subcategories of the extended MIT AI Risk Mitigation and Response Taxonomy, using an LLM-assisted RAG pipeline to automate the analysis. The mapping achieved an F1 score of 75.5% after majority voting, with moderate inter-reviewer agreement (Fleiss' Kappa = 0.509). This work addresses the fragmentation in the AI risk mitigation tooling landscape by aligning technical tools with governance frameworks, enabling practitioners to identify which risks are covered and where gaps remain. It supports the scaling of AI risk management from pilot to production, which is critical for enterprise adoption of generative AI. The analysis reveals a highly skewed landscape: tools cluster around technical and operational controls, while governance, legal/regulatory, and financial/market controls are largely unaddressed. The paper proposes a layered risk-mitigation architecture that combines tool-based controls with organizational and regulatory processes, and notes where human oversight remains necessary.

## [CoinRAG: Fine-Grained KV Cache Reuse for Efficient Long-Context RAG](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07458) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CoinRAG introduces a novel method for reusing fine-grained KV caches in retrieval-augmented generation (RAG), moving beyond coarse-grained chunk-level reuse. It achieves a new Pareto frontier with an average 5.3% relative improvement in answer quality (F1) under low prefill latency constraints. This work addresses the critical efficiency-quality trade-off in long-context RAG systems, where prefill latency dominates user experience. By enabling more efficient reuse of KV caches, CoinRAG could make high-performance RAG more practical for real-world deployment, benefiting applications that rely on long retrieved contexts. CoinRAG identifies query-relevant semantic units within retrieved chunks via two-stage retrieval and assembles their sliced KV representations with chunk-level context. Evaluations on LongBench multi-hop question answering tasks show significant operational cost reduction while maintaining accuracy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) enhances language models by retrieving relevant documents, but long retrieved contexts increase prefill latency (time to first token). Existing chunk-level KV cache reuse reduces this latency but retains noise and redundancy. CoinRAG proposes finer-grained nugget caches to improve both efficiency and accuracy.

## [MEDLEY-BENCH: New Benchmark for LLM Belief Revision Under Social Pressure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.16009) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MEDLEY-BENCH is a new open benchmark that evaluates how large language models revise beliefs under social pressure, using a three-step protocol and the Medley Metacognition Score (MMS). The study tested 35 models from 12 families and found that evaluation-mapped composites were consistently low across models. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in LLM evaluation by focusing on metacognition and belief revision rather than just final-answer accuracy. It provides a structured methodology for assessing model robustness and alignment, which is timely given the increasing deployment of LLMs in decision-making contexts. The study used an ipsative procedure where the Evaluation-mapped composite had the lowest relative rubric score in 30 of 35 models. An exploratory adversarial analysis of 11 models showed sensitivity to manipulated consensus labels ranging from near zero to larger response shifts, and a human rubric-application study found a mean composite difference of 0.727 (95% CI: 0.500-0.942).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Most LLM benchmarks evaluate final-answer quality but do not reveal how models revise beliefs under disagreement or conflicting evidence. MEDLEY-BENCH introduces a three-step protocol that compares structured private self-review and analyst-conditioned social revision from a common solo baseline, aiming to characterize prompted belief revision under ambiguity and social disagreement.

## [Trust in Agent Networks Must Be Baked In, Not Bolted On](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.19035) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This vision paper argues that trustworthiness in LLM-based agent-to-agent (A2A) networks cannot be achieved by retrofitting existing protocols designed for individual agents. Instead, it proposes that trust must be architected from the very beginning of the A2A coordination framework, introducing four design pillars. As autonomous agents increasingly collaborate in networks, systemic vulnerabilities like adversarial composition and cascading failures pose serious risks. This paper highlights the need for a foundational shift in how trust is designed into these systems, which is critical for the safe deployment of multi-agent AI in real-world applications. The paper identifies three systemic vulnerabilities: adversarial composition, semantic misalignment, and cascading operational failures. It argues that existing agent alignment techniques are insufficient and proposes a conceptual framework with four design pillars for embedding trust in A2A networks.

However, this introduces new security challenges that traditional single-agent alignment techniques cannot address, necessitating a new approach to trustworthiness.

## [Ratchet: A Method to Retire LLM Agent Skills with Reliable Judges](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22148) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Ratchet, a method for maintaining LLM agent skill libraries by evicting skills based on measured contribution, with a theoretical condition for judge reliability and empirical gains on MBPP+. This work addresses a practical problem in LLM agent skill management, providing both theoretical and experimental contributions that could improve the reliability and efficiency of self-improving agents. It offers a principled way to decide when to trust an LLM judge, which is crucial for safe and effective skill library maintenance. The paper proves a condition: a judge scoring failures as passes at rate (1-τ)/2 or above retires nothing, for eviction margin τ. Empirically, Ratchet lifts held-out pass@1 by +0.328 on a hard MBPP+ slice, while LLM-written skills are worth +0.0 pp against a no-skill control, and human-written ones +16.2 pp.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents that write and edit their own skill libraries must decide which skills to keep, using noisy scalar evaluations. The paper models the judge's errors as a binary channel, distinguishing between passes scored as failures (which cost sample efficiency) and failures scored as passes (which displace the eviction statistic). Without maintenance, libraries can enter 'library drift', where injecting a skill scores worse than injecting nothing.

<a id="item-238"></a>
## [Protocol Choices Flip LLM Confidence Calibration Results](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.27752) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2605.27752) shows that the choice of scoring protocol—which answer is scored and under which prompt—can flip conclusions about whether verbalized confidence or token likelihood is better calibrated in LLMs. Across four QA datasets and three 7-8B Instruct models, point estimates flipped in 4 of 12 settings under ECE and 9 of 12 under AUROC. This finding is critical for researchers and practitioners evaluating LLM confidence, as it reveals that current comparisons are highly sensitive to arbitrary methodological choices. It underscores the need for standardized evaluation protocols to ensure reliable and comparable calibration assessments across studies. The AUROC result cannot be explained by rescaling, since AUROC is invariant to order-preserving transformations; the items are ordered differently. Additional choices—substituting the reference string for the model's own answer and reading the first answer token instead of the answer span—also affect results, leaving the sign of the ECE comparison undetermined in 6 of 12 settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: LLM confidence calibration compares two signals: verbalized confidence (the model's stated probability) and token likelihood (the probability assigned to the generated tokens). ECE (Expected Calibration Error) measures how well predicted confidence aligns with actual accuracy, while AUROC (Area Under the Receiver Operating Characteristic) evaluates how well uncertainty separates correct from incorrect predictions. The paper highlights that these metrics are sensitive to protocol choices, which are often not explicitly stated in published studies.

## [MGSD: Modality-Gap-Aware Self-Distillation for Visual Spatial Planning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06076) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces MGSD, a two-stage modality-gap-aware self-distillation framework that improves visual spatial planning in vision-language models by first establishing reliable visual state recovery and then transferring symbolic planning knowledge through token-level supervision on student-generated prefixes. Experiments show consistent performance gains of 19.3% and 18.4% macro average across different model scales. This work addresses a critical limitation in vision-language models for embodied AI and robotics, where visual spatial planning is essential. By reducing the gap to symbolic upper bounds, MGSD could enable more reliable autonomous decision-making in real-world visual environments. The framework consists of a cold-start grounding stage and a symbol-guided on-policy self-distillation stage. Symbolic information is used only during training; inference relies exclusively on visual inputs. Ablation studies confirm improvements in both visual state recovery and optimal-path reasoning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) often struggle with visual spatial planning due to a perception-reasoning modality gap: they must infer latent state structures from pixels and then reason over them, whereas symbolic planning uses explicit representations. Self-distillation is a technique where a teacher model (often text-only) guides a student model (multimodal) by providing token-level supervision during training. This approach is related to on-policy distillation methods that improve reasoning capabilities in LLMs.

## [ForesightSafety-SAGE: Automated Safety Evaluation for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.08531) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ForesightSafety-SAGE introduces a fully automated framework that generates 1,072 safety evaluation scenarios across five risk dimensions and evaluates 12 LLM agents under two authority contexts, revealing an average attack success rate (ASR) of 47.1% and several models exceeding 70%. This framework addresses a critical gap in LLM agent safety evaluation by moving beyond static prompts and final-output judgments to executable, process-level assessment. It provides a scalable method for identifying behavioral safety risks, which is essential for improving the safety of increasingly autonomous LLM agents. The framework instantiates abstract safety risks into measurable scenarios and uses an automated evaluation pipeline. The evaluation covers 12 LLM agents, and the high ASR values highlight that current agents still face substantial behavioral safety risks during task execution.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents are evolving from simple text-based systems to autonomous entities that can maintain memory, use tools, and access external environments. Traditional safety evaluations rely on manually written scenarios and static prompts, which fail to capture the diverse risks agents encounter during dynamic task execution. ForesightSafety-SAGE automates scenario generation and evaluation to address this limitation.

## [Homebot: A Local AI Agent for Conversational Home Automation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02254) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Homebot is a locally deployable AI agent that combines language models with tools and skills for conversational home assistance, separating channel-specific messaging from voice interaction. It uses a shared runtime to handle voice and instant-messaging requests, with session ownership scoped by channel and wake-word activation. This design addresses key challenges in smart home AI, such as privacy, session management, and tool integration, by enabling local deployment. It could influence the development of personal AI agents that are more private and customizable, impacting the broader ecosystem of conversational AI and smart home technology. Homebot separates common request processing from session ownership: messaging history is scoped to a channel and chat, while voice interaction is bounded by wake-word activation. For hands-free use, it combines local wake-word detection, streaming speech recognition and synthesis, and an explicit dialogue-state protocol for ending, following up, or continuing a conversation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Wake-word detection, also known as keyword spotting, is a technique that identifies predefined words or phrases in audio streams to activate a device. Dialogue state tracking maintains a representation of the current dialogue state, including user intents and conversation history, which is crucial for managing multi-turn interactions. Streaming speech recognition and synthesis allow real-time processing of audio, enabling responsive voice interfaces.

## [Cross-Layer Attention-MLP Interaction Bound Verified on Qwen2.5](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03629) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper extends a previous single-layer theoretical result on attention-MLP interaction under weight-space ablation to multi-layer settings, deriving exact decompositions and a closed-form Jacobian bound for the attention sub-block, which is verified on Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct's real weights without violation. This work advances mechanistic interpretability by providing theoretical guarantees for cross-layer interaction analysis, which is crucial for understanding how information flows in large transformers. The verification on a real pretrained model suggests the bounds are practically relevant, potentially aiding interpretability research and model debugging. The paper decomposes cross-layer ablation interactions into same-block terms plus a cross-layer remainder, which is isolated as a double integral of a mixed second derivative. It derives a closed-form Jacobian bound for the attention sub-block and verifies it on Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct, but does not yet chain it across layers. Additionally, it finds an emergent circuit for indirect object identification in the same model, with mixed results on collapse, dissociation, and interaction.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Activation patching is a mechanistic interpretability technique that replaces activations from one model run with those from another to causally probe circuitry. Weight-space ablation is a related method that removes or modifies weights to study their contribution. Transformers consist of alternating attention and MLP layers, and understanding their interaction is key to interpreting model behavior. This paper builds on a companion paper that studied a single residual block, extending the analysis to multiple layers.

## [SkillTrace: Multi-Trace Provenance Auditing for LLM-Agent Skill Reuse](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05204) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SkillTrace, a multi-trace provenance auditing framework, has been introduced to detect LLM-agent skill reuse by analyzing expression, implementation, and operational traces. It achieves AUROC 0.938 and F1 0.898 on the SKILLTRACE-BENCH benchmark, outperforming existing single-modality detectors. This framework addresses a critical gap in auditing LLM-agent skill reuse, which is increasingly important as skills become marketplace artifacts. By providing trace-attributed evidence, it enables more effective and actionable auditing, potentially improving security and accountability in LLM-agent ecosystems. SkillTrace extracts three provenance traces: Expression, Implementation, and Operational, with the Operational Trace represented as a Skill Operational Graph (SOG). The framework uses an LLM only for Operational-trace extraction at ingestion, and performs deterministic comparisons at audit time, calibrating each trace against same-function strict negatives. It also includes a wild audit of 36,446 skills, demonstrating actionable review queues beyond repository-level baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: LLM-agent ecosystems are growing rapidly, with reusable skills as mixed-modality packages of metadata, instructions, code, tools, and workflows. Existing code clone detection methods target single-modality source code or whole-package similarity, but skill reuse evidence is distributed across multiple modalities, making them insufficient. Provenance auditing frameworks track data origins and transformations to ensure accountability, and SkillTrace extends this concept to LLM-agent skills.

## [DASH: Adaptive Supervision Horizons Boost Reasoning Model Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06243) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces DASH (Divergence-Adaptive Supervision Horizons), a method that adjusts token-level supervision weights in on-policy self-distillation based on the temporal evolution of divergence between teacher and student. Experiments on three mathematical reasoning benchmarks across three model scales show consistent improvements over vanilla OPSD. This addresses a known limitation in RLVR where verifiable rewards are sparse and sequence-level, by providing dense token-level supervision that adapts to the divergence history. It could improve the efficiency and effectiveness of training reasoning models, potentially benefiting the broader LLM reasoning community. DASH maps the gap between each local distillation signal and the sequence-level mean to an adaptive propagation gate, controlling backward multi-step aggregation. It reuses teacher and student distributions already computed by OPSD, so no additional forward passes are needed. Code is available at https://github.com/DBtxy/DASH-OPSD.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) improves reasoning in large language models using automatically verifiable outcome signals, but these signals are sparse and sequence-level. On-policy self-distillation (OPSD) mitigates this by querying a privileged teacher at student-visited prefixes to provide dense token-level supervision. However, standard OPSD assigns the same coefficient to every local divergence, ignoring the temporal context. DASH adapts these coefficients based on the divergence sequence, improving training.

## [PURe: Log-Domain Product-Unit Residual Module Boosts Vision Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04397) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces PURe, a plug-and-play residual module that uses 2D product units with a real-valued log-domain formulation to enable stable multiplicative interactions in deep vision networks. It serves as a drop-in replacement for native residual units and consistently improves accuracy and parameter efficiency across ImageNet, CIFAR-10, Galaxy10 DECaLS, and AMOS benchmarks. This work demonstrates that explicit multiplicative local interactions are a practical and effective design primitive for deep residual networks, challenging the dominance of additive transformations. It offers a way to achieve performance comparable to much deeper ResNet baselines with smaller parameter budgets, which could lead to more efficient vision models in resource-constrained settings. PURe is built around a 2D product unit with a real-valued log-domain formulation that keeps multiplicative aggregation numerically well-behaved inside residual hierarchies. It is instantiated in residual CNNs for image classification and in 2D residual encoder-decoder networks for slice-based CT segmentation, showing consistent improvements across multiple benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Modern vision networks predominantly rely on additive local transformations, while explicit multiplicative interactions are underexplored. Product units, which compute products of inputs raised to learned exponents, offer a direct way to model such interactions but have historically suffered from optimization instability. PURe addresses this by using a log-domain formulation, making product units practical within deep residual architectures.

## [Minimal Ingredients for Reward Assignment in Imitation Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.06793) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper empirically identifies that proximity alone suffices for offline reward assignment, with lightweight temporal alignment offering modest gains, across 32 benchmarks. The findings advocate algorithmic minimalism in reward design for both offline and online imitation learning. This study provides systematic empirical evidence on the minimal structure needed for reward assignment, which could guide future method design and simplify existing approaches. It addresses a fundamental question with broad applicability across offline and online RL settings. The study evaluates two design axes: proximity approximation and temporal alignment, across 32 benchmarks and three downstream RL algorithms. It also provides a lightweight theoretical result characterizing when simple proximity-based rewards suffice, compared with optimal transport approaches.

This principle underlies many methods, but the core ingredients driving performance were underexplored. The paper systematically investigates these ingredients, providing empirical insights into when simple proximity suffices and when temporal alignment is needed.

This paper introduces a Dyna-style reinforcement learning method that augments PPO with synthetic transitions from a learned model for quadrupedal locomotion. It achieves convergence in 19.64M simulation steps versus 27.53M for standard PPO, a 12.24% reduction in wall-clock time, validated on Unitree Go1 and cross-platform on ANYmal and Go2. This work addresses the critical issue of sample inefficiency in on-policy RL for legged robots, potentially reducing training costs and accelerating deployment. It offers a practical improvement that could benefit the broader robotics and RL communities by making model-based data augmentation more accessible. The method uses a learned transition model to generate short-horizon synthetic tails anchored by physics-based simulation, with a scheduling strategy to gradually integrate synthetic data. Ablation studies analyze the impact of data parameters, and cross-platform experiments show reward trade-offs on complex morphologies like ANYmal.

## [Sketch-Based Regularization Enables In Situ Training of Implicit Neural Compressors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.02659) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a novel in situ training protocol for implicit neural representation-based hypernetworks, using limited memory buffers of full and sketched data samples to prevent catastrophic forgetting. The method achieves strong reconstruction performance at high compression rates on complex 2D and 3D scientific simulation data, approximately matching offline performance. This work addresses a critical challenge in continual learning and neural compression, enabling efficient in situ compression of large-scale scientific simulation data without catastrophic forgetting. It could significantly reduce storage and transmission costs for long-running simulations, benefiting fields like computational physics and climate modeling. The theoretical motivation is grounded in a Johnson-Lindenstrauss-informed result, showing that sketching acts as a regularizer. The method is evaluated on unstructured grids and non-Cartesian geometries over long time horizons, demonstrating that sketching enables the in situ scheme to approximately match offline performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Implicit neural representations (INRs), also known as neural fields, parameterize continuous signals using neural networks, offering discretization independence and lightweight models. In situ training refers to updating models directly on the device or within the simulation environment, which is challenging due to limited memory and the risk of catastrophic forgetting. The Johnson-Lindenstrauss lemma provides a mathematical foundation for dimensionality reduction while preserving distances, which underpins the sketching approach used here.

## [MAC: First Public CVR Benchmark with Multi-Attribution Labels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.02184) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces MAC, the first public conversion rate (CVR) prediction dataset featuring labels from multiple attribution mechanisms, along with PyMAL, an open-source library for multi-attribution learning (MAL). It also proposes a novel model, Mixture of Asymmetric Experts (MoAE), which outperforms existing state-of-the-art MAL methods on MAC. This work fills a critical gap in CVR prediction research by providing a public dataset with multiple attribution labels, enabling the development and evaluation of multi-attribution learning approaches. The consistent performance gains and design insights could influence future research and industrial practices in conversion modeling. The MAC dataset includes labels from four diverse attribution mechanisms, and PyMAL covers a wide array of baseline methods. The paper reveals three key insights: MAL consistently improves performance, especially for long conversion paths; performance scales with objective complexity, but adding auxiliary objectives can be counterproductive for first-click targets; and two architectural principles are crucial—fully learning multi-attribution knowledge and fully leveraging it for the main task.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Conversion rate (CVR) prediction is a critical task in online advertising and recommendation systems, where attribution mechanisms assign credit to user actions leading to conversions. Traditional public datasets use a single attribution mechanism, limiting the study of multi-attribution learning (MAL), which aims to improve prediction by learning from multiple attribution labels simultaneously.

<a id="item-255"></a>
## [Probing Visual Concepts in Lightweight VLMs for Automated Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.06054) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a diagnostic method using linear probes and counterfactual image sets to analyze intermediate activations of five state-of-the-art lightweight vision-language models (VLMs) for automated driving. It identifies two failure modes—perceptual and cognitive—that explain why these models fail on simple driving-related visual questions. Understanding why VLMs fail in automated driving is critical for improving their reliability and safety in real-world applications. This work provides a novel diagnostic framework that can guide future model development and evaluation, potentially accelerating the deployment of trustworthy VLMs in autonomous systems. The study creates counterfactual image sets that differ only in a targeted visual concept and trains linear probes on activations from five SOTA VLMs, including two training variants of one model. Results show that object presence is linearly encoded, while spatial concepts like orientation are only implicitly encoded or not at all, and increasing object distance degrades linear separability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) combine visual and textual understanding, making them attractive for automated driving to handle long-tail scenarios. Linear probing is a technique that trains simple classifiers on internal activations to test whether specific concepts are linearly separable, revealing what models encode. Counterfactual images are modified versions of an image that differ in a controlled way, enabling targeted analysis of concept encoding.

This paper introduces structured tag-based schemes to extract implicit writing and citing intents, which enhance LLM performance on attributed long-form question answering. ## [Cluster Attention Boosts Graph Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.07492) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces cluster attention (CLATT), which partitions graph nodes into clusters using community detection algorithms and lets each node attend to all nodes within its cluster. Augmenting MPNNs or Graph Transformers with CLATT significantly improves performance on various graph datasets, including the GraphLand benchmark. This work addresses a key limitation of Graph Transformers by incorporating graph-structure inductive biases while maintaining large receptive fields. It offers a practical enhancement that could improve performance across a wide range of graph machine learning applications, potentially influencing future model designs. CLATT uses off-the-shelf graph community detection algorithms to form clusters, providing a balance between local and global information. The method is model-agnostic and can be integrated into existing MPNNs or Graph Transformers, showing consistent gains on real-world benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Message Passing Neural Networks (MPNNs) are popular for graph tasks but suffer from limited receptive fields due to finite message passing layers. Graph Transformers use global attention to overcome this, but they lack graph-structure inductive biases. Cluster attention aims to combine the strengths of both by using community detection to define attention scopes that respect graph topology.

## [GRM: Utility-Aware Jailbreak Attacks on Audio LLMs via Gradient-Ratio Masking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.09222) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces GRM, a utility-aware jailbreak attack framework for audio large language models (ALLMs) that selects frequency bands via gradient-ratio masking. Experiments on four ALLMs show GRM achieves an average jailbreak success rate of 88.46% while reducing utility degradation compared to full-band perturbation baselines. This work addresses a gap in existing perturbation-based jailbreak attacks by explicitly controlling frequency bands, showing that partial-band perturbations can achieve higher success with less utility degradation. This finding could influence future security research on audio LLMs and the design of more stealthy attacks, as well as inform defense mechanisms. GRM ranks Mel bands by the ratio of jailbreak contribution to transcript sensitivity, confines a universal perturbation to selected bands, and regularizes deviations from intended request semantics. The paper reports that jailbreak success rate varies non-monotonically with coverage, while utility degradation grows with coverage, motivating the frequency-selective approach.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Audio large language models (ALLMs) enable spoken interaction but introduce new jailbreak vulnerabilities. Existing perturbation-based jailbreaks do not explicitly control which frequency bands carry the perturbation, which can degrade utility on normal tasks and leave a conspicuous behavioral footprint. GRM aims to address this by selecting frequency bands that maximize attack success while minimizing utility loss.

This paper characterizes a bell-shaped membership signal along the interpolation path of Rectified Flows, deriving its peak location in closed form under Gaussian assumptions and validating it on audio and images. This work provides a novel theoretical and empirical analysis of membership signals in a widely used generative model family, offering insights into memorization and privacy with implications for copyright and membership inference attacks. ## [openPangu Quantization on Ascend NPUs: 8-bit Lossless, 4-bit Practical](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21257) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper presents a controlled empirical study evaluating post-training quantization methods for openPangu 1B and 7B models on Huawei Ascend 910B1 NPUs. It finds that 8-bit weight-only quantization is lossless, while 4-bit is practical for the 7B model but harmful for the 1B model, and ultra-low precision remains challenging. This study provides actionable insights for deploying openPangu models on domestic hardware, which is crucial for private and domestic LLM deployment. The findings help practitioners choose appropriate quantization settings to balance performance and efficiency on Ascend NPUs. The study evaluates weight-only and weight-activation methods including RTN, GPTQ, AWQ, SmoothQuant, GPTAQ, BiLLM, and SliM-LLM under a unified protocol across 18 tasks. Notably, W4A4 SmoothQuant produces non-finite perplexity, and most 2-bit and binary settings collapse to near-random behavior.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Post-training quantization (PTQ) reduces memory and compute requirements of large language models without retraining. Weight-only quantization quantizes only weights, while weight-activation quantization also quantizes activations, which is more challenging. Ascend NPUs are Huawei's AI accelerators, and the 910B1 is a specific model used in this study.

## [LoCA: Spatially-Aware Low-Rank Convolutional Adaptation for Vision Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06918) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces LoCA (Low-Rank Convolutional Adaptation), a novel parameter-efficient fine-tuning method that decouples channel and spatial adaptation for convolutional layers in vision foundation models. It uses low-rank channel adaptation and SVD-based spatial bases to preserve spatial priors while achieving competitive performance. LoCA addresses a key limitation of LoRA, which is designed for 2D matrices and disrupts the spatial topology of 4D convolutional kernels. This could enable more efficient and effective fine-tuning of convolutional vision models, benefiting applications in classification, segmentation, and generative tasks. LoCA introduces a low-rank channel adaptation for dense cross-channel mixing and refines spatial bases extracted from pre-trained kernels via Singular Value Decomposition (SVD). Experiments show it achieves state-of-the-art or competitive performance on fine-grained classification, domain-generalized semantic segmentation, and generative benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning (PEFT) methods like LoRA enable efficient adaptation of large pre-trained models by updating a small number of parameters. However, LoRA is typically applied to transformer layers with 2D weight matrices, while convolutional kernels are 4D tensors that couple spatial and channel information. LoCA is designed to handle this spatial-channel entanglement, preserving the inherent spatial topology of convolutional layers.

## [DeepLoop: New Residual Scaling for Stable Looped Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13491) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DeepLoop introduces a theoretical framework for depth scaling in looped transformers, proposing a new residual scaling exponent that increases from 1/4 to 1/2 as loop count grows. The method sets α=(2N)^(1/2) and β=(8N)^(-1/2) for unrolled depth N, and shows improved validation loss and downstream accuracy on GPT-2 scale models. This work addresses a critical challenge in scaling looped transformers, which are promising for achieving deep computation without increasing parameters. The findings provide practical guidance for training stable recurrent models, potentially impacting efficient AI architectures. The framework formalizes a 'tied-depth effect' through a first-order perturbation bound controlled by a visit-alignment coefficient κ_R. In the conservative aligned regime, the residual scaling exponent must increase from 1/4 to 1/2 as loop count grows, whereas it recovers the DeepNorm exponent when visits decorrelate.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Looped transformers reuse a compact stack of physical blocks for multiple rounds, increasing unrolled depth without adding parameters. DeepNorm is a normalization technique that scales residual connections to stabilize training of very deep transformers. The visit-alignment coefficient measures how much parameter updates from repeated visits align, affecting the required scaling.

This paper introduces F(AI)2R, a method that generalizes the provenance model into aiprov, a PROV-O extension for any AI-in-the-loop artifact, and packages it as an executable skill that AI agents can operate. The skill scaffolds continuous integration that gates every push on graph conformance and publishes the current build of the paper, using the paper itself as a case study. This addresses the timely issue of verifiability in AI-generated content, providing a concrete mechanism for recording and auditing AI contributions in research. It could enhance reproducibility and trust in AI-assisted research, impacting researchers, publishers, and AI tool developers. The provenance graph enforces two invariants: no parentless claim, and verification rungs that only humans may grant. The setup process asks for the operator's ORCID ID, resolves identity from the public registry, and scaffolds CI/CD integration.

## [FGPT: A Fortran Transpiler to Bridge Legacy Code and Modern Python Ecosystems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00130) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces FGPT, a transpiler framework that systematically converts Fortran code into GPU-adapted Fortran, auto-differentiable Fortran via Tapenade, or NumPy/JAX scripts. It was verified on climate modeling kernels, producing correct, differentiable Python implementations without manual intervention. FGPT addresses the expertise gap in Fortran by enabling modern scientists to leverage legacy code within Python-based ecosystems like JAX, which is crucial for high-performance computing and scientific computing. It offers a scalable, trustworthy path for modernizing legacy Fortran code, potentially accelerating research in fields relying on large-scale simulations. FGPT's architecture consists of three stages: a frontend that parses Fortran and extracts target procedures with dependencies, a middle-end that lowers code to an intermediate representation and then to GPU-adapted or auto-differentiable Fortran or a NumPy class, and a backend that transforms NumPy scripts into JAX modules optimized for GPU acceleration and automatic differentiation. The framework preserves program semantics throughout translation, addressing challenges where LLMs fail on large-scale community codes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Fortran has been a cornerstone of high-performance computing for decades, but a new generation of scientists is less familiar with it, while experienced developers are transitioning to modern ecosystems like JAX. This has led to 'Fython'—Python code written with a Fortran mindset—that fails to leverage modern frameworks. Transpilers like FGPT aim to automate the conversion of legacy Fortran to modern languages, preserving numerical fidelity and enabling automatic differentiation for machine learning and optimization.

## [WAM-Diff2: Hierarchical AR-to-Diffusion Distillation for Efficient Driving VLA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01035) ⭐️ 7.0/10

WAM-Diff2 introduces a three-stage hierarchical distillation strategy that converts pre-trained autoregressive Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models into parallel discrete diffusion models for autonomous driving. This approach achieves a 2.8x decoding speedup, scaling to 15.1x with system-level optimizations like FlashInfer and CUDA Graphs, while maintaining performance parity with autoregressive baselines. This work addresses critical bottlenecks in deploying VLA models for autonomous driving—high latency and exposure bias from sequential decoding—by enabling parallel execution without sacrificing multi-task reasoning. It could significantly improve real-time performance and efficiency of autonomous driving systems, making advanced VLA models more practical for real-world deployment. The three-stage distillation includes progressive block-wise adaptation, block-wise distillation, and model-wise cross-scale distillation, preserving semantic foundations while accelerating inference. Evaluations across driving understanding, perception, and planning benchmarks show WAM-Diff2 mitigates exposure bias and achieves performance parity with autoregressive baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models integrate visual perception, language understanding, and control into a single policy, but their autoregressive decoding is computationally heavy and prone to exposure bias. Diffusion policies offer low-latency parallel execution but are typically trained from scratch as narrow, single-task models. WAM-Diff2 bridges this gap by distilling pre-trained autoregressive generalists into parallel diffusion models, combining cognitive intelligence with execution efficiency.

## [LPIFM: A Learned Pairwise Preference Measure for Human-Aligned IVIF Ranking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01301) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces LPIFM, a source-conditioned model that predicts human A/B/Tie judgments for infrared-visible image fusion (IVIF) comparison, trained on a dense preference corpus of 6,300 comparisons among 25 fusion methods on 21 VIFB scenes. It achieves pairwise accuracy of 0.792-0.840 and Spearman correlation of 0.941-0.977 with human-derived rankings, and the dataset, model weights, and code are publicly released. This matters because existing objective metrics for IVIF often disagree with human perception, and LPIFM provides a scalable, human-aligned alternative for ranking fusion methods. It could become a standard evaluation tool in the IVIF field, reducing reliance on costly human studies and enabling more reliable algorithm comparison. LPIFM is antisymmetric under candidate swap, free of preference cycles, and fully transitive, matching or exceeding the internal consistency of the human panel. It outperforms the strongest conventional metric by 0.163-0.211 in accuracy on full 25-method pools, and the best checkpoint is released as lpifm_evafusion_ft_v1.pt.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Infrared-visible image fusion (IVIF) combines infrared and visible images into a single representation, but there is no ideal fused reference, so algorithms are typically ranked by objective metrics that approximate information transfer, structure, or source similarity. These proxies often disagree with human preference, and direct pairwise comparison is the established protocol for subjective assessment but becomes costly as the number of algorithms grows quadratically. LPIFM addresses this by learning to predict human A/B/Tie judgments from source and candidate images, making the protocol repeatable and scalable.

## [SSTQ: Privacy-Preserving Vector Quantization with Optimal MSE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05127) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Subsampled Stochastic TurboQuant (SSTQ), a framework for privacy-preserving vector quantization that combines overcomplete equal-norm tight frames, coordinate subsampling, and privacy-aware one-dimensional quantization. SSTQ achieves optimal mean squared error scaling with low communication cost, offering two variants: Flat Randomized Response and Metric-Aware Laplace. This work addresses a significant open problem in distributed optimization: achieving local differential privacy with low communication cost. The theoretical contributions, including optimal MSE scaling and reduced codebook-dependent MSE, are likely to influence future research in privacy-preserving federated learning. SSTQ uses only ceil(log2 N) + b bits per client, where N = Theta(d) is the frame size. The surrogate privacy-aware codebook objective reduces the codebook-dependent MSE scaling from O(4^b) to O(2^b). Empirical evaluations on CIFAR-10 and Fashion-MNIST show favorable utility and communication efficiency compared to baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Vector quantization methods like vqSGD use high-dimensional geometric constructions but suffer from dimension-dependent variance. Differential privacy mechanisms such as randomized response and the Laplace mechanism are common tools for privacy preservation. SSTQ combines these concepts to achieve both privacy and communication efficiency.

## [HyTBE: Hyperbolic Expert Model Boosts Cross-Domain Infrared Small Target Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05771) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces HyTBE, a Hyperbolic Target-Background Expert model that addresses cross-domain generalization in infrared small target detection by explicitly modeling target-background relation shift. It employs a Target-Background Relation Intervention, Hyperbolic Relation Modeling in a Poincaré ball, and a Hyperbolic-guided MoE Adapter to expand relation patterns and calibrate features. This work tackles a critical limitation in infrared small target detection: performance degradation on unseen domains. By introducing a novel hyperbolic perspective to relation shift, it offers a fresh approach that could improve robustness and generalization in real-world applications such as surveillance and remote sensing. The model uses a Target-Background Relation Intervention to selectively perturb targets or backgrounds, broadening observable relation patterns during training. Hyperbolic Relation Modeling maps multi-scale visual cues into a Poincaré ball, and a Hyperbolic-guided MoE Adapter calibrates features using hyperbolic relation representations. Leave-one-domain-out experiments on NUAA-SIRST, NUDT-SIRST, and IRSTD-1K show stronger cross-domain generalization than baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Infrared small target detection (IRSTD) aims to detect tiny, low-contrast targets in infrared imagery, which is challenging due to limited target pixels and complex backgrounds. Traditional methods often assume consistent domains, but in practice, infrared images vary across sensors and environments, causing performance drops. Hyperbolic geometry is increasingly used in deep learning to model hierarchical or tree-like data structures, offering advantages over Euclidean spaces for capturing relational patterns. This paper applies hyperbolic geometry to model target-background relations, a novel direction for IRSTD.

A new study systematically evaluates whether self-pretraining (SPT) improves transformer performance on medical time-series tasks, finding consistent accuracy gains of 0-6 percentage points across three datasets and multiple model depths. The gains appear in multivariate and univariate settings, with larger improvements for deeper models. This work provides practical guidance for medical AI practitioners, showing that self-pretraining is a simple, general strategy to improve accuracy in data-limited clinical settings without architectural changes. It also clarifies when SPT helps, which is crucial for efficient model development in healthcare. The study uses four masking-based objectives for SPT and varies model depth to examine capacity interactions. Experiments cover rehabilitation robotics (Camargo), stress detection (Non-EEG Stress), and Parkinson's disease detection (Gait Parkinson's Disease). Gains are dataset- and architecture-dependent, with deeper models benefiting more.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

Transformers have shown benefits from SPT in long-context benchmarks, but its effectiveness for medical time series was unclear. This study addresses that gap by testing SPT across diverse medical time-series datasets and model capacities.
