## [DKG-MTI: Dual Knowledge Graph Framework for User Intent Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06752) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces DKG-MTI, a dual knowledge graph framework that dynamically constructs a User-Specific Intent Knowledge Graph from each travel review and aligns it with a Global Hotel Knowledge Graph using structure-aware semantic smoothing, then feeds the aligned knowledge to an LLM for multi-task prediction of aspect ratings and reverse intent statements. Experiments on TripAdvisor reviews show consistent improvements over strong LLM and retrieval-based baselines. This framework addresses limitations of existing hierarchical pipelines and retrieval methods by providing an inference-only, structure-aware knowledge augmentation that improves both classification and generation tasks in user intent inference. It demonstrates the potential of knowledge graph alignment to enhance LLM performance in specialized domains, which could be applied to other review-based or domain-specific applications. The framework is inference-only, meaning it does not require training, and it dynamically constructs knowledge graphs per review. It uses structure-aware semantic smoothing to align the user-specific graph with a global hotel knowledge graph, and the aligned knowledge is combined with the original review for LLM processing. The experiments are conducted on TripAdvisor reviews, covering both aspect rating classification and reverse intent generation tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: User intent inference aims to understand the underlying goals of users from their queries or reviews, which is crucial for applications like search, e-commerce, and conversational systems. Traditional approaches often use hierarchical pipelines that can suffer from error propagation, or retrieval methods that ignore structural relationships in domain knowledge. Knowledge graphs provide a structured way to represent entities and relationships, and aligning them can enhance semantic understanding. ## [SkillEval: Interpretable Framework for Agent Skill Quality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06891) ⭐️ 6.0/10

SkillEval is a new interpretable framework that decomposes agent skill quality into document-level properties, scoring each property with fixed, inspectable directions. It learns interpretable directions from controlled positive-negative skill pairs and projects new skills onto these directions to produce scores. This framework addresses a gap in current agent skill evaluation by focusing on document-level properties rather than only downstream task performance, providing more interpretable and actionable feedback. It could help developers diagnose and improve skill documents, potentially enhancing the reliability and effectiveness of AI agents across various applications. SkillEval measures and reduces the influence of unrelated document features such as length and formatting, ensuring each score captures its intended semantic property. It also shows that SkillEval scores closely reflect downstream task performance, and that revising skills based on its diagnostics improves targeted properties and pass rates.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Agent skills are reusable procedural knowledge that helps AI agents solve specialized tasks, often stored as SKILL.md documents. Traditional evaluation methods measure skill quality by testing performance on specific downstream tasks, which only provides a partial view and doesn't identify which aspect to improve. SkillEval introduces a document-level evaluation approach that decomposes skill quality into interpretable properties, offering a more comprehensive and actionable assessment.

SetEasy, a new framework, fuses multimodal sensing (wristband physiology, 4K video, environmental data) with a v-Gage model and CP-SAT optimization to generate seating plans. In a four-week deployment with 23 students across 331 classes, it reduced RMSE from 0.75 to 0.53 and raised mean engagement from 0.30 to 0.70. The v-Gage model is grounded in a revised ISEQ (Individual Student Engagement Questionnaire) and converges across affective, behavioral, cognitive, and overall dimensions. The CP-SAT solver generates seating plans under visual-access and social-dynamics constraints, with over two-thirds of seats reaching high engagement and back-row low-activity patterns markedly reduced.

## [Interpretable Models Match LSTM in Predicting Human Randomization Failures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07220) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper demonstrates that simple frequency tracking models can match the predictive performance of black-box LSTM models in predicting human randomization failures in O'Neill's game, using 84,060 decisions from 2,802 pairs. This finding is significant because it suggests that interpretable models can provide both predictive accuracy and behavioral insights, potentially making AI systems more transparent and trustworthy in domains like behavioral economics and game theory. The analysis benchmarks naive and behavioral models against interpretable machine learning and deep learning models, and uses LASSO diagnostics to motivate a nested frequency tracking extension. The results show that repeat or avoid behavior, especially players' management of their own recent action histories, accounts for most of the interpretable signal.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: In game theory, mixed strategy equilibrium predicts that players randomize independently, so past actions should not predict future decisions. However, human players systematically deviate from this benchmark, and O'Neill's zero-sum card game is a classic experiment to study such deviations. Black-box models like LSTMs can predict these deviations, but this paper explores whether transparent alternatives can achieve similar performance while revealing the underlying behavioral structure.

PsychoAgent introduces a cognitive architecture for LLM agents that separates factual and affective memory, integrating them via a conflict-aware executive controller. In controlled conflict scenarios, it retrieved more conflict-critical memories than baselines (0.933 vs. 0.500 and 0.667) with a small semantic cost. This work addresses a gap in memory-augmented LLM agents by incorporating affective significance and conflict awareness, which are crucial for human-like cognition. It could improve the realism and emotional consistency of LLM agents in long-term interactions, benefiting applications like virtual assistants and game NPCs. The architecture filters affective memories by semantic relevance and re-ranks by salience. Five blinded raters evaluated 27 outputs; after within-rater standardization, the full architecture had the highest mean (+0.22 SD), but corrected pairwise differences were not statistically significant.

This paper builds on prior work in affective computing and conflict-aware retrieval, such as CARE, to enhance agent behavior.

## [Quantum-Embedded Attention Shows No Consistent Advantage on Classical Datasets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06846) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces Quantum-Embedded Attention (QEA), a hybrid quantum-classical architecture that uses a parameterized quantum circuit (PQC) to map compressed features to Pauli expectations, and tests it against an interface-matched classical control on the Breast Cancer Wisconsin dataset. The controlled factorial experiment found no consistent PQC contribution, with only one of four contrasts showing a temporary +1.63 percentage-point improvement that reversed at higher qubit counts. The findings challenge the assumption that adding a quantum layer to a hybrid model automatically improves performance, highlighting the need for rigorous component attribution in quantum machine learning research. This could influence how future hybrid quantum-classical models are designed and evaluated, potentially saving resources by avoiding ineffective quantum components. The experiment used a 2x2 factorial design with n_q ∈ {4, 8}, swapping the PQC for a classical map and the attention decoder for a linear head across five paired seeds per cell. Three of four paired quantum-minus-classical 95% confidence intervals included zero, and the one positive contrast did not survive multiple-comparison correction; a five-dataset grid showed a large deficit on CIFAR-10.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Parameterized quantum circuits (PQCs) are quantum circuits with tunable parameters, often used in variational quantum algorithms and hybrid quantum-classical models where a classical optimizer adjusts the parameters. Hybrid quantum-classical attention models aim to unify classical neural representations with quantum-encoded features, but their practical advantage over purely classical models remains debated. This paper uses a controlled experimental design to isolate the contribution of the quantum layer, addressing a common pitfall in quantum machine learning research where improvements are often attributed to the quantum component without proper controls.

This paper introduces DHC-ECS, a novel clustering method that integrates hierarchical, density-based, and graph clustering by using element-categorized connection subgraphs. It proposes a new inter-cluster similarity metric that combines distances, KNN subgraph element categorization, kernel density estimation, and local connectivity. This work addresses limitations of traditional hierarchical clustering that often ignores density variations and structural connectivity. By improving clustering accuracy and parameter robustness, it could benefit applications in pattern recognition and unsupervised learning, especially for low-dimensional datasets. The method is evaluated on heterogeneous benchmark datasets and shows superior performance compared to baselines including AChameleon, RNN-DBSCAN, McDPC, and G-RMS. This paper combines these approaches to handle density variations and structural connectivity.

This paper introduces a weighted LLM framework for analyzing Brazilian Central Bank (Copom) statements, extending iSent's sentence-level classification by adding intensity weights, a document-level score, and a separate layer for forward guidance and uncertainty. The framework was applied to 80 statements from August 2016 to August 2026, yielding a mean document score of +0.107 and a correlation of 0.719 between tone and guidance direction. This work provides a transparent, auditable method for quantifying central bank communication, which is crucial for market participants and policymakers. By separating rhetorical tone from policy guidance and uncertainty, it offers a more nuanced tool for predicting monetary policy moves and understanding market reactions. The framework identifies short hawkish and dovish expressions with 0-to-1 intensity weights, combines sentence counts with average signal intensities into a bounded -1 to 1 score, and adds a full-document layer for guidance direction, explicitness, uncertainty, and change in uncertainty. In the sample, 33.3% of sentences were hawkish, 18.0% dovish, 42.1% neutral, and 6.5% out of context; the most hawkish reading was +0.570 in August 2021.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Central bank communication is a key monetary policy tool, and analyzing its tone (hawkish vs. dovish) helps markets anticipate interest rate decisions. Traditional methods rely on manual reading or simple keyword counts, but recent advances use LLMs to classify sentences more accurately. The iSent classifier, developed by Itaú, is a notable example that divides statements into hawkish, dovish, neutral, and out-of-context categories. This paper builds on that by adding intensity weighting and a separate forward-guidance layer, aiming for a more comprehensive and transparent analysis.

## [Evolutionary Game Theory Models Predict Human-AI Coevolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16388) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper applies three evolutionary game theory models—Hawk-Dove, Iterated Prisoner's Dilemma, and War of Attrition—to analyze potential human-AI interaction and co-evolution, providing a computational simulation for illustration. This interdisciplinary approach offers a novel framework for understanding and predicting human-AI dynamics, which could inform AI safety and policy. It highlights the potential for cognitive co-evolution between humans and AI, raising profound ethical and cognitive implications. The selection of the three models was based on their widespread acceptance and relevance to human-AI dynamics. The paper notes that AI is shaped by human input and evolves in response, while human neuroplasticity allows the brain to evolve in response to stimuli, suggesting potential convergence.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Evolutionary game theory (EGT) models competitive and cooperative strategies among biological entities. The Hawk-Dove game predicts mixed-strategy equilibria based on conflict costs; the Iterated Prisoner's Dilemma shows that repeated interactions can foster cooperation; the War of Attrition models resource competition with time costs. These models are applied here to human-AI interaction to predict evolutionary equilibria.

## [CourseGraph: Automating Course Overlap Detection for Student Mobility](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05910) ⭐️ 6.0/10

CourseGraph, a new methodology, uses BERT embeddings and a Random Forest classifier to automatically assess whether external courses overlap with a student's home curriculum, based on course titles, descriptions, and learning outcomes. It was evaluated on Computer Science programs at Eindhoven University of Technology and Lund University, showing effective identification of overlapping courses. This work addresses a practical challenge in student mobility programs like Erasmus+, where students may take courses abroad that overlap with their home curriculum. By automating the evaluation process, CourseGraph can save time for administrators and help ensure that study-abroad experiences align with degree requirements, potentially improving the efficiency and quality of international education programs. The methodology extracts course information from webpages, represents it semantically using a BERT-based language model, computes pairwise similarities, and feeds them into a Random Forest classifier. Evaluation used the Computer Science program at Eindhoven University of Technology and six approved international programs from Lund University, including administrator decisions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Student mobility programs such as Erasmus+ allow students to study at partner universities, but they must ensure that courses taken abroad do not duplicate their home curriculum. Traditionally, curriculum administrators manually review course descriptions and learning outcomes to make these decisions, which is time-consuming. BERT is a transformer-based language model that produces contextual embeddings, enabling semantic similarity comparisons between texts. Random Forest is an ensemble machine learning method that combines multiple decision trees for classification tasks.

## [Deep Learning Framework Accelerates MXene Metasurface Spectral Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.08406) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces a deep learning framework that combines transfer learning, multi-channel spectral refinement, and Savitzky-Golay smoothing to predict absorption spectra for MXene-based metasurfaces. The model achieves an average RMSE of 0.0227, R² of 0.9563, and PSNR of 33.10 dB, outperforming baseline CNN and deformable CNN models. This work offers a scalable and computationally efficient alternative to traditional full-wave solvers, which are computationally intensive. It could significantly accelerate nanophotonic design workflows, benefiting researchers in computational materials science and photonics. The architecture uses a pretrained MobileNetV2 model fine-tuned to predict 102-point absorption spectra from 64×64 metasurface designs. The multi-channel spectral refinement module enhances feature extraction via multiple convolutional channels, while Savitzky-Golay smoothing mitigates high-frequency noise.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: MXenes are a family of two-dimensional transition metal carbides and nitrides, known for their metallic conductivity and hydrophilic surfaces. Metasurfaces are artificially engineered two-dimensional materials that control electromagnetic waves through subwavelength features, enabling applications like solar absorbers. Traditional spectral prediction relies on full-wave solvers, which are accurate but computationally expensive, motivating the use of deep learning for rapid approximation.

## [Rethinking Linguistic 'State' as a Unified Computational Learning Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00523) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper redefines the linguistic notion of 'state' as a systemic, context-dependent morphosyntactic mechanism, moving beyond its traditional restriction to Afroasiatic languages. It formalizes this concept within a computational learning framework using Riffian as a case study, proposing a set-valued function over grammatical templates and a learning algorithm based on finite-set operations. This work bridges linguistic theory and computational learning, offering a unified explanation for diverse nominal marking patterns and potentially influencing theories of nominal structure and lexical cognition. It suggests that 'state' is part of a broader class of syntactically conditioned dependencies, which could reshape how computational models handle morphosyntactic variation. The proposed model represents the state as a set-valued function over grammatical templates, and the learning algorithm uses finite-set operations to acquire and predict state-dependent grammatical configurations. The framework is based on the Template-Based Modular Cognitive framework and uses Riffian (Tarifit) as its primary empirical basis, with implications for determiner-noun structure analysis.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: The linguistic notion of 'state' traditionally refers to the construct (annexation) state in Afroasiatic languages, a language-specific morphosyntactic phenomenon. The Template-Based Modular Cognitive framework is an open-ended theoretical framework for understanding the mind as a network of systems, which the paper uses to ground its unified theory. Riffian is a Zenati Berber language spoken mainly in the Rif region of northern Morocco.

## [Whisper-Based Persian SER with PCA Dimensionality Reduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05165) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper proposes a Persian speech emotion recognition (SER) framework that uses Whisper encoder embeddings, applies PCA for dimensionality reduction instead of learned projection layers, and employs attention-based pooling for classification. Experiments on the ShEMO dataset show that PCA-based reduction improves performance while reducing training latency and memory usage. This work addresses the challenge of SER in low-resource languages, offering a more efficient way to leverage large pretrained models like Whisper. The findings could guide future efforts in adapting speech models for emotion recognition in languages with limited labeled data. The framework eliminates learned projection layers by using PCA, significantly reducing trainable parameters. ASR fine-tuning on Persian yielded only modest gains for SER, suggesting limited transfer from language adaptation to emotion-related representations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 10, 04:00

**Background**: Speech Emotion Recognition (SER) aims to identify emotions from speech, which is challenging for low-resource languages due to scarce labeled data. Whisper is a large pretrained speech model that extracts rich representations, and PCA is a classic dimensionality reduction technique that preserves important information while reducing feature dimensions. Attention pooling aggregates frame-level features into a fixed-length representation, which is useful for classification.
